## Opportunities by region

🔵 **Placement discipline, stated because this base has been wrong about it before:** a row is
placed in a region only where a *reached* source places it. Where nothing was reached, the region
reads **NIL with its denominator**, which is information; silence is not.

### North America

🟡 **Demand signal, snippet-grade.** Market returns give the region `36–41.7 %` of global share and
the largest absolute base, with `$951 M` (2024) → `$2.3 B` (2029) at `15.9 %` on one firm's
scope — 🔴 **a CAGR less than half the global figures in the table above, from the same query**,
which is itself a reason to distrust both.
🟢 **Regulatory shape: fragmented and sub-federal.** There is no FDA-equivalent for education
technology; adoption sits with districts and institutions, with state-level requirements
(Colorado, Texas named) rather than a national rule.
🟢 **The opportunity this base can evidence:** the region's foundation layer is the AGPL tier —
Open edX and OpenStax are both North American and both `AGPL-3.0`, read first-hand this pass. 🔵 **A
US district or university engagement that modifies and hosts either one has a §13 publication
obligation**, and the studio service with a market is *the compliance-clean customisation*: build
the modifications as separable `Apache-2.0`/`BSD-3-Clause` services around the AGPL core
(`oppia`, `jupyterhub`, `common-voice` are the rows that compose this way).
🔴 **Claim NOT made:** no named North American buyer, budget or procurement cycle was reached.

### EMEA

🟢 **The one region where this base holds a dated, decision-grade rule** — and it was re-served by
this pass's query rather than extended:
`Regulation (EU) 2024/1689` (the AI Act) puts education in **Annex III point 3**; the Digital
Omnibus (`Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`) defers the high-risk obligations to **`2027-12-02`**;
Art. 50 transparency applies **`2026-08-02`**; Art. 4 AI-literacy has applied since
**`2025-02-02`** and is **not** deferred; emotion recognition in schools is **prohibited since
Feb 2025**. 🔴 **A source in this pass's returns served the superseded *"2 August 2026"* high-risk
date again — the eighth recorded re-service.**
🟢 **The opportunity:** `2027-12-02` is **~14 months out**, which is exactly the window in which
conformity work is bought rather than deferred. 🔵 **Schools are *deployers*, vendors are
*providers*** — so a studio building an education AI system for an EU client is taking on the
**provider** duties, which is the billable scope.
🔴 **Claim NOT made:** `eur-lex.europa.eu` is unreachable on every transport here, so none of the
above was read from the instrument this pass. It is carried from this base's own prior verification.

🔴 **NIL on the education channel, with its denominator.** The prescribed query

🔵 **Opportunity, stated as a hypothesis rather than a finding:** a region described as the
fastest-growing with **no shared regulatory framework** is one where a permissive, forkable platform

### LATAM

🟢 **No longer a nil on the code channel.** `p311` recorded LATAM as a two-pass nil. This pass

[`portabilis/i-educar`](https://github.com/portabilis/i-educar) 🇧🇷, the Brazilian municipal SIS —

built onto i-educar cannot link `GPL-3.0` components**, which rules out a large part of the
contemporary Python/PHP tooling by default. That is a design decision that must be made at week 1,
not discovered at integration.

national AI policy and a bill in discussion; Brazil and Colombia have national strategies without

carry Chile's `Ley 21.719` as binding **`2026-12-01`** unless `18.623-07` is enacted, with the
contradictory public reading briefed alongside it.
🟡 **One unadopted lead, named so the next pass can take it:** an **IADB/BID technical note of
September 2026** on generative AI in classroom platforms, covering **Ceibal 🇺🇾** and Brazil's
edtech policy, at `publications.iadb.org`. 🔴 **Not fetched, nothing adopted.** It is the first
LATAM *primary-ish* source this battery has surfaced in several passes and it is worth a pass's
transport budget.

## 🔴 Pass 311 — THE MARKET NUMBERS FOR THIS SECTOR DISAGREE BY A FACTOR OF `7` ON THE SAME YEAR, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS STILL SNIPPET-GRADE

⏱️ **Pass 311 of `2026-10-19`.** 🔴 **Transport status, restated because it governs every figure
below:** no primary legal, government or market source was reached. `p310` confirmed that boundary
on **two** independent transports. 🔵 This pass narrows it in one direction and widens it in
another: **`github.com/topics/*` returns `403` on `curl`** while `raw.githubusercontent.com`
answers on `curl`, and the topic pages answer on the second transport. **The honest statement is
now per HOST *and* per PATH.**

### 🔴 The sizing spread, tabled rather than averaged

🔵 **`T37`/`p274`'s rule is that a quantity is re-derived from its rows, never splined between
them.** Four independent 2026-vintage estimates of the same market:

| source form | 2025 | 2026 | terminal | CAGR |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| IMARC-attributed roundup | `$6.4 B` | — | `$79.6 B` (2034) | `31.35 %` |
| second roundup | `$8.3 B` | `$11.4 B` | `$57.2 B` (2033) | — |
| third roundup | `$7.52 B` | `$10.6 B` | — | — |
| 🔵 carried from `p310`/`p65` | `$9.58 B` | — | `$42.48 B` (2030) | `41.5 %` |

🔴 **The 2025 column alone spans `$6.4 B` to `$9.58 B` — a 50 % spread — and the terminal values
span `$42.48 B` to `$79.6 B` on dates four years apart.** 🔵 **Do not average these and do not
quote a single one as "the market".** For a client deck the defensible statement is the SHAPE:
every source agrees on a high-twenties-to-low-forties CAGR off a high-single-digit-billions 2025
base. 🟡 **Nothing here is a primary source**; all four are search snippets of vendor research.

### 🔵 The one adoption asymmetry every source agrees on, and it is the sellable insight

| measure | value | source grade |
|---|---|---|
| students using AI | `66 %` (2024) → **`92 %`** (2025) | 🟡 vendor roundup |
| institutions with formal AI guidelines | 🔴 **`10 %`** | 🟡 vendor roundup |
| US teachers lacking AI training | 🔴 **`71 %`** | 🟡 vendor roundup |
| sector maturity | 🔴 **`35 / 100`** — most institutions still in pilot | 🟡 vendor analysis |

🔴 **Student adoption is near-universal and institutional readiness is in the single and low double
digits.** 🔵 `p309` measured the same gap from a different channel (`62 %` of students using AI for
homework by `2025-12` against `18 %` of teachers with any formal guidance) and `p310` showed the
consequence: the problem class the market's own numbers point at — **integrity, governance,
assessment design** — was structurally invisible to a keyword set built from solutions. **Two
independent channels, one gap, and it is a services gap rather than a product gap.**

🟡 **Segment mix, single-source and unverified:** STEM `34.78 %` of revenue · language learning the
fastest-growing segment · K-12 `45.62 %` of adoption.

## Opportunities by region

🔵 **Every regional figure below is snippet-grade. Where a region returned nothing
education-specific, that is written down as a measured gap rather than left blank** — an informed
gap is information; a blank looks exactly like coverage.

### North America

🟢 **The best-quantified region, and the one with the sharpest regulatory vacuum.**

- **Market:** `$951 M` (2024) → `$2 303.2 M` (2029), **`15.9 %`** CAGR (MarketsandMarkets,
  snippet). 🔴 **Note the CAGR is roughly HALF the global figure** while NA holds the largest
  regional share (`36–37.5 %`, 2025). 🔵 Both cannot be comfortably true: a dominant region growing
  at half the global rate implies the growth is elsewhere. **Flagged as a contradiction between two
  snippet sources, not resolved.**
- **Regulation:** 🔴 **described as a relative vacuum** — no sector regulator for educational
  technology, adoption decided school-by-school and district-by-district, with
  **Colorado and Texas** named as introducing piecemeal state requirements.
- **Named 2026 movements:** OpenAI launched a country-level education programme with **eight
  national partners** (Q1 2026); **Carnegie Mellon + the Gates Foundation, `$55 M`** into AI
  courseware aimed at gateway college courses. 🟡 A `$169 M` government commitment to higher-ed AI
  (Q1 2026) appeared in blog-grade material only and is recorded as unverified.
- 🟢 **Supply-side placement, FIRST-HAND:** `The Regents of the University of California` holds the
  `MIT` grant on [`GradeBridge-Student-Submission`](https://github.com/BridgeSuite/GradeBridge-Student-Submission)
  (`1 100` B · `6203d12e65d7`) — read from the payload this pass, not from a search snippet.
- 🔵 **The opportunity:** `10 %` guideline coverage against `92 %` student use, in the region with
  the most money and the least rulemaking. **Governance-and-assessment-design engagements, not
  tutor builds.**

### EMEA

🟢 **The only region where the regulatory obligation is concrete and dated.**

- **Regulation:** the **EU AI Act classifies education AI as high-risk** (Annex III(3)). 🟢 `p310`
  established the adjacent boundary first-hand on code: proctoring systems computing blink ratios,
  gaze and audio energy are **high-risk, not prohibited** — while anything inferring emotion or
  physiological state in education is **prohibited outright under Art 5(1)(f)**, and has been since
  **2 February 2025**. 🔴 A commissioned system is a compliance deliverable, not just a product.
- **Adoption, with a real denominator:** Eurostat 2025 enterprise AI adoption spans
  **`5.21 %` (Romania) to `42.03 %` (Denmark)**. 🔵 An eightfold intra-region spread, which is why
  "EMEA" is not a single go-to-market.
- **Barriers, ranked:** lack of relevant expertise **`70.89 %`**, legal uncertainty **`52.52 %`**
  (EESC, citing Eurostat). 🟢 **Both are services line-items.**
- 🟡 Growth is reported at roughly **`30 %` YoY** (AWS EMEA leadership, Jan 2026 interview) and the
  recurring warning is **inconsistent national implementation producing a patchwork**.
- 🔴 **Education-specific gap, stated:** this pass's EMEA searches returned **no ministry-level
  AI-in-schools programme and no education-sector adoption figure**. The regulatory and
  skills material above is economy-wide, applied to education by inference.
- 🔵 **The opportunity:** conformity assessment, Annex III documentation and Art 5(1)(f) screening
  as a billable practice — the one region where the buyer has a deadline.

### APAC

🟡 **The thinnest region on this channel, and the honest result is a near-nil.**

- 🔴 **This pass's APAC searches returned no education-specific regulation and no
  education-specific adoption figure.** The results were economy-wide enterprise-AI material. That
  is a measured outcome of the queries run, not a claim that none exists.
- **Named education-adjacent movements:** **TCS + Pearson** multi-year AI learning alliance aimed

- 🟡 **`49 %`** of APAC businesses cite insufficient real-time data infrastructure as an adoption

- 🟢 **Supply-side placement, FIRST-HAND:** `Sejong University` holds the `MIT` grant on
  [`ExamShield`](https://github.com/MORSHEDMDMONOARUL/ExamShield) (`p310`, `1 108` B ·
  `6176918ccd77`). 🔴 **With the standing Art 5(1)(f) caveat `p310` found in its roadmap** — pin the
  commit and exclude the biometric roadmap item contractually.
- 🔵 **The opportunity, stated as a hypothesis rather than a finding:** sovereignty requirements plus

### LATAM

🟡 **Regulation is well-covered; the education market is not, and the one number available is
narrower than it looks.**

- **Market:** `$18.2 M` (2024) → `$36 M` (2029), **`12.1 %`** CAGR. 🔴 **Read the label: this is
  *"Rest of Latin America"*** — a residual bucket that excludes the largest economies, so it is NOT

- 🔵 **Chile's `Ley 21.719`** — carried, **corroborated twice**, binds **`2026-12-01`** unless
  `18.623-07` is enacted. 🟡 **Brief the contradictory public reading alongside it**, per the
  standing instruction; not re-litigated this pass.
- **Education-specific policy:** a 2025 comparative study covers AI regulation in **higher
  education across Argentina, Brazil, Chile, Peru and Spain**, framed as a gap analysis. **Mexico**

- 🔴 **Gap, stated:** this pass's LATAM searches returned **no named 2026 education-sector player
  and no ministry-level rollout**. 🟡 **And LATAM is a NIL on the code channel for a second pass

  Spanish-language holder line read is a `PERSON`, which places nobody.
- 🔵 **The opportunity:** the region is legislating before it is buying. **Compliance-readiness and
  data-residency work ahead of Chile's December date** is the engagement that exists now, and

## 🔴 Pass 310 — TWO PASSES, TWO INDEPENDENT QUERY SETS, THE SAME `~80 %` ALREADY-CLAIMED RATE: THE REGIONAL CHANNEL HAS A MEASURED CEILING, NOT A BAD DAY

⏱️ **Pass 310 of `2026-10-19`.** Five fresh regional searches (North America, EMEA, APAC,
LATAM ×2, one of them Spanish-language), **25 tokens run through `make claims` before
publication**.

### 🔴 The ceiling, with both denominators side by side

| pass | tokens tested | already claimed | new | rate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `p309` | 22 | 18 | 4 | **82 %** |
| `p310` | 25 | **20** | **5** | **80 %** |

🔵 **`p309` could report this as a result; `p310` can report it as a PROPERTY.** Two passes,
different query languages, different regions emphasised, and the marginal yield is **4–5 new
tokens either way**. The regional channel at search-snippet grade is not having an off day — it
is **enumerated**, and a third pass of the same searches would be expected to return the same
handful. 🔴 **Where it has to go next is primary sources, and those are refused** (below).

🟢 **The `5` genuinely new tokens:** `AI Singapore` · `30 billion won` · `Índice Latinoamericano
de IA` · `ConectIA` · `190 universidades`. 🔵 **And one of the five is a near-duplicate stated
as such:** `AI Singapore` is the parent of `AICET`, which was **already claimed**. **So the
honest count is 4 of 25 = 16 %**, not 20 %.

### 🔴 "No primary source" — confirmed on a SECOND, INDEPENDENT channel

`ACTION p309-R` item 8 says not to re-litigate this, and it is not re-litigated: **the same
hosts were tested through a channel no previous pass had.** A page-fetch channel distinct from
`curl` was available this pass and was put against eight primary hosts.

| host | `curl` | the second channel |
|---|---|---|
| `bcn.cl` (Chile, Ley 21.719) · `congress.gov` · `eur-lex.europa.eu` · `artificialintelligenceact.eu` · `education.gov.in` · `moe.go.kr` · `unesco.org` · `huggingface.co` | 🔴 **`000` on all eight** | 🔴 **`ENOTFOUND`** |
| `github.com` · `raw.githubusercontent.com` | 🟢 200 | 🟢 200 |

🔵 **That changes the grade of the finding, not its direction.** *"No primary legal, market or
government source"* is no longer one channel's behaviour that a better `curl` might fix — it is
an **allowlist boundary reproduced on two independent transports**. 🟢 **Every regulatory and
market figure in this file is therefore SEARCH-SNIPPET GRADE, stated once here so it need not be
restated per cell.**

### 🟢 One standing conclusion independently corroborated, and the corroboration is NOT a re-litigation

A Spanish-language LATAM search returned Chile's **Ley 21.719** with its entry into force at
**`2026-12-01`** — and *also* returned a source asserting it has been in force since December
2024. 🟢 **`p309` settled this: the date binds `2026-12-01` unless `18.623-07` is enacted.**
🔵 **The new fact is not the date — it is that the contradictory reading is live in the public
record and will be quoted at a Globant team by a client's own counsel.** Brief the date **and
the contradiction**, or the correction will arrive from the other side of the table.

## Opportunities by region

🔵 Each row below is an opportunity **this pass's own measurements** support, not a restatement
of the market sizing above. Grades are stated: `CODE` = first-hand payload; `SNIPPET` = search
summary.

### North America

- 🟢 **`CODE` — the first first-hand NA placement this base has from a licence payload.**
  [`BridgeSuite/GradeBridge-Student-Submission`](https://github.com/BridgeSuite/GradeBridge-Student-Submission)
  is MIT and held by **`The Regents of the University of California`**. 🔵 A UC-held MIT
  submission front end is a credible reference artefact in any US public-university pitch.
- 🟡 **`SNIPPET` — the policy surface is STATE-level and it is moving.** Nearly **100 state
  bills** tracked this session touching student AI use; **over half of US states** have issued
  K-12 AI guidance; named instruments include **Maryland SB 720** (state guidance + mandatory
  local policies), **Idaho SB 1227** (AI may not replace human teachers) and **Arizona HB 4040**
  (K-12 *and* public universities must adopt student-AI-use policies). Federal **H.R. 8747**
  (K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026) has advanced through committee and **is not law**.
- 🟢 **The engagement shape this implies is POLICY-TO-PRODUCT, not a platform.** Fifty
  jurisdictions writing incompatible student-AI-use rules is a **configuration** problem.
  Pair `sam-illingworth/Integrity-Debt-Audit` (assessment resilience) with
  `bruchris/canvas-lms-mcp`'s FERPA pseudonymisation mode — both MIT, both already on these
  shelves — and the deliverable is a per-district policy configuration, not a build.
- 🔴 **Do not pitch detection as the answer.** `Open-Detector` (MIT, 249★, carried since `p97`)
  leads the **AI-text-detection sub-tier** — not the axis, where `dodona-edu/dolos` sits at 350★
  — and this base **takes no position on its accuracy**: `p97` recorded that its *99.57 %* figure
  is the author's own unreplicated claim. US institutions are visibly split on AI-text detectors.
  **Sell assessment redesign, and offer detection as an input.**

### EMEA

- 🔴 **`SNIPPET` + 🟢 `CODE` — the hardest finding of the pass is here.** Under the EU AI Act,
  education systems used for **admission, evaluation and exam scoring** are **Annex III
  high-risk**, and **Art 5(1)(f) PROHIBITS inferring emotions in education outright**. 🟢 **This
  pass ran the screen on code rather than citing the article**: four camera/keystroke rows were
  cloned and read, and **none infers emotion today**
  ([ledger](../evidence/p310-art5-screen.2026-10-19-p310.tsv)).
- 🔴 **But `ExamShield`'s roadmap (`FUTURE_ROADMAP.md:174-175`) plans `Heart rate monitoring
  (via camera)` and `Stress level detection`.** 🔵 **The EMEA opportunity is exactly that gap:
  an Art 5(1)(f) + Annex III conformity screen delivered as a reusable instrument over whatever
  proctoring stack the institution already bought.** Every EU institution running proctoring
  needs this and almost none of them can read the code.
- 🟢 **`CODE` — EMEA-origin foundational supply exists and is permissive:**
  [`TUBAF-IFI-DiPiT/github2pandas`](https://github.com/TUBAF-IFI-DiPiT/github2pandas) (BSD, TU

  LRS (France Université Numérique).

  which favours integration over new platforms.

default is that it BINDS on `2026-12-01`, and the client-facing advice is to plan for that date

jurisdictions re-read this pass.**

🔴 **South Korea unwound its AI-textbook programme.** In **`2026-08` the National Assembly

textbook programme" as a reference deployment — it was reversed by the legislature.** 🟢 **What
is still expanding there is narrower and more sellable: an AI Korean-language platform for

ministry's own Student Learning Space, teacher-supervised**, explicitly not on open platforms;

training at all levels is due in 2026. 🔵 **An engagement here integrates with SLS or it does
not happen** → `P94`.

COMPULSORY from Class 3 in the `2026-27` academic year**, backed by the IndiaAI Mission.
🔴 **Single secondary source; verify before quoting a number of students.**

🟡 **Japan is deliberately slow** — guidelines published with a selected set of pilot schools,
characterised as *"AI last, not first"*, with official digital textbooks moving on a separate
track. 🟡 **Australia** has national guidance on responsible school AI use and a flagged
education-ministers' discussion later in 2026.

🔴 **And the demand-side number that should change a pitch:** the **Ipsos Education Monitor
2026** puts support for **banning** AI in schools at **23% (Indonesia) to 31% (South Korea)**
across APAC — but **52% in Australia and 47% in New Zealand**. 🔵 **AU/NZ is the most
AI-restrictive public opinion in the region, and it is where English-language pitches go first.**

### 🟢 North America — the US is now the best-evidenced procurement surface in this base

🟢 **Roughly `33`–`35` states plus Puerto Rico have official AI-in-education guidance, up from
~`28` in early 2025.** 🔴 **Two secondary trackers; the range is theirs and is not reconciled.**
🟢 **What matters is the subset that MANDATES a district policy, with a date:**

| jurisdiction | instrument | deadline | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Ohio** | district AI policy mandate + state model policy | 🟢 **`2026-07-01`** | 🟢 **IN FORCE — already passed** |
| **Illinois** | ISBE statewide guidance duty | 🟢 `2026-07-01` | 🟢 IN FORCE. 🔵 No district adoption deadline of its own |
| **North Carolina** | state model, then district policies | 🟡 `2026-12-31` → `2027-06-30` | 🟡 PENDING, dated. 🔴 One tracker, uncorroborated |
| **Oklahoma** | `S.B. 1734` | 🟡 before the `2027-28` school year | 🟢 ENACTED. 🔴 **Parental OPT-OUT, no academic penalty** |
| **Maryland** | `SB 720` | 🟡 **120 days after** state guidance is released | 🟢 ENACTED, clock conditional |
| **Idaho** | `SB 1227` | 🟡 framework approved `2026-08` *(contested)* | 🔴 **CONFLICTED** — one source says approved, another says K-12 standards are still draft |
| **Michigan** | MDE AI guidance | `2026-05-12` | 🟡 PUBLISHED, **guidance only**, no mandate |
| **Virginia** | `HB1186` / `SB394` | `2026-03` introduced | 🔴 **CONFLICTED — enacted vs pending. NOT carried as fact** |
| **Texas · Pennsylvania** | a statewide guidance document | as of `2026-09` | 🔴 **DOES NOT EXIST** (informed absence, 🔴 **~6 weeks stale, not re-verified**) |

🔵 **Why this beats every other regulatory finding in this base commercially:** an Ohio district
is already out of compliance if it has no policy, and a North Carolina district has a **dated
deadline ten weeks out**. 🟢 **A dated, budgeted, district-level obligation is a procurement
hook; an advisory framework is not.** 🔵 **It is the same shape as `p307`'s Ontario finding
(*"Cyber Safety and Artificial Intelligence" as one of four mandatory PA-day topics*), at
roughly fifty times the scale.**

🟢 **Canada is carried UNCHANGED from `p307` and was deliberately not re-searched.**

### 🟡 EMEA — one hard number, and a declared regulatory nil for the four largest markets

🔴 **The OECD Digital Education Outlook 2026 is the EMEA sales problem in one figure: teacher
agreement that AI helps build lesson plans runs from `18%` in FRANCE to `91%` in Viet Nam, and

floor, and France is also building a state *sovereign AI* for teacher lesson planning — which
makes the French state a competitor to a studio pitch rather than a customer.**

🟡 **France** teaches AI literacy through **Pix** (prompting, system functioning, data) with the
**CREIA** teacher community. 🟡 **Netherlands**: **Kennisnet** publishes generative-AI guidance
and model agreements for schools; **NOLAI** (National Education Lab AI) builds and tests
responsible school AI. 🔴 **A claim that staff AI training became a legal duty in `2025-02` is
poorly sourced and is NOT carried.** 🟡 **Spain** has a national reference framework citing the
EU AI Act, GDPR and LOPDGDD, suggesting local AI action plans — 🔵 **a framework, not a rollout.**
🟡 **Germany**: AI use is allowed with rules set by the individual school, under federal
privacy-by-design/DPIA expectations; 🟢 **`p306`'s `§ 115b Abs. 9 SchG` + `§ 6 DUVO`
(Baden-Württemberg) remains the only legally binding school-AI rule found in the country, and it
BANS AI FROM AWARDING GRADES.**

🔴 **Declared nil, so the silence does not read as coverage: no education-specific national AI
instrument was found for Germany, France, Spain or the Netherlands.** The EU AI Act's general
obligations apply to all four. 🔵 **Annex III education duties bind on `2027-12-02` under
`Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`** — settled at `p233`, relied on at `p241`, and not re-opened here.

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟢 **The district-policy compliance engagement, and it is the only opportunity in this base with
an obligation that has ALREADY lapsed.** Ohio districts were required to have adopted an AI
policy by **`2026-07-01`**; North Carolina's district deadline is **`2027-06-30`** with the state
model due **`2026-12-31`**; Maryland's clock is **120 days** from a guidance release. 🔵 **Sell
the artefact the mandate names — a policy, an inventory of tools in use, a data-protection
position — and attach the platform work to it.** 🟢 **`p309` adds the teacher-enablement
engagement, and it is the better-evidenced one: `62%` of middle-school and college students used
AI for homework by `2025-12` while **`18%`** of teachers report any formal guidance. Sell teacher
capacity, delivered through an AI grading assist that leaves the mark with the teacher**
→ `P95`. 🔴 **Oklahoma's `S.B. 1734` makes a
**per-learner AI opt-out with no academic penalty** an architectural requirement, which no
platform row on [`verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) currently declares** →
`P93`. 🟢 **Canada's hook is `p307`'s: a mandatory, budgeted PA-day topic for `2026-27`.**

### EMEA

🟢 **Sell the human-in-the-loop REVIEW STEP, not the generation.** Baden-Württemberg bans AI from
**awarding grades**, and a platform with a built-in review gate is architecturally on the right
side of that line. 🟢 **`Open-TutorAi/open-tutor-ai-CE` (BSD-3-Clause) is the demo asset**, and
🟢 **`Selleo/mentingo` (MIT, Poland) is now a permissive, EMEA-origin LMS option** — see
[`verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) for its honest adoption caveat. 🔴 **France
is the hardest sell in the OECD set (`18%` teacher agreement) AND is building a state sovereign
AI: treat it as competitor territory, not a market.** 🔵 **The Netherlands is the opposite — NOLAI
and Kennisnet mean a buyer with an existing evaluation framework to plug into.**

### APAC

🔴 **Do not lead with autonomy anywhere in this region.** 🟢 **Singapore: build INSIDE the
Student Learning Space, teacher-supervised; the ministry has already named cognitive offloading
as the thing to avoid, so a proposal that pre-empts it is halfway in** → `P94`. 🟢 **India is the
volume play: AI compulsory from Class 3 from `2026-27`, which is a teacher-training and
materials-generation problem at national scale, not a tutoring problem.** 🔴 **South Korea:
reposition. The AI-textbook programme was stripped of official status in `2026-08`; the live
money is in the narrow, high-need case — the immigrant-background Korean-language platform.**
🔴 **Australia/New Zealand: `52%`/`47%` support an outright ban, so an AU pitch must be framed as
governance and teacher support first.** 🟡 **Japan: pilot-school framing only.**

### LATAM

🟢 **The best reference deployment in the world for this vertical is already here — PUC Chile's
`194` instructor-configured agents over ~`100` courses.** 🔵 **Sell that pattern, by name, with
its measured student response.** 🟢 **Mexico's entry point is named and non-ministerial: the
UNESCO LAC Observatory pilot with CONALEP and DGETI, plus `190`+ universities with AI
programmes.** 🟢 **Colombia's hook is `CONPES 4144`'s digital-talent objective.** 🔴 **In both
countries the education ministry is still absent from this base's evidence — pitch to
institutions and to the science/technology side, not to the ministry.** 🟢 **Chile: plan for
`2026-12-01` and sell the delay as the contingency — CORRECTED `p309` from *"do not quote the
`Ley 21.719` date to a client this quarter; it is contested"*.** The postponement bill
(`18.623-07`) is still in **first trámite** with six weeks to run, so the date **binds unless it
is enacted**, and *"contested"* invited the opposite inference. 🔴 **The enforcing Agency is still
not constituted**, and until `2027-11-30` the Agency may answer a smaller firm with a **written
warning instead of a fine** — 🟡 one source only. 🟢 **Brazil: the compliance surface has TWO owners (MEC and Justice/Human
Rights), which is a reason to scope a compliance artefact rather than assume one regulator.**

### Global

🟢 **The honest cross-region pitch is instructor-controlled, narrow-scope agents with a review
gate** — it is what the `474`-study review supports, what PUC Chile measured, what
Baden-Württemberg's grading ban requires, and what Singapore's supervision model mandates.
🔵 **Four regions, four different reasons, one architecture.**

## 🟢 Pass 307 — CANADA IS SETTLED ON BOTH OPEN QUESTIONS, AND THE MOST ACTIONABLE LATAM DATUM THIS BASE CARRIES IS NOW IN DOUBT SIX WEEKS BEFORE IT BINDS

⏱️ **Pass 307 of `2026-10-19`.** Evidence:
[`p307-regional.2026-10-19-p307.tsv`](../evidence/p307-regional.2026-10-19-p307.tsv) ·
[`p307-new-supply.2026-10-19-p307.tsv`](../evidence/p307-new-supply.2026-10-19-p307.tsv).

🟡 **Provenance grade for everything in this section: SEARCH-SNIPPET.** 🔴 **No primary
legal or government source is reachable from this environment** — `eur-lex.europa.eu`,
`ec.europa.eu`, `gesetze-im-internet.de`, `ontario.ca`, `unesdoc.unesco.org` and
`oecd.org` all refuse (`curl` → `000`, `WebFetch` → `ENOTFOUND`). 🔵 **Addresses listed per
`T-p306a`'s rule, so the absence is informed rather than inherited.**

### 🟢 `ACTION p306-R` item 6 — Canada, and BOTH open questions close

🟢 **`p306` carried an unresolved Ontario conflict and a suspicion about Alberta. Both are
settled, and the Alberta suspicion was right.**

🟢 **Ontario — the conflict resolves as *"AI rides inside other instruments; there is no AI
memorandum."*** The Ministry's own **Mandatory Professional Activity Days for 2026-27**
makes **"Cyber Safety and Artificial Intelligence" one of four mandatory PA-day topics**,
with an appendix covering AI and digital-literacy learning in the curriculum, educator
cyber-security, and the duty to safeguard student data when using AI tools.
**Policy/Program Memorandum 172** separately requires French-language boards to include
emerging technologies such as AI in in-service training. 🔴 **Neither is an AI memorandum,
and no AI-specific PPM was found.** 🔵 **For a studio this is the better finding: a
mandatory PA-day topic is a DATED, BUDGETED, province-wide training obligation, which is a
procurement hook an advisory memorandum is not.** 🟡 **One third-party guide asserts the
ministry's index contains no AI memorandum; not independently verified, carried as
corroboration only.**

🔴 **Alberta — `p306` was right.** The document circulating as Alberta's guidance is the
**Alberta School Boards Association**'s *AI Policy Guidance* **v1.0, 2024-09-13**, built on
principles from a University of Alberta team. 🔴 **It is not the ministry's, and no
provincial ministry framework was found.** 🟢 **Policy is being made at the DIVISION
level**: Northern Gateway Public Schools' board policy (reported `2026-07`) leaves the
teacher authority over classroom deployment; **Lethbridge School Division's 2025/26
generative-AI statement requires a full Privacy Impact Assessment before any generative-AI
model is approved division-wide.** 🔵 **That PIA requirement is the single most concrete
procurement gate found in Canada this pass.** 🟡 **A Fraser Institute-commissioned survey
puts ~63 % of teachers as having received no training on instructing students in reasonable
AI use** — demand-side evidence for the Ontario PA-day hook.

🟢 **This confirms `T-p306e` on a second continent: the binding instruments are
SUB-NATIONAL** — Länder in Germany, states in the US, **and now school DIVISIONS in
Alberta, which is one level below the province.**

### 🔴 LATAM — Chile's date, which `p306` called the region's most actionable datum, is now IN DOUBT

🔴 **`p306` carried *"Chile Ley 21.719 in force `2026-12-01`, ~6 weeks out — the most
actionable LATAM datum here."*** 🟡 **It needs three qualifications, and a studio briefing
that omits them is wrong:**

1. 🔴 **A postponement bill is live.** Boletín **18.623-07**, filed `2026-09-01`, *suma
   urgencia* renewed in October, **still in first trámite**. The alternative date
   (December 2027) applies **only** if it passes.
2. 🔴 **The regulator does not exist.** The Agencia de Protección de Datos was **not
   constituted as of October 2026** — the Senate rejected the proposed *terna* in May.
3. 🟡 **The sources disagree with each other** on whether the sanctions regime is staged.

🟡 **Provenance: all of it is compliance-consultancy blogs. The Diario Oficial and the
Congress bulletin are both unreachable from here.** 🔵 **Brief it as *"a date under active
challenge, with no regulator seated"* — which is a different engagement conversation from
*"six weeks to comply."***

🟢 **Brazil placed twice, and both are new.** The **MEC guide on AI in basic education
(`2026-03`)** frames classroom routines for questioning model outputs and protecting
personal data. Separately, a **federal guide on children's AI use published `2026-10-15`**
— 🔵 **four days before this pass** — comes from the **justice and human-rights**
ministries, not education, and addresses families, young people and educators.
🔵 **Two ministries issuing AI guidance into schools from different directions is the
pattern to watch; it is how a compliance surface becomes multi-owner.**

🟢 **Peru enters the base** with a World Bank evaluation of AI in Latin American schools
(`2026-07-02`) — 🔵 **the only outcome EVIDENCE, as opposed to policy, surfaced for the
region in this pass.**

🔴 **Mexico and Colombia: an INFORMED gap.** A ministry-level school-AI instrument was
searched for and not found in either. One undated blog claims Mexico has no mandatory
AI-literacy strand and one cites a 2023 Colombian general AI bill that does not address
schools; 🔴 **neither is carried as a finding.**

### 🔴 APAC — searched, and nothing new. This is a declared gap, not coverage.

🟢 **A jurisdiction-named search (Japan, Australia, India, Korea — `p305`'s fix, which is
the correct query shape) returned NO 2026 school-AI instrument for any of the four.**
🔵 **One roundup places Korea on AI language tools and Japan on official digital textbooks
— education developments, not regulation.** 🔴 **`p305`'s five APAC jurisdictions are
CARRIED UNCHANGED and are now two passes old.** 🟡 **A risk-tiered APAC compliance guide
lists education as high-risk with a September 2027 deadline but names no jurisdiction;
NOT CARRIED.**

🔵 **Worth stating plainly, because silence would look like coverage: `p305`'s query fix
locates rules that EXIST. Running it twice and finding nothing new is evidence the 2026
window is quiet in APAC, not evidence the fix stopped working.**

### 🟢 EMEA — the region gains SUPPLY rather than regulation this pass

🟢 **[`Open-TutorAi/open-tutor-ai-CE`](https://github.com/Open-TutorAi/open-tutor-ai-CE)
places EMEA on the SUPPLY side for the first time at platform scale** — BSD-3-Clause,
111★ / 193 forks, UI in **Arabic, French and English**, authored by *R2D-dev*.
🔵 **A tutoring platform that ships Arabic and French interfaces as first-class is
directly usable in Maghreb, Gulf and francophone-Africa engagements, and nothing else on
these shelves is.** 🔴 **Germany, France, Spain and the Netherlands were NOT re-searched;
`p305` and `p306`'s readings are carried unchanged.**

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟢 **Ontario's mandatory PA-day topic is a dated, province-wide training obligation** —
sell the educator-enablement programme against it, not an AI platform.
🟢 **Alberta's live gate is the Privacy Impact Assessment**, division by division
(Lethbridge is the worked example): the sellable artefact is a **reusable PIA pack plus
model-inventory tooling**, and the open-source anchor for the governance half is
[`microsoft/agent-governance-toolkit`](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-governance-toolkit)
(🟢 MIT, payload-pinned `p307`).
🟢 **US: 31 state guidance documents, Ohio district policies due `2026-07-01`, Utah local
policy by `2027-07`** — carried from `p306`, unchanged.
🔵 **The two markets diverge: the US buys policy-conformance tooling at STATE level; Canada
buys it at DIVISION level, which means many small buyers and a template product rather
than a bespoke engagement.**

### EMEA

🟢 **A permissive, self-hostable tutoring platform now exists for this region**
([`open-tutor-ai-CE`](https://github.com/Open-TutorAi/open-tutor-ai-CE), BSD-3-Clause,
Docker, Ollama): 🔵 **sovereignty-compatible, no copyleft to price, Arabic/French UI.**
🔴 **Baden-Württemberg's `§ 6 DUVO` still bans AI from awarding grades** (`p306`) — the
product boundary is *assist the teacher's judgement*, never *produce the grade*.
🟡 **Annex III education duties bind `2027-12-02` under `Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`**
(settled at `p233`/`p241`; restated here with its authority, not re-opened) — 🟢 **that is
a 14-month readiness window, which is a sellable programme.**

### APAC

🔴 **No new instrument found this pass.** 🔵 **The honest posture for an APAC engagement is
that this base's regulatory reading is two passes old** — `p305`'s China MoE *"AI Plus
Education"*, Singapore's Code for Fun and Student Learning Space boundary, India's Class 3
rollout, Korea's teacher-training setback, Japan carried as possibly stale.
🟢 **Singapore remains the only APAC jurisdiction on BOTH sides** — GovTech's
`open-attestation` (Apache-2.0) on supply, SLS on rules.
🟡 **Opportunity, stated as a gap to close rather than a lead: re-run the jurisdiction-named
search per country before any APAC pitch.**

### LATAM

🔴 **Do not sell Chile against a December 2026 deadline without the caveat** — the bill is
under challenge and the regulator is not seated. 🟢 **Sell the readiness work, which is
date-insensitive.**
🟢 **Brazil has TWO ministries issuing guidance** (MEC `2026-03`; justice + human rights
`2026-10-15`): 🔵 **a multi-owner compliance surface, which favours an advisory engagement
over a tool sale.**
🟢 **Peru's World Bank evaluation is the region's only outcome evidence** — 🔵 **cite it in
pitches; it is what LATAM buyers ask for and the region has almost none.**
🟢 **Credentialing: the region ADOPTS and does not BUILD** (`p306`, `P87`), on a substrate
that is MIT and verified. 🔵 **`p307` adds that the substrate's whole neighbourhood is
permissive — 23 grants across `digitalcredentials`, plus 6/6 more probed this pass, zero
reserved.** 🟢 **A LATAM credentialing build has no licence obstacle anywhere in its stack.**
🔴 **Mexico and Colombia remain an informed gap at ministry level.**

### Global

🟢 **The licence correction travels with every engagement**: three of this base's tutoring
systems are **GPL-3.0, not AGPL-3.0**, so they may be **hosted for a client with no
source-disclosure duty**. 🔵 **That moves `LTLTutor`, `logicits` and `tutor-gpt` from
*"study it, reimplement"* to *"deployable"*, and it is the single largest change to this
base's buildable surface this pass.**
🔴 **The AGPL constraint stands exactly where `p306` predicted: the systems of record**
([`frappe/lms`](https://github.com/frappe/lms),
[`instructure/canvas-lms`](https://github.com/instructure/canvas-lms)).
🟡 **Market size unchanged and still a factor-2.4 range** (`$9.58 bn` Precedence · `$10.6 bn`
R&M · `$12.3 bn` and `$23 bn` both HolonIQ) — 🔵 **brief the range, never a single HolonIQ
