## 🟢 Pass 306 — THE BINDING RULES ARE SUB-NATIONAL: `p305`'s JURISDICTION RULE WORKS, AND IT HAS TO GO ONE LEVEL DEEPER — BADEN-WÜRTTEMBERG HAS THE ONLY LEGALLY BINDING SCHOOL-AI RULE IN GERMANY, AND IT BANS AI GRADING OUTRIGHT

⏱️ **Pass 306 of `2026-10-19`.** Evidence:
[`p306-regional.2026-10-19-p306.tsv`](../evidence/p306-regional.2026-10-19-p306.tsv).
🔴 **Every regulatory and market figure below is SEARCH-SNIPPET-ATTRIBUTED.** 🟢 **The payload channel
restored this pass reaches `raw.githubusercontent.com` only — it is a channel to licence files, not to
ministries or market reports.** 🔵 **No primary legal instrument was opened.**

### 🟢 EMEA — `ACTION p305-R` item 5 discharged, and Germany's Länder level pays immediately

🟢 **`p305` established by reasoning that German school-AI rules live at Länder level and ran out of leg
before searching it. `p306` searched it, and the reasoning was right.**

| Land | instrument | date | 🔴 binding? |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **Baden-Württemberg** | 🔴 **`§ 115b Abs. 9 Schulgesetz` + `§ 6 DUVO`** (Digitalunterrichtsverordnung), `GBl. 2024 Nr. 81` | in force **`2024-10-10`** | 🔴 **YES — the only one** |
| 🟡 **Bayern** | *Handlungsleitfaden „Künstliche Intelligenz in der pädagogischen Praxis"*, plus `AIS.chat` and `ByCS-KI` | **`2026-05-19`** | 🟡 guidance |
| 🔴 **Nordrhein-Westfalen** | *Handlungsleitfaden zum Umgang mit textgenerierenden KI-Systemen* + a QUA-LiS Moodle course | 🔴 **`2023-02`** | 🟡 guidance |

🔴 **The finding worth briefing: `§ 6 DUVO` prohibits AI systems from AWARDING GRADES or making other
essential school decisions.** The Kultusministerium states the final assessment of pupil performance
remains reserved to the teacher, and that **Art. 9 GDPR special-category data may not be processed in AI
applications.** 🟢 **Permitted explicitly: supporting the individual learning path, e.g. differentiated
exercises.**

🔴 **This is a SECOND prohibition class, and it is not the one this base has been tracking.** EU AI Act
Art 5(1)(f) bans **emotion inference**; `§ 6 DUVO` bans **automated assessment**. 🔵 **Art 5(1)(f) hits a
technique almost nobody was going to ship. `§ 6 DUVO` hits the single most common pitch in the
vertical — "the AI grades it."** ⚠️ **A studio proposal that is perfectly clean under the AI Act can be
illegal in Baden-Württemberg.** → `P88`.

🟡 **Provenance, stated plainly: the `§ 115b/§ 6` citation and the 2024-10-10 date come from the
Kultusministerium's own pages as rendered in search results, corroborated by an independent September-2026
aggregator that also calls BW the only Land with binding rules.** 🔴 **The Gesetzblatt text itself was not
opened and the exact wording of `§ 6` is NOT verified.** 🔵 **Two sources, one of them the regulator,
agreeing on the operative effect — carried, with the wording flagged.**

🟢 **NRW's leading instrument is three and a half years old** (`2023-02`) and no newer Handreichung was
found — 🟡 **recorded as a STALENESS finding, not as an absence.**

### 🟢 North America — and it is TWO markets, not one

🟢 **United States — the state layer is where the binding duties are:**
- 🟢 **31 state education departments had published K-12 AI guidance as of `2026-01`** — mostly voluntary.
- 🔴 **Ohio: districts were required to adopt their own AI policy by `2026-07-01`** (a state model policy
  is available to adopt or adapt). **A deadline that has already passed.**
- 🔴 **Utah: a 2026 law phases in state-model and local-policy duties, local deadline `2027-07`.**
- 🟡 **Maryland SB 720** (department guidance + mandatory local policies, and an opt-out right for parents
  with no academic penalty) — 🔴 **passed first chamber only. Not law. Not carried as one.**

🟢 **Canada — a different shape entirely, and the first time this base has placed it:**
- 🔴 **Three provincial education ministries had published AI guidance as of `2025-12`.**
- 🟡 **British Columbia**: *Considerations for using AI tools in schools* (`2023`), seven areas.
- 🔴 **Alberta**: the guidance document is the **Alberta School Boards Association**'s
  (`2024-09`) — **a school-boards body, not the ministry.**
- 🔴 **Ontario**: no ministry memorandum on AI; boards such as Kawartha Pine Ridge have written their own.
  🟡 **One source describes 2026-27 Ontario expectations on responsible AI use; it could not be reconciled
  with the "no memorandum" finding and is CARRIED AS A CONFLICT, not resolved.**
- 🔴 **The governance gap, measured**: a Fraser Institute survey of Grades 6-12 teachers found **no
  province above 50 % for staff AI policy** (Ontario highest, **41.1 %**); for student policies only
  Ontario exceeded half (**50.7 %**). Training is worse and uneven — **67.6 % untrained in
  Manitoba/Saskatchewan against 37.0 % in Atlantic Canada.**

🔵 **Briefing consequence: "North America" is not a procurement territory for this product. A US K-12
engagement lands in a state with a policy deadline; a Canadian one lands in a district writing the policy
itself, because the ministry has not.** 🟢 **The Canadian deal is a governance deal first and a software
deal second.**

### 🟢 APAC — the credentialing leg places an institution, not just a rule

🟢 **[`Open-Attestation`](https://github.com/Open-Attestation/open-attestation) is built by Singapore
GovTech and is Apache-2.0** (payload 11 370 B, `3a932454c909`, read this pass). 🔵 **A government-built,
permissively licensed credentialing platform is a different kind of regional datum from a policy
document: it is a dependency a client can actually adopt, placed by the institution that wrote it.**
🟢 **It joins `p305`'s Singapore finding (Code for Fun, compulsory 10 h from P4, inside the Student
Learning Space rather than open platforms) — the same jurisdiction appearing on both the rule side and
the supply side.**

🔵 **`p305`'s five APAC jurisdictions (China, Singapore, India, Korea, Japan) were NOT re-searched** —
one pass old, and `ACTION p305-R` did not ask for them. 🔴 **Carried, not re-verified.**

### 🟡 LATAM — an adopter with no local supply, and a data-protection deadline six weeks out

🔴 **No school-level AI regulation was found in Brazil, Mexico, Chile or Colombia** — consistent across
four independent sources. 🟢 **This is an INFORMED GAP: the national AI instruments exist and the school
layer is genuinely empty, which is not the same as the search failing.**

- 🟡 **Chile** — National AI Policy since 2021; an AI bill still in committee. 🔴 **Ley 21.719 (personal
  data) enters into force `2026-12-01` — about six weeks from this pass.** 🔵 **The most actionable LATAM
  datum in this file: any Chilean education engagement scoped now will be delivered under a data-protection
  regime that is not yet in force.**
- 🟡 **Brazil** — no specific AI statute; **PL 2338/2023** approved by the Senate (`2024-12`), pending in
  the Câmara.
- 🟡 **Colombia** — national strategy, no sector-specific education rule.
- 🟡 **Mexico** — AI training folded into educational objectives; the binding constraint is **teacher
  capacity (>1 million to train)**, not law. 🔴 **A single source mentions a "Ley Nahui" — NOT
  corroborated, NOT carried.**

🟢 **The credentialing picture is the sharpest regional finding of the pass, and it is a SUPPLY gap rather
than a demand gap:**
- 🟢 **Tec de Monterrey has issued blockchain diplomas on the MIT-originated Blockcerts standard since
  2018.** Universidad Nacional de Río Negro (Argentina) issues micro-credentials on blockchain.
  A vendor claims ≥17 institutions across Chile, Peru, Colombia and the Dominican Republic.
- 🔴 **The newest flagship, NTT DATA + Darshana (`2026-05`), is built on `CertiDigital`/EBSI — a EUROPEAN
  platform — with W3C VC 2.0.**
- 🔴 **No LATAM-origin open-source credentialing project was found.** 🟢 **The region ADOPTS this layer and
  does not BUILD it.**
- 🟢 **And the substrate it already runs is permissive:**
  [`blockchain-certificates/cert-issuer`](https://github.com/blockchain-certificates/cert-issuer) is
  **MIT, 1 096 B `06d87bf42a0a`**, payload-read this pass → `P87`.

🔴 **Vendor-sourced adoption counts (POK, Thomas Signe, NTT DATA) are NOT independently verified** and are
recorded as directional.

### 🟡 Market size — the range WIDENED, and the honest instruction is unchanged

| source | 2026 figure |
|---|---|
| Precedence Research | **$9.58 bn** (and $7.05 bn for 2025) |
| Research and Markets | **$10.6 bn** |
| HolonIQ *(as cited by one aggregator)* | **$12.3 bn** |
| HolonIQ *(as cited by a different aggregator)* | 🔴 **$23 bn** |

🔴 **A factor of 2.4 between the extremes, and two of the four figures are attributed to the SAME firm.**
🟢 **`p305` recorded the disagreement as "a factor of two"; it is wider this pass, not narrower.**
🔵 **Brief the range. Never a point estimate — and never a HolonIQ number without saying which one.**

🟢 **Adoption is the better-evidenced number, and two independent instruments agree on level and
direction:** RAND reports K-12 teachers using generative AI for work doubling **25 % → 53 % in one school
year**; Gallup reports **34 % (2023) → 61 % (2026)**. 🔵 **Two surveys, two methodologies, the same
story — this is the figure to brief, not the market size.**

### 🟢 The demand-side finding that reframes the credentialing opportunity

🟢 **1EdTech's `2026-06` research (two reports) states the problem precisely: the technology EXISTS and is
not used consistently, because EMPLOYERS cannot find, trust or interpret credential data — so they fall
back on degrees and résumés.** The recommended fix is a **minimal, structured set of data elements**; the
companion report covers how credential data moves between education, employment and job platforms.
🟢 **1EdTech has partnered with the HR Open Standards Consortium.** 🟢 **The 2026 standards pair is **Open
Badges 3.0** on the **W3C Verifiable Credentials Data Model**.

🔴 **Join this to the supply finding and the gap is specific: the permissive open-source code in this
layer is overwhelmingly ISSUING and WALLET code. The failure is on the EMPLOYER side.** 🔵 **That is the
rarest thing in this knowledge base — a named, sourced demand gap sitting directly on top of a
payload-verified permissive substrate** → `P86`.

🟡 **Vendor blogs (sertifier, virtualbadge) were read and NOT carried** — unverified multiplier claims.

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟢 **The credentialing supply is here and it is permissive** — the Digital Credentials Commons (ex-Digital
Credentials Consortium, MIT-originated) ships 23 payload-verified grants, 22 of them MIT.
🟢 **The employer-side interpretation gap named by 1EdTech is a US-shaped problem** (skills-based hiring,
LERs, the HR Open Standards partnership) → **`P86` is a North America play first.**
🔴 **K-12 software engagements carry a policy-deadline dimension that varies by state** — Ohio's
`2026-07-01` has passed, Utah runs to `2027-07`. 🔵 **Ask which state before scoping.**
🟡 **Canada is a separate motion**: three provinces with guidance, Ontario without a ministry memo, and
teacher-reported policy coverage below 50 % everywhere. **Lead with governance artefacts, not features.**

### EMEA

🔴 **Baden-Württemberg's `§ 6 DUVO` is the hardest constraint in this file and it is sub-national**: no AI
grading, no Art. 9 GDPR data in AI applications. 🟢 **Design for it and you are clean everywhere else in
the EU** → **`P88`**.
🟢 **Permissive platform-scale supply exists here**:
[`walt-id/waltid-identity`](https://github.com/walt-id/waltid-identity) (Apache-2.0, 319★/137 forks,
Austria) is the largest permissive credentialing platform this base carries.
🟡 **The AGPL counterweight is also EMEA** —
[`edubadges/edubadges-server`](https://github.com/edubadges/edubadges-server) (AGPL-3.0, 34 519 B), the
Dutch SURF programme. 🔵 **Both options are available in-region; the choice is a pricing decision, which
is exactly what the LMS layer does not offer.**
🟡 **Germany's guidance layer is fragmented and partly stale** (NRW `2023-02`) — **a market for the
governance artefact itself.**

### APAC

🟢 **Singapore is the standout and it appears twice**: GovTech's
[`Open-Attestation`](https://github.com/Open-Attestation/open-attestation) (Apache-2.0) on the supply
side, and the Student Learning Space / Code for Fun rules on the demand side. 🔵 **A government that both
regulates and publishes permissive infrastructure is the cheapest APAC entry point in this base.**
🟡 **China's MoE "AI Plus Education" plan (`2026-04-10` → 2030) remains the largest stated programme**,
carried from `p305` with its *"mandatory"* framing still recorded as a CONFLICT.
🔴 **No APAC jurisdiction was re-searched this pass** — one pass old, carried.

### LATAM

🟢 **The clearest opportunity in this file, because it is a supply gap rather than a demand gap.** The
region already issues verifiable credentials (Tec de Monterrey on Blockcerts since 2018; UNRN Argentina;
claimed adoption across Chile, Peru, Colombia, Dominican Republic) and the newest flagship runs on a
**European** platform. 🟢 **The substrate is MIT and verified**
([`cert-issuer`](https://github.com/blockchain-certificates/cert-issuer), 1 096 B `06d87bf42a0a`) →
**`P87`**.
🔴 **Scope any Chilean engagement against Ley 21.719, in force `2026-12-01`.**
🔴 **No school-level AI regulation in BR/MX/CL/CO** — 🟢 **for a studio this lowers the compliance barrier
and raises the governance-advisory opportunity; it does not remove the EU-style duties for any client
operating cross-border.**
🟡 **Mexico's binding constraint is teacher training at scale (>1 M), not law** — a services shape, not a
software shape.

### Global

🟢 **Brief the adoption number, not the market number**: teacher use roughly doubled in a year on two
independent surveys, while 2026 market estimates span **$9.58 bn to $23 bn**.
🟢 **The layer rule is the most portable finding of the pass**: in **credentialing** a permissive
platform-scale option exists; in **LMS** it does not, after 26 draws; in **tutoring** the best pedagogy is
AGPL. 🔵 **Pick the layer before picking the licence strategy.**
🔴 **Two prohibition regimes now bite education AI and they are different**: EU AI Act Art 5(1)(f)
(emotion inference, in force `2025-02-02`, no consent exception) and sub-national rules on **automated
assessment** (`§ 6 DUVO`). 🟢 **Response-data mastery modelling satisfies both.**

## 🟢 Pass 305 — `p304`'s PRE-REGISTERED QUERY FIX WORKED ON EMEA, AND THE SAME FIX THEN BROKE APAC OPEN TOO: NAMING JURISDICTIONS INSTEAD OF REGIONS IS THE INSTRUMENT, NOT A PHRASING PREFERENCE

⏱️ **Pass 305 of `2026-10-19`.** Evidence:
[`p305-regional.2026-10-19-p305.tsv`](../evidence/p305-regional.2026-10-19-p305.tsv) ·
[`p305-prohibited-practice.2026-10-19-p305.tsv`](../evidence/p305-prohibited-practice.2026-10-19-p305.tsv).

🔴 **`p304` recorded EMEA as a declared gap — *"returned nothing education-specific"* — and PRE-REGISTERED
the remedy: drop the acronym "EMEA", name jurisdictions.** 🟢 **`p305` ran it. It paid four jurisdictions.**
🟢 **Then the same fix was applied to APAC, where `p304` reported *"only corporate training; zero K-12 or
university rules"* — and it paid five.** 🔵 **Two regions, one query change: the acronym was the defect.
A region is not a search term because no ministry publishes to a region.**

### 🟢 EMEA — four jurisdictions, and the regulation finally has a NUMBER

| jurisdiction | instrument | date | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **France** | **Cadre d'usage de l'IA en éducation** (Ministry framework) | `2025-06` | 🟢 **IN FORCE — the most formal national framework in the region.** Covers teacher *and* student AI literacy (OECD *Digital Education Outlook 2026*) |
| 🟡 **Spain** | Ministerio de Educación recommendation | `2026-04` | 🟡 **NON-BINDING.** Endorses AI literacy from secondary; **devolves tool choice to the regions.** Pilots *"IA en el aula"* in **Catalonia** and **Andalusia** |
| 🟡 **Netherlands** | **NOLAI** (National Education Lab AI) | ongoing | 🟡 **A PROGRAMME, NOT A RULE** — government + academia + industry + schools co-designing GenAI tools. No ministry rulebook surfaced |
| 🔴 **Germany** | **none AI-specific for schools** | — | 🔴 **DECLARED GAP, with its reason:** education is **Länder** competence, so national guidance is the wrong place to look. **Digitalpakt 2.0** (in force `2026-09-01`, **€5 bn to 2030**) is general digitalisation, not AI policy |

🟢 **The EU-level instrument is now identified by NUMBER, not just by mechanism.** `p303` named the
mechanism (*"the `Digital Omnibus on AI`"*); 🟢 **`p305` has it as `Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`, in force
`2026-07-27`** — Annex III high-risk moved to **`2027-12-02`**, Annex I to **`2028-08-02`**.
🔴 **And a thing `p303`/`p304` did not carry: Article 50 transparency duties were NOT delayed.** ⚠️ **So the
Omnibus is not a blanket reprieve — the disclosure obligations bite on the original timetable while the
conformity work slipped.**

🔴 **THE BINDING CONSTRAINT IN EMEA IS NOT A FUTURE DEADLINE — IT IS ALREADY IN FORCE.** **AI Act Article
5(1)(f) prohibits AI inferring emotions in educational institutions, and it has applied since
`2025-02-02`.** Public **and** private, **all levels**, in-person **and** online, 🔴 **no consent
exception**; the only carve-out is **medical or safety** purpose.

⚠️ **A date this pass nearly published wrong, recorded because the save is the lesson:** a first snippet
placed Art 5(1)(f) at *"December 2026"*, conflating it with the Omnibus's two genuinely **new** Article 5
prohibitions. 🟢 **A second targeted search placed it at `2025-02-02` before anything was written up** —
`ACTION SH` discipline, and the fifth time this base has stopped a confident wrong date at the door.

🟢 **OECD DEO 2026 measurement:** **nine** systems were developing or planning GenAI guidance — Greece,
Latvia, Lithuania, Luxembourg, **Netherlands**, Portugal, Slovakia, Slovenia, Switzerland.
🔵 **Useful as a pipeline: these are the jurisdictions where a governance product has a buyer and no
incumbent framework.**

### 🟢 APAC — five jurisdictions, ending `p304`'s "zero K-12 or university rules"

| jurisdiction | instrument | date | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **China** | **MoE *"AI Plus Education"* action plan** | announced `2026-04-10`, full by **2030** | 🟢 **Primary → university.** Introductory AI courses from **AY 2026-27**; **AI in teacher-qualification exams**; universities to make AI a core general-education subject |
| 🔴 **China** | the word *"mandatory"* | — | 🔴 **CONFLICT, RECORDED NOT RESOLVED.** One source says compulsory from AY 2026-27; a separate analysis states **no official source calls it mandatory**. ⚠️ **Do not brief it as a mandate** |
| 🟢 **Singapore** | **Code for Fun** — compulsory 10-hour programme from **Primary 4** | current | 🟢 **IN FORCE, and it is a GOVERNANCE PATTERN worth copying:** P1–P3 **not** required to use AI directly; P4 tools **teacher-supervised inside the Student Learning Space, not open platforms** |
| 🟡 **India** | AI + computational thinking from **Class 3** | from AY `2026-27` | 🟡 **REPORTED — single secondary source.** Do not brief as settled |
| 🟡 **South Korea** | AI talent as *"national survival strategy"* | 2026 | 🟡 **Rollout SETBACK reported: mandates without matching teacher training.** 🔵 **The most briefable APAC lesson — the constraint is teacher capacity, not policy** |
| 🔴 **Japan** | guidelines + selected pilot schools | ~2025 | 🔴 **POSSIBLY STALE** — source ≈1 year old. Flagged, not carried as current |

🔴 **REFUSED:** a claim that **Korea has AI-powered textbooks in every school** — it conflicts with other
reports in the same draw. **Not carried.**

### 🟡 Market size — unchanged and still unreconciled

🔴 **`p304`'s verdict stands verbatim and `p305` adds nothing to it: the global figure is UNRESOLVED to
within a factor of two** — $6.4 bn (2025), $11.4 bn (2026) and ≈$10.6 bn (2026) for the same market.
🔵 **Brief the RANGE, never a point estimate.** ⚠️ **No primary source — 24th consecutive pass; every
figure here is search-snippet-attributed.**

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟡 **No new NA measurement this pass; `p303`'s state layer is carried unchanged and not re-tested** — 134
bills across 31 states, CA **AB 1159** (bars student data for model training), **Oklahoma + Maryland bar AI
from high-stakes decisions about students**, `H.R. 8747` advanced in committee `2026-07-21`, NYC K-8
moratorium as the counter-signal. Canada's **"AI for All"** (`2026-06`) remains a strategy with **no
legislation date**.

🟢 **The opportunity is the convergence `p303` named, now with a third instrument behind it.** Oklahoma and
Maryland regulate **an automated decision ABOUT a student**; the EU regulates the same act via Annex III;
Vietnam lists education in its high-risk tier. 🟢 **One conformity product, three dossiers** → `P76`.
🔵 **NA is where that product can be sold first, because the state statutes are already enforceable while
the EU's Annex III work has slipped to `2027-12-02`** — the buyer has a deadline that has not moved.

### EMEA

🔴 **The highest-value finding in this file this pass, and it is a STOP, not an opportunity: emotion
inference on students has been PROHIBITED in the EU since `2025-02-02`.** ⚠️ **Any engagement-detection,
attention-tracking or affect-aware tutoring feature in an EMEA proposal is not a compliance risk to be
managed — it is a prohibited practice, with no consent route and no high-risk conformity path.**
🟢 **This is immediately actionable as a DUE-DILIGENCE product: screen an institution's existing edtech
estate for Art 5(1)(f) exposure.** 🔵 **Proctoring tools are the named example, and the Commission's
guidelines reportedly leave open whether emotion inference as a *secondary* functionality is covered — so
the screen finds real, arguable exposure rather than a clean bill.** → `P84`.

🟢 **The second EMEA opportunity is the fragmentation itself.** France has a binding framework, Spain has a
non-binding recommendation with regional implementation, the Netherlands has a lab and no rulebook, and
Germany has **no national AI schools policy at all** because the competence sits with the Länder.
🔵 **A multinational operating across these four cannot run one compliance posture** — and the nine OECD
systems still drafting guidance are the pipeline.

🟢 **Third: `Regulation (EU) 2026/1744` moved the conformity deadline to `2027-12-02` but left Article 50
transparency in place.** ⚠️ **Clients who read the Omnibus as a reprieve are wrong about disclosure** —
that gap is a short, concrete engagement.

🟢 **Permissive EMEA-placed substrate now exists on the shelf:**
[`thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem`](https://github.com/thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem) (**Apache-2.0**, 10 785 B,
`65125d9e480b`), from a German university of applied sciences.

### APAC

🟢 **China's `AI Plus Education` plan is a 2030 programme announced `2026-04-10`, reaching primary through
university — the largest single addressable education-AI programme this base has recorded.** ⚠️ **Entry is
constrained by the content and data regime, and the *"mandatory"* framing is contested; brief the programme,
not the mandate.**

🟢 **Singapore's Code for Fun is the transferable GOVERNANCE pattern of the pass: age-gating by *mode of
access* rather than by prohibition** — P1–P3 do not use AI directly, P4 uses it only teacher-supervised
inside the Student Learning Space rather than on open platforms. 🔵 **That is a walled-garden architecture,
and it is exactly what an EMEA or NA district under an AI moratorium (see NYC K-8) actually needs:
a deployable middle path between a ban and open ChatGPT access.** 🟢 **Sell the Singapore pattern into the
NA moratorium districts.**

🟡 **South Korea is the cautionary case and the clearest services opportunity: the rollout stalled on
teacher training, not on technology.** 🔵 **Teacher-capability programmes are the product, and `p304`'s
carried NA datum — 68 % of urban teachers with no AI training — says the same constraint is global.**

🟡 **India (Class 3 from AY 2026-27) is reported by a single secondary source and is a lead to verify, not
a plan to brief.** 🔴 **Japan's guidance is ~1 year old and may be stale — unmeasured, not absent.**

### LATAM

🟡 **No new LATAM measurement this pass; `p303`/`p304`'s instrumentation is carried unchanged** — UNESCO
IESALC across **200 institutions / 19 countries**: **87 %** using AI in at least one area against **26 %**
with a formal strategy (**~43-point governance gap**, the adoption hierarchy running teaching & learning
73.5 % > research 57.0 % > administration 34.1 % > community 20.0 %); Brazil **53 % / 22 %** (Cetic.br);
Argentina **75 % vs 42 %** by socio-economic class; Uruguay first CoE Framework Convention signatory;
UNESCO's **Observatory on AI in Education for LAC** launched `2026-04` at ECLAC, Santiago.

🟢 **The governance gap remains the most briefable number in this file, and `p304` established it is
STRUCTURAL rather than LATAM-specific — ~40 points wherever both sides share a denominator.** 🔵 **So the
remediation product is the same one that sells in EMEA and APAC; LATAM is where the gap is best MEASURED,
not where it is worst.**

🟡 **The code-supply finding this pass is a loss:**
[`JustinoLM/STI-matematicas`](https://github.com/JustinoLM/STI-matematicas) — sixth-grade decimal-operations
tutoring **validated with 74 students**, the only row in all 41 of the `intelligent-tutoring-system`
population with a stated classroom validation — **carries no licence.** 🔵 **Cite the validation as design
evidence and reimplement; the pedagogical result is the asset, and it is citable even though the code is
not usable.** ⚠️ **`p304`'s LATAM platform-scale gap STANDS.**

### Global

🔴 **Market size unresolved to within a factor of two; no primary source for the 24th consecutive pass.**

🟢 **The cross-regional finding of the pass is the REGULATORY ONE, and it does not respect regions:**
Art 5(1)(f) (EU, in force), Oklahoma/Maryland (US states, in force), Annex III (EU, `2027-12-02`) and
Vietnam's high-risk list all regulate **what the system DECIDES or INFERS about a student** rather than
which model it uses. 🟢 **A technique screen therefore travels where a model-governance posture does not**
→ `P84`, and it composes with `P76`'s one-product-three-dossiers conformity play.

🔵 **Standing caution for every figure above: search-snippet-attributed, mostly vendor and aggregator
sources. The regulatory instruments are the strongest material in this file; the market numbers are the
weakest.**


## 🟡 Pass 304 — THE GOVERNANCE GAP IS THE SAME SIZE IN EVERY REGION THAT MEASURES IT (~40 POINTS), AND THAT IS THE MOST BRIEFABLE NUMBER IN THIS FILE — BUT EMEA NIL'D ON EDUCATION AND THE FOUR-REGION STREAK ENDS AT ONE

⏱️ **Pass 304 of `2026-10-19`.** Evidence:
[`p304-websearch.2026-10-19-p304.tsv`](../evidence/p304-websearch.2026-10-19-p304.tsv) ·
[`p304-reachability.2026-10-19-p304.tsv`](../evidence/p304-reachability.2026-10-19-p304.tsv).

⚠️ **Provenance statement, up front and applying to every datum in this section: all of it is
SEARCH-SNIPPET-ATTRIBUTED.** Not one publisher could be read. `curl` → **`CONNECT tunnel failed, response
403`**; `WebFetch` → `ENOTFOUND`. **23rd consecutive pass without a primary source**, and per `p303`'s
standing order the channel was **not re-tested**. 🔴 **Nothing below is verified at a publisher. Market
sizes in particular come from vendor research firms and aggregator blogs, and they disagree with each other
by a factor of two.**

🔴 **Honest regression to report first: `p303` achieved the first pass in this base's history where all four
regions paid. `p304` does not repeat it.** **EMEA returned NOTHING education-specific** and APAC returned
only corporate-training material. 🔵 **Both are written down below as declared gaps, because an informed gap
is information and silence looks exactly like coverage.**

### 🟢 The finding of the pass: adoption outruns governance by ~40 points wherever both are measured

🟢 **Three regions published an adoption figure and a governance figure in the same window. The spread
between them is almost identical in each, and it is the one number in this file that survives the
provenance caveat — because it is a RATIO of two figures from the same instrument, not an absolute.**

| region | adoption | governance | 🔴 **gap** | instrument |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **LATAM** | **73.5%** of institutions have adopted AI in teaching & learning | only **30%** of universities have published an AI policy | 🔴 **~43 points** | UNESCO IESALC / UNU-IAS, 200 institutions in 19 countries |
| 🟡 **APAC** | **87%** of organisations encourage the use of AI agents | **47%** have governance, oversight and controls for them | 🔴 **40 points** | OneTrust 2026 survey (cross-sector, not education-specific) |
| 🟡 **North America** | **92%** of students use AI *(global student figure, 2025)* | **~10%** of institutions have formal AI guidelines | 🔴 **~80 points** ⚠️ | ⚠️ **mismatched denominators — students vs institutions. Do NOT brief this as 80** |

🟢 **The defensible claim: in the two regions where adoption and governance are measured on the SAME
denominator (LATAM institutions; APAC organisations), the gap is 40–43 points.** 🔵 **That it reproduces
across two independent instruments on two continents is what makes it worth a slide.**
⚠️ **The North America row is included to be refused**: 92% is a global STUDENT figure and 10% is a US
INSTITUTION figure, so the 80-point spread is an artefact of mixing denominators. 🔴 **This base has been
burned by exactly this before — a denominator is only half an instrument.**

🟢 **Why it is the commercial headline for AI Studios: the gap IS the engagement.** Every region has already
adopted; almost none has governed. **Policy scaffolding, audit trails, assessment-integrity evidence and
model-use disclosure are the work, and they are the same work in four regions.**

### 🟡 Market size — a two-fold disagreement, recorded as a range and not reconciled

| figure | 2025/26 | forecast | CAGR | source class |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AI in education, global | **$6.4B** (2025) | — | ~31% to 2034 | 🟡 vendor research |
| AI in education, global | **$11.4B** (2026) | **$57.2B** (2033) | — | 🟡 vendor research |
| AI in education, global | **~$10.6B** (2026) | — | — | 🟡 vendor research |
| North America | **$951M** (2024) | **$2.3B** (2029) | — | 🟡 MarketsandMarkets |
| North America, "AI for kids" | **$540M** (2025) | **$1.08B** (2034) | — | 🟡 vendor research |

🔴 **$6.4B and $11.4B for the same global market in adjacent years cannot both be right.**
🟢 **Brief the RANGE and the direction, never a point estimate** — and note that the North American
sub-figure ($951M on a $6.4–11.4B global base) is inconsistent with the same sources' claim that **North
America holds 36–37.5% of the market**. ⚠️ **At least one of those two is wrong; this environment cannot
determine which.**

🟢 **Segment and delivery mix, where the sources do agree:** **STEM** took **34.78%** of revenue;
**language learning** is the fastest-growing segment; **cloud** delivery held **71.22%** (2024); global
student AI use went **66% (2024) → 92% (2025)**.

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟢 **The money is institutional and it is moving in named instruments.** Q1 2026 alone: **OpenAI launched a
country-level education programme with eight national partners**; **Carnegie Mellon and the Gates Foundation
put $55M into AI courseware for gateway college courses**; and a **$169M government commitment to
responsible AI in higher education** was reported. ⚠️ **The $169M figure does NOT name the government in any
snippet read — treat as unconfirmed and do not attribute it to a specific country.**

🔴 **The regulatory shape is a vacuum with state-level patches.** There is no FDA-equivalent for education
AI; adoption is decided district by district. US rules are **fragmented by state**, with **Colorado** and
**Texas** named as examples. ⚠️ **From an opinion piece — verify against state statute before briefing.**

🟢 **Where a Globant engagement lands:** **only ~10% of institutions have formal AI guidelines and 71% of US
teachers lack AI training.** 🔵 **Those two numbers define a single sellable package — policy scaffolding
plus educator enablement — and the gateway-course money ($55M, CMU/Gates) says the buyer is the
institution's provost, not its CIO.** 🟢 **The permissive substrate for it is already on this base's shelf:
[`Ed-Fi-ODS`](https://github.com/Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/Ed-Fi-ODS) (Apache-2.0) is the US state education data
standard, and this pass measured it as the most deployed-per-watcher repository in the tier (48 forks / 28★).**

### EMEA

🔴 **DECLARED GAP — this is the second consecutive window in which the regional query returned NOTHING
education-specific, and it is written down rather than filled.** The query
`AI education EMEA 2026 adoption regulation players` returned **only generic enterprise AI material**:
Workday's EMEA adoption study, CompTIA's 2026 AI-fluency outlook, and a Bandwidth/Cavell note on regulation,
privacy and trust as adoption pressures.

🔴 **Named in the results: ZERO education ministries. ZERO edtech vendors. ZERO university programmes. ZERO
school-level AI rules.** 🔵 **This is a measurement failure, not evidence of a quiet market** — EMEA plainly
has the world's most developed AI statute. **The query phrasing is the defect:** "EMEA" is an
industry-analyst region that education ministries never use about themselves.
🟢 **Pre-registered fix for `p305`: drop "EMEA" and query NAMED JURISDICTIONS** — *EU AI Act education
high-risk Annex III*, *UK DfE generative AI schools guidance*, *France/Germany/Spain national AI-in-schools
programme*, *UAE/Saudi ministry of education AI curriculum*. **Four country queries will outperform one
regional one.**

🟡 **What can still be said, and its source quality:** the **EU AI Act** is the regulatory anchor, and one
North American commentary piece **says** education AI is classified high-risk, **reported as** taking full
effect **August 2026**. ⚠️ **That date arrives here via that commentary, not via EU primary text** —
[`intel/open-gaps.md`](../intel/open-gaps.md) records that `eur-lex` is `403` at CONNECT and
`artificialintelligenceact.eu` does not resolve. 🔵 **`p303` established a named mechanism for this date;
`p304` adds no independent confirmation and does not claim one.**
🟢 **The opportunity, stated from the statute rather than from market data: on the date **reported as**
**August 2026**, high-risk classification is **said** to bring conformity assessment, logging, human
oversight and post-market monitoring obligations onto systems European institutions are ALREADY running
ungoverned — and that date is weeks away.** 🔵 **EMEA is the one region
where the governance gap has a statutory deadline attached, which makes it the region where the gap converts
fastest.**

### APAC

🟡 **PARTIAL — and the partition is informative.** The query paid on **corporate training** and nil'd on
**formal education**, which is itself a finding about where APAC's AI-in-learning money currently sits.

🟢 **Named players, all corporate-training:** **TCS + Pearson** multi-year AI learning alliance aimed at
employer skills gaps · **LearnUpon** expanding with a Sydney HQ and AI course-authoring · **NIIT MTS** on
Training Industry's 2026 custom-content list · **Alteryx** Academy relaunched with AI learning paths.
🔴 **ZERO K-12 or university AI rules in any named country were returned.**

🟢 **Governance and regulation:** **Singapore** is consulting on AI use in financial institutions
(transparency, accountability, risk oversight) — **a template being watched across sectors, not an education
rule**. **Canberra** is tightening AI governance and copyright, and **OpenAI has appointed an ANZ policy
lead**. 🔵 **The regional read: policymakers are converging on shared principles — safety, transparency,
accountability — while implementing them per market.** 🔴 **Nothing education-specific.**

🟡 **The binding constraint is infrastructure, not policy: nearly 50% of APAC businesses cite insufficient
infrastructure for real-time data processing as a barrier to AI adoption**, and the OneTrust survey's
**87% encourage / 47% govern** split is the sharpest governance gap measured this pass.
🟢 **Where a Globant engagement lands: the corporate-training buyer is already spending, and this pass put a
permissive, APAC-placed, high-stakes-assessment row on the shelf for exactly that buyer** —
[`jeliael/fakao-ai-exam-engine`](https://github.com/jeliael/fakao-ai-exam-engine) (MIT, China bar exam),
which pairs with `kaogong-skill` (MIT, civil-service exams) to cover **national licensing examinations**
under permissive grants. 🔵 **Professional certification is the APAC entry point; ministries are not
procuring yet.**

### LATAM

🟢 **The richest regional leg of the pass, and the only one with institution-level instrumentation.**

| instrument | scope | finding |
|---|---|---|
| **Digital Education Council, LATAM survey 2026** | **30 000+ responses, 29 institutions**; co-partners **Tecnológico de Monterrey** + Institute for the Future of Education | 🟢 **92% of students and 79% of faculty already engaging with AI; 94% of faculty expect to use it in future teaching** |
| **UNESCO IESALC + UNU-IAS** | **200 institutions, 19 countries**, fielded **Aug–Oct 2025** | 🟢 **Adoption hierarchy: teaching & learning 73.5% > research 57.0% > administration 34.1% > community engagement 20.0%** |
| regional policy review | 19 countries | 🔴 **Only 30% of LATAM universities have published an AI-use policy** |

🔵 **The two adoption figures are not in conflict — 92%/79% counts PEOPLE, 73.5% counts INSTITUTIONS.**
🟢 **Read together they say the quiet part: individual use is near-universal while institutional adoption is
at three-quarters and institutional POLICY is at 30%.** 🔴 **Students are adopting faster than institutions
can govern, and the 43-point gap above is the measurement of it.**

🔴 **Regulation is heterogeneous and mostly non-binding — SEVENTH consecutive confirmation of
[`intel/open-gaps.md`](../intel/open-gaps.md)'s `gap 104` (no education-specific AI regulation anywhere in
LATAM):** **Chile** leads with a **2021 national AI policy** and a bill under discussion; **Brazil's
`PL 2.338/2023`** and **Colombia's `CONPES 4144`** are national strategies, **not sector statutes**; in
**Mexico**, recommendations from **SEP** and **ANUIES** *"lack binding character."*
🟢 **Institutional actors worth naming for an engagement:** Tecnológico de Monterrey · UNAM (a cross-faculty
AI-in-education project) · UPC · Pontificia Universidad Católica de Chile · the **IDB** (publishes AI
regulation and education reports) · **CEPAL** (publishes the **ILIA** index across 19 countries).

🟢 **A supply-side first for this region: LATAM is no longer empty on the permissive shelf.**
[`pedroestevaodev/plataforma-estudo-personalizado`](https://github.com/pedroestevaodev/plataforma-estudo-personalizado)
(MIT, payload-pinned `p304`) was built for **Alura + Google's *Imersão IA*** — refuting the standing
sub-clause *"LATAM returned nothing at any star level."* ⚠️ **It is 8★ / 40 commits and NOT a platform, so
the platform-scale gap stands.** 🔵 **The strategic point is different and better: Alura's *Imersão IA* put
**180 000–190 000** people through a free Gemini course in 2025. **That is a talent pool, not a repository
—** and for a studio staffing a LATAM engagement it is the more valuable finding.

### Global

🟢 **The cross-regional synthesis, and the one thing to lead a client conversation with: the governance gap
is structural, not local.** 40 points in APAC, 43 in LATAM, ~10% formal-guideline coverage in North America,
and a statutory deadline in EMEA. 🔵 **Four regions, one problem, and the EMEA deadline (August 2026) makes
it urgent in the market with the most mature statute.**

🟡 **Delivery and segment mix (global, vendor-sourced):** cloud **71.22%** · STEM **34.78%** of revenue ·
language learning fastest-growing · student use **66% → 92%** in one year · North America **36–37.5%** of
market share. ⚠️ **The last figure is inconsistent with the same sources' North American absolute dollar
figures; brief share or absolutes, not both.**

🔴 **Maturity, stated against the hype:** the sector is still **early** — most institutions remain in pilot
mode while competition among big tech, publishers and startups is intense. 🟢 **That is consistent with
everything above: near-universal individual adoption, three-quarters institutional adoption, 30%
institutional policy, and pilots that have not become systems.**

### 🔴 What this pass could NOT establish

🔴 **No primary source, 23rd consecutive pass** — every figure above is search-snippet-attributed. Not
re-tested, per `p303`.
🔴 **EMEA education-specific data: NIL.** Declared gap with a pre-registered query fix above.
🔴 **APAC formal-education regulation: NIL.** Corporate training only.
🔴 **The $169M "responsible AI in higher education" government commitment names no government.**
🔴 **The global market size is unresolved to within a factor of two**, and the North America share figure
contradicts the North America absolute figures.
🔴 **The EU AI Act August 2026 high-risk date still has no primary-source confirmation** from this
environment (`eur-lex` 403 at CONNECT; `artificialintelligenceact.eu` does not resolve).
🔴 **Chile's bill and Mexico's SEP/ANUIES guidance were not re-checked for status changes later in 2026.**

## 🟢 Pass 303 — THE VIETNAM INSTRUMENT IS RECONCILED RATHER THAN RE-CORRECTED, THE EU HIGH-RISK DATE FINALLY HAS A NAMED MECHANISM, AND ALL FOUR REGIONS PAID IN ONE PASS FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS BASE'S HISTORY

⏱️ **Pass 303 of `2026-10-18`.** Evidence:

### 🟢 RECONCILIATION — APAC: Vietnam's two instruments are a STATUTE and a LIST, not two readings of one thing

🔵 **`p302` "corrected" the carried Vietnam datum from *"the Law on AI, in force `2026-03-01`"* to
*`Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg`, effective `2026-08-15`*. This pass's APAC leg returned the OLD reading again** —
**`Law No. 134/2025/QH15`, effective `2026-03-01`, with extraterritorial reach.** 🟢 **The resolution is that
both are right and they are different instruments at different levels**, which is why the base kept
oscillating:

| layer | instrument | effective | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Statute | **`Law No. 134/2025/QH15`** | **`2026-03-01`** | The AI law itself; **extraterritorial** — reaches foreign technology companies participating in AI activities in Vietnam |
| Implementing list | **`Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg`** (signed `2026-06-30`) | **`2026-08-15`** | Promulgates the **list of high-risk AI systems** — 46 systems / 6 sectors, **education = 3** — issued *under* the Law |

🟢 **So `p302`'s correction was right about the EDUCATION LISTING and the carried datum was right about the
LAW.** 🔵 **The briefing-safe formulation, and the one to use from now on:** *"Vietnam's AI law has applied
since 1 March 2026 and reaches foreign providers; the high-risk list that names three education systems took
effect on 15 August 2026."* 🟢 **Neither date is wrong — they answer different questions**, and the earlier
oscillation was a category error in this base, not a conflict in the sources.

🔴 **A THIRD date now enters and is REFUSED.** This pass's leg reports a **transition deadline of
`2027-09-01`** for *health, education and finance* (against `2027-03-01` generally). ⚠️ **This base has
already refused Vietnam's compliance deadline once (`p302`, three incompatible readings) and this is a fourth
reading.** 🔴 **Do not put a Vietnamese compliance date in a client deck.** 🔵 **State the structure, which is
stable across every reading: the law applies now, the education high-risk listing applies now, and the
compliance transition for education runs into 2027 — then have the client's Vietnamese counsel fix the date.**
🟢 **The commercially important half is NOT in dispute: education is in the high-risk tier, and an
unrestricted RAG tutor is in scope on its face** (the first of the three listed education systems is content
generated for learners **from uncontrolled data sources**).

### 🟢 EMEA — the EU high-risk date has been refused three times; this pass NAMES THE MECHANISM

🔵 **`p302` recorded three incompatible readings of the EU AI Act timeline in a single pass and let the
carried `2027-12-02` stand on a coin-flip.** 🟢 **This pass supplies the instrument that explains why the date
moved, which is what was missing:**

| milestone | date | status |
|---|---|---|
| EU AI Act entry into force | **2024-08** | 🟢 Stable across all readings |
| General application | **`2026-08-02`** | 🟢 Stable across all readings |
| **`Digital Omnibus on AI`** — the amending instrument | **in force `2026-07`** | 🟢 **NEWLY NAMED — this is the thing that moved the high-risk dates** |
| High-risk obligations, **stand-alone** systems | **`2027-12-02`** | 🟢 **CARRIED DATE CORROBORATED, and now with a mechanism** |
| High-risk obligations, AI **embedded in regulated products** | **`2028-08-02`** | 🟢 **NEW — a second-tier date this base did not carry** |

🟢 **Why this upgrades the datum rather than merely repeating it:** a date with no mechanism is a rumour that
survives by repetition, and this base had been carrying `2027-12-02` as exactly that. **A named amending
instrument is checkable** — and it also explains the conflicting "August 2026" readings still circulating in
secondary sources: **those are pre-Omnibus and now stale.** 🔵 **So a client who was told "August 2026" was
told the truth as it stood before July 2026.** ⚠️ **Still snippet-attributed; `eur-lex.europa.eu` and
`digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu` are both unreachable from this environment.** 🟢 **Education's high-risk
status itself is NOT date-dependent and is stable across every reading: systems used to evaluate learning
outcomes or to steer learning processes fall in the high-risk category.**

🟢 **MENA, newly characterised — and the useful finding is a DIVIDE, not an average** (EdTech Hub brief):
**Saudi Arabia, the UAE and Qatar** have national AI-in-education strategies and large-scale initiatives,
while **Lebanon, Yemen and Libya** lack comprehensive digital education strategies, leaving fragmented,
pilot-driven adoption. 🔴 **The risk datum that matters commercially:** administrative AI platforms named in
the brief — **`Naseej`** (Saudi Arabia) and **`UM6P ChatGPT.Edu`** (Morocco) — **collect sensitive student
data in countries that largely lack robust data-protection law.** 🔵 **Read that as the opportunity it is: the
governance layer is missing and is billable, and it is the same gap LATAM shows at 87 % adoption against 26 %
strategy.** 🟢 **The UAE is ranked first worldwide in Microsoft's AI Diffusion Report** with Qatar at 38.3 %.
⚠️ **Sub-Saharan Africa coverage was thin to absent in this leg — an informed gap, recorded as one.** The WEF
framing is that early investment in AI skilling *in lockstep with digital infrastructure* is what separates
leaders, which is directional and not a datum.

### 🟢 North America — a second consecutive paying pass, and the state layer is where the activity is

🟢 **The volume datum, which is the one to lead with: a 2026 tracker counts `134` AI-in-education bills
across `31` states.** 🔵 **That is the finding — US AI-in-education regulation is a STATE phenomenon, and the
reported direction of travel is research and guardrails rather than mandated adoption.**

| instrument | jurisdiction | what it does | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **`AB 1159`** | California | 🔴 **Bars using student data to train AI models** | Named in tracker |
| **`SB 1227`** | Idaho | Requires privacy protections for school AI tools | Named in tracker |
| *(statutes named by state)* | Oklahoma, Maryland | 🔴 **Require human oversight and bar AI from high-stakes decisions about students** | Named in tracker |
| *(curriculum)* | Georgia, Mississippi | Adding AI content to computer-science credits | Named in tracker |
| **`H.R. 8747`** K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026 | US federal | Would make AI-literacy curriculum an allowable use of federal funds | 🟢 **CORROBORATED — advanced by the House Education and Workforce Committee `2026-07-21`, on largely party-line votes.** 🔴 **Still not law** |
| **One-year student-facing AI moratorium through grade 8** | New York City | 🔴 A **ban**, in the largest US district | Feedback window ran to `2026-05-08` |
| **`AI for All`** strategy | Canada (federal) | National AI-literacy commitment | 🟢 **`2026-06-04`** 🔴 **no legislative date; Canada has no federal AI statute** |

🟢 **`H.R. 8747`'s committee date corroborates `p302` exactly** (`p302` recorded the markup at `2026-07-21`
with an 18Y–15N tally; this leg independently reports the same date and "largely party-line"). 🔵 **Two
independent snippet attributions on the same date is the strongest provenance this base can currently
achieve on a US federal instrument.**
🔵 **Oklahoma's and Maryland's "no AI in high-stakes decisions about students" is the US analogue of
Vietnam's automated-assessment listing and the EU's learning-outcome-evaluation category.** 🟢 **Three
jurisdictions, independently, have converged on the same boundary: automated decisions ABOUT a student are
the regulated act.** **That convergence is the single most reusable regulatory fact in this file.**
🔴 **NYC's K-8 moratorium is the counter-signal to carry honestly:** the largest US district's answer was a
**ban**, not a governance framework. ⚠️ Canada's PIPEDA and Quebec's Law 25 already apply to AI handling
personal data; Mexico has bills but **no enforcing regulator**.
⚠️ **The "36 % North America share of the AI-in-education market" figure in this leg comes from a statistics
aggregator and is NOT adopted by this base.** Same for "60 % of US K-12 teachers used AI in 2024-25, 32 %
weekly" — plausible, aggregator-sourced, **not** citable.

🟢 **Four new LATAM datums, each with a named source:**

| datum | source as named | why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| **53 %** of Brazilian school administrators use AI; 🔴 **only 22 %** report school guidelines | **Cetic.br, 2026** | 🟢 **Reproduces the 87/26 governance gap at K-12 and in the region's largest market** — the gap is structural, not a higher-education artefact |
| 🟢 **Nine in ten 15-year-olds** have used AI chatbots for schoolwork, across **13 countries** of the region | **PISA 2025** | 🔵 **Demand-side saturation.** The adoption question is settled; the integrity and assessment-design question is not |

🟢 **Regulatory map, enriched:** **Colombia** adopted a national AI policy via **`CONPES 4144`** (February
2025) — policy, not statute. **Mexico** has a federal AI bill in the Senate since 2024, 🔴 **no enforcing
regulator**, and this pass could not establish its current status. 🔵 **Regional direction of travel: lawmakers
are converging on an EU-style taxonomy of unacceptable / high / lower risk** — which means **the conformity
work built for the EU and Vietnam ports to LATAM**, and that is the reusable commercial point.

🟢 **Institutional players, which matter in LATAM because procurement runs through them:** **UNESCO** is the
most active, having launched the **Observatory on Artificial Intelligence in Education for Latin America and
the Caribbean** (**14 April**), partners named as **CAF, CENIA (Chile), CETIC.br (Brazil), ECLAC, Tecnológico
de Monterrey, ProFuturo and Fundación Ceibal (Uruguay)**. 🔵 **That partner list is a route to market, not
just context.** The **Digital Education Council** runs a regional higher-education survey with **30 000+

### 🟢 Global — trend-level market signals, with the weak statistics explicitly refused

🟢 **Direction of travel, consistently across independent 2026 sources** (detail in
[`intel/trends.md`](../intel/trends.md)): a shift **from experimentation to governance** (1EdTech); gains
concentrated in **workflow-first** deployments — course design, teacher productivity, administrative workflow
— rather than broad personalisation promises (HolonIQ); **evidence** demanded that products improve learning
quality, persistence, well-being or job-relevant skills (HolonIQ); movement **away from generic AI tools
toward education-purpose-built platforms** (TeachBetter); **digital credentials** becoming core to
skills-based learning and hiring (1EdTech).

🔴 **Three circulating statistics are REFUSED and must not be used:**
🔴 **"$12.3B global AI education platform market by 2026" and "83 % of institutions planning AI teaching

primary source**.
🔴 **"86 % of students in schools and higher education utilise AI"** — statistics-aggregator figure with no
traceable original survey.
🔴 **"62 % test-score gain"** — vendor claim, no study behind it.
🔵 **Named this explicitly because these three are exactly the numbers a deck tends to pick up**, and this
base has refused them.

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟢 **Lead with the convergent regulatory boundary, because it is now three-jurisdiction and defensible.**
Oklahoma and Maryland bar AI from **high-stakes decisions about students** and require human oversight; the
EU's high-risk category covers **evaluating learning outcomes**; Vietnam's list names **automated assessment,
grading and ranking**. 🔵 **The sellable workstream is a human-oversight and evidence layer around any
system that scores or ranks a learner** — not a new model.
🟢 **`134 bills / 31 states` makes a multi-state compliance matrix a product, not a slide.** A district or
edtech vendor operating across states has no single rule to follow; **California `AB 1159`'s bar on using
student data to train models is the sharpest constraint** and shapes architecture (no training on client
student data, provable).
🟢 **`H.R. 8747`, if enacted, makes AI-literacy curriculum an allowable use of federal funds** — a funding
line to build toward. 🔴 **It is not law; do not present it as one.** Permissive assets that fit it today:
[`mytechnotalent/RE-GPT`](https://github.com/mytechnotalent/RE-GPT) (Apache-2.0) and
[`alicetinkaya76/microgpt-academy`](https://github.com/alicetinkaya76/microgpt-academy) (MIT).
🔴 **Carry NYC's K-8 moratorium as a real risk to any K-12 pitch**: the largest district's answer was a ban.
🔵 **Brief the "assistive, teacher-in-the-loop, no student-facing autonomy below grade 9" posture** — it is
the one a moratorium district can still buy.
🟢 **Canada's `AI for All` (2026-06-04) is an AI-literacy commitment without a statute** — advisory and
curriculum work, with PIPEDA and Quebec Law 25 as the actual compliance surface.

### EMEA

🟢 **The EU date structure is now briefable with a mechanism**: general application `2026-08-02`; **stand-alone
high-risk `2027-12-02`**; **embedded-in-regulated-products `2028-08-02`**; the mover is the **`Digital Omnibus
on AI`**, in force July 2026. 🔵 **Lead with the two-tier split** — most education AI is a stand-alone system,
so `2027-12-02` is the date that binds, and a client told "August 2026" was reading pre-Omnibus guidance.
⚠️ **Snippet-attributed; have the client's counsel confirm before it drives a contract.**
🟢 **Education's high-risk status is NOT date-dependent** — evaluating learning outcomes or steering learning
processes is in scope under every reading. **Conformity-evidence work can be sold today** against a deadline
that is at least a year out, which is the comfortable version of this conversation.
🟢 **The Gulf is the state-led buyer:** Saudi Arabia, the UAE and Qatar have national strategies and
large-scale initiatives; the **UAE ranks first worldwide in Microsoft's AI Diffusion Report**. 🔵 **Sell
delivery capacity and governance, not evangelism** — the strategy already exists and is funded.
🔴 **The MENA data-protection gap is the sharpest EMEA opportunity in this file:** `Naseej` (Saudi Arabia) and
`UM6P ChatGPT.Edu` (Morocco) **collect sensitive student data in jurisdictions largely lacking robust
data-protection law.** 🔵 **A student-data governance and minimisation engagement needs no new regulation to
justify it** — and the architecture ports from the EU work.
🟢 **Lebanon, Yemen and Libya have no comprehensive digital education strategy** — strategy and
capacity-building work, typically donor- or IGO-funded, on a different procurement path from the Gulf.
🟢 **Permissive EMEA-relevant assets:** [`thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem`](https://github.com/thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem)
(Apache-2.0, **institutional holder**, Helm/Kubernetes) and `microgpt-academy` (MIT, Turkish + English).
⚠️ [`aimclub/ai-competency-model`](https://github.com/aimclub/ai-competency-model) is **BSD-3-Clause** with an
institutional holder, but the holder is a **Russian state university — run a counterparty and sanctions check

🔴 **Sub-Saharan Africa: this leg returned nothing specific to education. An informed gap, not coverage.**

### APAC

🟢 **Vietnam is the most actionable jurisdiction in this file, because the obligation is live and specified.**
The law applies (`2026-03-01`, **extraterritorial — it reaches a foreign supplier**), and the high-risk list
naming **three education systems** applies (`2026-08-15`). 🔵 **The three systems are the scoping tool:**
(1) content generated for learners **from uncontrolled data sources** — 🔴 **an unrestricted RAG tutor is in
scope on its face**; (2) systems that **assess, grade or rank**; (3) **biometric or automated behavioural
monitoring**. 🟢 **Sell a source-control story and a conformity dossier.** 🔴 **Do not put a compliance DATE in
the deck — four incompatible readings now.**
🟢 **Korea: the `AI Basic Act` has applied since `2026-01-22`**, covering high-impact AI and generative-AI
labelling. 🔵 **Labelling is a concrete, cheap, demonstrable deliverable** — a good first engagement.
🟢 **The regulatory topology is the regional insight: there is no single APAC model.** Korea has a general AI
law; China regulates by service type; Japan promotes without penalties; Singapore, India and Australia rely
on voluntary guidance plus existing data and sector rules. 🔵 **So a regional rollout needs a
per-jurisdiction conformity matrix — that matrix is the billable artefact.** India's **DPDP** obligations
phase in through **May 2027**.
🟢 **Public opinion is a genuine market signal here:** the **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026** finds *lower*
support for banning AI in schools across the Asian markets examined — 🔴 **with Australia and New Zealand the
exception, showing higher support for a ban.** 🔵 **ANZ needs the same cautious, teacher-in-the-loop posture
as NYC; the rest of APAC does not.**
🟢 **Named specialist players to position against:** **Riiid** (South Korea), **Squirrel AI** (China),
**Alef Education** (UAE), **Byju's**, plus Google, Microsoft, IBM and Pearson.
🟢 **Permissive APAC-relevant assets:** [`tong666-bit/liu-xiaoyan-skill`](https://github.com/tong666-bit/liu-xiaoyan-skill)
(MIT, CET-4/6 and postgraduate English) and `feifei-companion` (Apache-2.0, **curriculum-aligned** 统编版/人教版,
carried from `p302`). 🔵 **Curriculum alignment is the APAC differentiator** — almost nothing on this shelf has it.

## 🟢 Pass 302 — THE CARRIED VIETNAM DATUM NAMES THE WRONG INSTRUMENT AND THE WRONG DATE, AND IT IS CORRECTED HERE; NORTH AMERICA BROKE A TWO-PASS REGIONAL DROUGHT WITH THREE NEW DATUMS; EMEA AND LATAM NIL'D AGAIN

⏱️ **Pass 302 of `2026-10-18`.** Evidence:
[`p302-websearch.2026-10-18-p302.tsv`](../evidence/p302-websearch.2026-10-18-p302.tsv) ·
[`p302-reachability.2026-10-18-p302.tsv`](../evidence/p302-reachability.2026-10-18-p302.tsv).

🟢 **All four mandated regional legs ran. They produced three new datums and one correction, ending the
two-pass zero recorded at `p300` and `p301`** — and the correction matters more than the new datums,
because this base has been briefing the wrong instrument for several passes.

### 🔴 CORRECTION — APAC: the instrument that puts education in Vietnam's high-risk tier is a DECISION, not the Law, and it took effect in AUGUST not MARCH

| cell | carried (through `p301`) | read `p302` | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| instrument | "Vietnam's **Law on AI**" | 🔴 **`Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg`** — the Prime Minister's decision *promulgating the list of high-risk AI systems*, issued under the Law | 🔴 **CORRECTED** |
| in force | **`2026-03-01`** | 🔴 **Effective `2026-08-15`**; the decision was **signed `2026-06-30`** | 🔴 **CORRECTED** |
| education systems named | **two** (automated assessment, behavioural monitoring) | 🔴 **THREE** | 🔴 **CORRECTED — one was missing** |
| scope | education named as high-risk | 🟢 **46 systems across 6 sectors**, of which **education = 3** | 🟢 **ENRICHED** |

🔴 **The carried date `2026-03-01` appears to be the AI Law's own commencement, which this base attached to
the education listing.** The education listing is not in the Law; it is in the **Decision**, and the
Decision is the thing a client has to comply with. 🔵 **Any deck that says "in force since March" is wrong
by five months in the client's favour — which is the dangerous direction, because it overstates how much
runway has already elapsed.**

🟢 **The three education-sector high-risk systems, as listed:**
1. **Systems that generate self-study content for learners from UNCONTROLLED DATA SOURCES.** 🔵 **This is the
   one this base was missing, and it is the broadest of the three** — an unrestricted RAG tutor over
   scraped material is in scope on its face. Every retrieval-grounded tutoring pitch now needs a
   source-control story in Vietnam.
2. **Systems that automatically assess, grade or rank students.**
3. **Systems that monitor or analyse learner behaviour using biometric data or other automated
   monitoring** — facial recognition and eye tracking given as examples.

🟢 **Sector split of the 46:** transportation **31**, ethnic and religious affairs **7**, education **3**,
healthcare **2**, banking **2**, judicial proceedings **1**.

⚠️ **Two things are NOT asserted, because this pass's sources CONFLICT on them and neither could be read at
the publisher:**
- 🔴 **The compliance deadline.** One source puts already-operating systems at **`2027-03-01`** with
  **`2027-09-01`** for health, education and finance; another gives **`2027-09-01`** for health, education
  and finance; a third describes a **six-month extension** for education systems operating before
  **`2026-08-15`**. **These are not reconcilable from secondary text. Brief the range, not a date.**
- 🔴 **The human-review qualifier.** One source states the systems are high-risk **only where the AI output
  is the sole basis for a decision without meaningful human review**. 🔵 **If that qualifier holds it is the
  single most important fact in this section** — it converts the obligation into a *design* requirement
  (keep a human in the loop and the classification may not attach) — **but it is from one secondary source
  and must be verified against the Appendix before it is ever put in front of a client.**

⚠️ **All of the above is SEARCH-SNIPPET-ATTRIBUTED.** `www.vietnam-briefing.com` and
`www.allenandgledhill.com` both returned `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`, and the official Vietnamese text was not
reachable. **Brief it with that attribution.**

### 🟢 North America — three new datums, the first new NA material in three passes

1. 🟢 **`H.R. 8747`, the K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026 — a FEDERAL funding instrument, which
   is new in kind for this file.** Introduced by **Rep. Randy Fine (R-FL)** on **`2026-05-12`**; **full
   committee markup `2026-07-21`** in House Education and Workforce; **Ordered to be Reported (Amended) by
   `18Y-15N`**; a **Ranking Member Scott amendment failed `15Y-18N`**. Substance: it amends the
   **Elementary and Secondary Education Act of 1965** to add **AI knowledge and skills for educators and
   administrators** to the allowable uses of funds, for both student instruction and teacher/leader funding
   streams. 🔴 **No floor vote was found — it is a reported committee bill, NOT law.**
   🔵 **Why it matters commercially:** every NA datum this file has carried is *restrictive* — bans,
   moratoria, red tiers. This is the first **permissive-and-funded** one. If it passes, ESEA money becomes
   spendable on AI professional development, and **teacher enablement becomes a budgeted line rather than a
   pilot**. The near-party-line committee vote is the risk to state alongside it.
2. 🟢 **Canada — a national "AI for All" strategy launched `2026-06-04`, with an AI-literacy component.**
   🔵 **The first Canadian datum on this file, which until now has been entirely US state-level.** ⚠️ It is a
   **strategy, not a statute**; no legislative date was found, and Canada has no comprehensive AI law.
3. 🟡 **Nevada `AB 406` (2025) prohibits public schools from using AI-powered tools to perform certain
   functions.** ⚠️ **The scope is NOT established** — the source text was truncated and the statute was not
   reachable. **Recorded as a pointer for `p303`, not as a briefable restriction.**

🔵 **The regional posture is now explicitly two-sided and should be briefed that way:** US **states**
restrict classroom use and student-data training (134 bills across 31 states; **California AB 1159**;
**Idaho SB 1227**; Oklahoma and Maryland barring AI from high-stakes student decisions; **NYC's red tier**
barring AI from grading, discipline and IEP development), while the **US federal** layer and **Canada** are
moving to *fund capability*. **An engagement that sells teacher enablement and governance tooling is aligned
with both halves; one that sells student-facing automated decisions is fighting the state layer.**

### 🔴 EMEA — a THIRTEENTH nil on the mandated phrasing, and the EU dates are refused again

🔴 **Zero new datums.** Everything returned was carried: European AI-in-education at **`USD 2.64 bn`
(2026) → `8.0 bn` (2030)** at **31.9 %** CAGR; Finland, Estonia and the Netherlands leading K-12
integration; the **OECD 2026 Digital Education Outlook** and its recommendation to move from
general-purpose tools to **purpose-built educational AI with durable learning gains**; only **10 %** of
450+ institutions surveyed having formal AI guidelines.

🔴 **The EU AI Act timeline was returned in three mutually inconsistent forms this pass** — an "AI Omnibus
package entering into force `2026-07-27`", an AI Act entry into force of `2026-07-31`, and "high-risk
provisions entering into force August 2026". 🟢 **This base's carried position — that education's high-risk
obligations bind `2027-12-02` — is NOT revised on secondary text that contradicts itself**, and
`digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu` was unreachable from this session. ⚠️ **`p296` already caught this deadline
moving sixteen months once. The carried `2027-12-02` stands, and the honest pitch remains the 2027 date and
roughly fourteen months of runway, not a manufactured August urgency.**

### 🔴 LATAM — zero new, and the flagship triple is STILL unread at its publisher

🔴 **Zero new datums.** The Digital Education Council 2026 LATAM survey (**92 %** of students and **79 %**
of faculty engaging with AI, **30 000+** responses across **29** institutions; **88 %** of faculty at
"minimal to moderate" engagement; assessment uses lowest; **50 %** of students supporting AI-assisted
feedback against **19 %** of faculty using it that way) and UNESCO IESALC (**200** institutions, **19**
countries) both returned exactly as carried.

⚠️ **`www.iesalc.unesco.org` returned `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND` again — the 87/74/26 triple has now been
AGGREGATOR-ATTRIBUTED since `p282` and has never once been read at the publisher.** 🔵 **It remains this
file's most-used LATAM number and its least-verified one. Brief it with the attribution, every time.**

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟢 **The opening has flipped from defensive to fundable, and `H.R. 8747` is why.** The state layer
restricts: 134 AI-in-education bills across 31 states; **California AB 1159** barring student data from
model training; **Idaho SB 1227**; Oklahoma and Maryland requiring human oversight and barring AI from
high-stakes student decisions; Maryland / Idaho / Oklahoma / Virginia mandating district policy; **Ohio**'s
`2026-07-01` district deadline; North Carolina staged to **2028**; **NYC's traffic-light guidance** whose
**red tier bars AI from grading, discipline and IEP development**; Katy ISD's K-6 ban. 🟢 **The federal
layer funds: `H.R. 8747` (reported `18Y-15N`, `2026-07-21`) would make AI knowledge and skills for
educators an ESEA allowable use** — and **Canada's "AI for All"** (`2026-06-04`) carries an AI-literacy
push.

🔵 **So the compliant, fundable build is specific:** teacher-and-administrator enablement plus governance
tooling, with student-facing automated decisions deliberately out of scope. 🟢 **The component that makes it
demonstrable is unchanged:**
[`chiptoe-svg/canvas-lms-mcp`](https://github.com/chiptoe-svg/canvas-lms-mcp) (**MIT**, payload-pinned
`p301`) ships **165 tools on a stated 117-read / 48-write split** plus a **FERPA mode** — deploy the 117
read tools, leave the 48 write tools unregistered, and the agent is *architecturally* incapable of the three
things NYC's red tier prohibits. 🟢 **New this pass:** [`ProjectAJ14/eklavya`](https://github.com/ProjectAJ14/eklavya)
(**MIT**) is the enablement-side artefact — it quizzes a learner on concepts behind code an agent wrote,
which is the measurable form of "AI literacy" the federal bill would fund. Governance documentation layer:
[`microsoft/agent-governance-toolkit`](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-governance-toolkit) (**MIT**).

### EMEA

🔵 **The opening is a timing argument and it must be stated with the correct date.** Education's high-risk
obligations under the EU AI Act bind **`2027-12-02`** on this base's carried reading — **not** August 2026,
and this pass again refused three conflicting secondary timelines rather than revise it. 🟢 **That is roughly
fourteen months of runway: enough to build conformity-ready assessment AI before the deadline instead of
retrofitting it**, which is a far stronger pitch than manufactured urgency.

🟢 **Market frame:** **`USD 2.64 bn` (2026) → `8.0 bn` (2030)**, **31.9 %** CAGR; Finland, Estonia and the
Netherlands leading K-12 integration; the UK's **£4 m** DfE investment in lesson-planning and marking tools;
Germany's **€20 bn** 2025-2030 AI commitment (general, **not** education-specific); only **10 %** of 450+
institutions with formal AI guidelines. 🔵 **That last figure is the engagement**: the obligation arrives in
2027 and nine in ten institutions have no policy to attach it to.

🔴 **Kazakhstan is filed here** under this base's enterprise-geography convention: a **presidential decree of
`2026-05-12`**, **43 curriculum objectives**, standards due **`2026-09-01`**, **all secondary schools by
2029**, with personal-data protection written into the plan. ⚠️ **PRESS-ATTRIBUTED** — `primeminister.kz` has
never been reachable from this environment.

🟢 **New permissive component for a conformity engagement:**
[`thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem`](https://github.com/thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem) (**Apache-2.0**, payload-pinned
`p302`, **held by Technische Hochschule Mittelhessen**) — an EU-institution-held automatic feedback service
with Helm/Kubernetes deployment, which is a materially easier provenance story for an EU public buyer than a
personal repository.

### APAC

🟢 **The region's instruments remain the most education-specific anywhere, and this pass sharpened the
headline one.** **Vietnam's `Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg`** (signed `2026-06-30`, **effective `2026-08-15`**)
lists **46 high-risk AI systems across six sectors**, of which **three are education**: self-study content
generation **from uncontrolled data sources**, automatic **assessment / grading / ranking**, and
**biometric or automated behavioural monitoring**. **Korea's AI Basic Act** is in force **`2026-01-22`**,
with MSIT treating 2026 as a pilot period under limited enforcement and a one-year penalty grace. Taiwan's
AI Basic Act (Dec 2025) and Japan's AI Basic Plan impose few direct duties; **Singapore**'s frameworks are
voluntary; **Australia**'s National AI Plan leans on existing statute; **China** has the most binding
regime (registration, content labelling, penalties to **CNY 50 m**). **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026: `39 %`
globally would ban AI in schools**, Australia and New Zealand higher than the Asian markets surveyed.

🔵 **The opening, restated precisely because the dates changed:** Vietnam's obligations attached in
**August 2026** and bite on already-running systems in **2027** (range, not a date — see the correction
above). 🟢 **An engagement that produces EVIDENCE about an automated-assessment system — not assurances —
arrives exactly on time**, and this pass finally has the full evidence stack to offer:
[`UKGovernmentBEIS/inspect_ai`](https://github.com/UKGovernmentBEIS/inspect_ai) (**MIT**) as the harness,
plus [`bydeng01/conv-vs-ped-tutor`](https://github.com/bydeng01/conv-vs-ped-tutor) and
[`bydeng01/ability-levels-audit`](https://github.com/bydeng01/ability-levels-audit) (both **MIT**, pinned
`p302`) as the worked **design** — pre-registration, one frozen base model, policy-only variation, a frozen
answer-leakage matcher and per-component ablations.

🟢 **Second opening, new this pass:** Vietnam's **first** listed system — self-study content from
*uncontrolled* sources — makes **source control a compliance feature**, and
[`JuneYaooo/lineage-skill`](https://github.com/JuneYaooo/lineage-skill) (**Apache-2.0**, carried) exists
precisely to keep generated teaching material tied to its sources. 🔵 **That is a pre-built answer to a
named legal requirement.** 🟢 **Curriculum-aligned delivery** now also has a permissive reference in
[`SimonsTang/feifei-companion`](https://github.com/SimonsTang/feifei-companion) (**Apache-2.0**).
⚠️ **`p292`'s displacement trap stays armed and is not re-triggered:** much of the APAC education corpus is
**corporate L&D**, not schooling.

### LATAM

🔴 **Zero new datums; the adoption-versus-governance pair is carried and remains this region's defining
fact.** UNESCO IESALC across **200 institutions in 19 countries**: **87 %** use AI somewhere, **74 %** in
teaching and learning, and only **26 %** have a formal AI strategy — **84 %** of private non-profit
universities against **68 %** public and **52 %** private for-profit. OECD **TALIS 2024**: Brazil **56 %**,
Chile **55 %**, Colombia **53 %**, Costa Rica **52 %** of secondary teachers had used AI, against an OECD
average of **36 %**. Uruguay's **Ceibal**: **75 %** of public-school teachers. UNESCO's LAC AI-in-education
**Observatory** launched **`2026-04-14`**. Digital Education Council 2026 LATAM survey: **30 000+** responses
across **29** institutions, **92 %** of students and **79 %** of faculty engaging, **88 %** of faculty at
only minimal-to-moderate depth, and assessment uses the **lowest**-adopted of all.

🟢 **The opening is unchanged and remains the clearest in this file: the gap between `87 %` doing it and
`26 %` governing it is the engagement.** 🔵 Nothing in that pitch needs a new model — it needs policy,
evaluation and audit, and the instruments are permissive and payload-pinned
([`microsoft/agent-governance-toolkit`](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-governance-toolkit),
[`UKGovernmentBEIS/inspect_ai`](https://github.com/UKGovernmentBEIS/inspect_ai), and now the two `bydeng01`
audit designs). 🟢 **The faculty-depth figure sharpens the offer**: with **88 %** of faculty shallow and
assessment the least-adopted use, the sellable first phase is **faculty capability plus assessment
governance**, in that order.

🔴 **A refusal to carry forward:** [`aaryansamanta/ai-ethos`](https://github.com/aaryansamanta/ai-ethos)
(499★) is **multilingual AI tutoring for underserved learners** — on its face the ideal LATAM component —
and it has **no licence whatsoever** (payload-checked `p302`). **Do not put it in a proposal.**

⚠️ **The 87/74/26 triple remains AGGREGATOR-ATTRIBUTED** — `www.iesalc.unesco.org` is unreachable from this
environment and the figures have **never** been read at the source, now since `p282`. **Brief them with that
attribution.**

### Global

🟡 **The 2026 market is between `USD 6.4 bn` and `USD 11.4 bn` depending on which of four firms is asked —
a `1.8×` spread**, which is why this file still refuses to publish a single figure. North America holds the
largest share (**36 %**); APAC grows fastest (**35.3 %** CAGR). Teacher use roughly doubled in a year to
about **60 %**; **86 %** of students across 16 countries and a reported **92 %** of university students use
AI in their studies; **86 %** of education organisations use generative AI and **most have no policy**.

🔴 **Institutions lag behaviour everywhere** — professional development, student training and district
policy all trail usage, which is the same shape as LATAM's 87/26 gap measured globally. 🟢 **And the
convergent regulatory question across all four regions is unchanged and sharper after this pass: is AI
allowed to DECIDE about a student?** Vietnam names assessment and ranking; Oklahoma and Maryland bar
high-stakes decisions; NYC's red tier bars grading, discipline and IEP development; the EU puts assessment
in its high-risk annex. 🔵 **Every one of those lines falls on the same side of the same boundary, so a
read-only-first architecture is the globally portable answer** — which is exactly what
`chiptoe-svg/canvas-lms-mcp`'s 117-read / 48-write split and
[`YeetingWaterbottle/gradescope-mcp`](https://github.com/YeetingWaterbottle/gradescope-mcp)'s read-only-by-design
posture demonstrate.

🔴 **New global licence caution, and it is a briefing line:** the second-most-starred education tutoring
artefact on GitHub, [`JushBJJ/Mr.-Ranedeer-AI-Tutor`](https://github.com/JushBJJ/Mr.-Ranedeer-AI-Tutor)
(**29.6k★, 3.3k forks**), has **no licence at all** and is marked **DISCONTINUED**; the best-maintained
visual tutor, [`HugeCatLab/ChatTutor`](https://github.com/HugeCatLab/ChatTutor) (**1.3k★**), is **AGPL-3.0**.
🟢 **Across the star-ordered head of this vertical, `p302` refused 9 of 23 repositories on licence grounds —
about 39 %.** 🔵 **Whenever a client cites a star count, that rate is the answer.**

🔴 **Three date sets are REFUSED and must not be asserted:** Vietnam's compliance deadline (three

🔴 **`make hooks` was NOT run** — `gate-dates`, `gate-absence` and `inbound-claims` did not run against this

phrasings are now at nine runs with a heavy carried-datum rate. **Keep the NA leg** (it paid this pass).

## 🟢 Pass 301 — THE FOUR MANDATED REGIONAL LEGS PRODUCED ZERO NEW FOR A SECOND CONSECUTIVE PASS WHILE ONE DIRECTED LEG PRODUCED THREE; EMEA ENDED A TWELVE-PASS NIL STREAK; AND THE TRENDS LEG'S ONE NEW DATUM IS A DISPERSION, NOT AN ESTIMATE

⏱️ **Twenty-third pass of `2026-10-18`.** Evidence:
[`p301-websearch.2026-10-18-p301.tsv`](../evidence/p301-websearch.2026-10-18-p301.tsv) ·
[`p301-instrument-substitution.2026-10-18-p301.tsv`](../evidence/p301-instrument-substitution.2026-10-18-p301.tsv).

**Ten legs: four mandated, four regional, two directed.** Two returned nils; both are declared below
with their queries.

### 🟢 Every datum was checked against the COMMITTED file before being called new — and the result replicates `p300` exactly

| leg | new / returned |
|---|---|
| **North America** *(mandated)* | 🔴 **0 / 13** |
| **EMEA** *(mandated)* | 🔴 **0 / 8** — 🟢 but **non-nil**, ending a **twelve-pass nil streak** |
| **APAC** *(mandated)* | 🔴 **0 / 8** |
| **LATAM** *(mandated)* | 🔴 **0 / 9** |
| **trends** *(mandated)* | 🟡 **1 / 9** |
