🟢 **`p300` measured this same shape and `p301` reproduces it on four different mandated legs:** the
**mandated regional phrasing has now produced ZERO new datums across two consecutive passes and eight
leg-runs**, while the **directed unworked-region slot has paid in both** — three datums at `p300`
(Lusophone Africa), three at `p301` (Central Asia). 🔵 **Sample stated: 2 passes, 8 mandated regional
runs, 0 new; 2 directed runs, 6 new.** That is enough to act on and not enough to call a law about all
regions — `p295`'s correction stands.

### 🟢 The three new datums, in full — Kazakhstan's decree, read closer than this base had it

🔴 **This base already carried Kazakhstan's `1 September 2026` school date** (added `p297`). 🟢 **The
directed leg refined it into an instrument with a signatory, a span and a curriculum:**

| datum | value | status |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **the signatory and the signing date** | the decree was signed by **President Tokayev** on **`2026-05-12`** | 🟢 **NEW** — `Tokayev` appeared nowhere on this base |
| 🟢 **the renamed subjects** | grades **1–4** → *"Digital Literacy and **Artificial Intelligence**"*; grades **5–11** → *"Computer Science and **Artificial Intelligence**"* | 🟢 **NEW** |
| 🟢 **the measurable target** | the updated curriculum carries **43 AI-related learning objectives** | 🟢 **NEW** |
| 🟡 the span | AI across **all secondary schools by 2029** | 🟡 refines the carried single date into a **2026–2029 programme** |
| 🟡 the sequence | 2026–2029 national plan due **`2026-07-01`** · pilot schools' high-speed internet by **`2026-08-01`** · AI-in-secondary **standards** due **`2026-09-01`** | 🟡 🔴 **and it corrects how this base read its own carried date**: `1 Sept 2026` is the deadline for **STANDARDS**, not for the rollout |
| 🟡 the pilot | from **grade 4**, in **Kazakh language, mathematics and digital literacy** | 🟡 refines the carried *"pilot in 10 rural schools"* |
| 🟢 the groundwork | **2025**: national standards for AI use in education covering **ethics, legal regulation, personal-data protection and academic integrity** | 🟢 context for the 2026 decree |

🔵 **Why the `43 objectives` datum is the most useful thing on this page for a studio.** Every other
national AI-in-education instrument this base holds is a *policy*: an intention, a date, a budget.
🟢 **Kazakhstan has published a COUNTABLE curriculum target.** 43 objectives is a specification — it
can be mapped to items, items to a QTI bank, and the bank to a coverage report. **That turns a
ministry engagement from advocacy into delivery against an acceptance criterion**, and the permissive
QTI tier on [`verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) is what delivers it.

🔴 **UZBEKISTAN: NIL, declared.** The leg named both countries and returned **no Uzbek instrument at
all** — no policy, no date, no curriculum. 🔵 This base already records that *"neither Kazakhstan nor
Uzbekistan has a national OPEN-SOURCE AI education strategy"*; `p301` narrows the Uzbek half further:
**not merely no open-source strategy — no retrievable national AI-education instrument.** An informed
gap, not silence.

### 🟡 The trends leg's one new datum is a SPREAD, which is worth more than another point estimate

🟡 **Four research firms priced the 2026 AI-in-education market between `USD 6.4 bn` and
`USD 11.4 bn`.** 🟢 **NEW** — this base carries several point estimates (Precedence **`9.58 bn`**
→ `136.79 bn` by 2035; an alternative **`11.4 bn`** 2026 → `57.2 bn` 2033 at **25.9 %** CAGR) and had
never recorded their **dispersion**.

🔵 **The spread is a `1.8×` ratio between the lowest and highest 2026 figure for the same market in
the same year.** 🟢 **That is the briefable fact.** A deck quoting one number implies a precision the
sector does not have; a deck quoting the range and naming the 2035 forecasts' divergence
(~`32 bn` by 2030 in one source against `136.79 bn` by 2035 in another) is defensible. 🔴 **Prefer the
adoption figures to the market figures** — `60 %` of US K-12 teachers used AI in 2024-25 and `32 %`
weekly, LATAM's `87 %`/`74 %`/`26 %` triple, Uruguay's `75 %` — **those are counts of behaviour, not
forecasts of revenue.**

### 🔴 A refuted date arrived through the EMEA leg and was stopped before it reached a shelf

🔴 **The EMEA corpus asserted that the EU AI Act's high-risk obligations for education apply from
`August 2026`.** 🟢 **This base has already refuted that date and the correction is restated here
rather than silently dropped:** Annex III point 3 (education) applies **`2027-12-02`**; what falls on
**`2026-08-02`** is **Article 50 transparency**. 🔵 **The claim was written down in the evidence TSV and
written into NO shelf cell.** `p295` recorded the first time a standing gate caught *incoming*
research; `p301` caught the same class **with the gate chain unrunnable**, by reading the corpus
against what this base already knows.

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🔴 **Zero new datums; the regulatory map is carried and stable.** 134 AI-in-education bills across 31
states; **California AB 1159** barring student data from model training; **Idaho SB 1227**; Oklahoma
and Maryland requiring human oversight and barring AI from high-stakes student decisions;
Maryland / Idaho / Oklahoma / Virginia mandating district-level policy; **Ohio**'s `2026-07-01`
district deadline; North Carolina staged to **2028**; Utah folding AI into CS standards; NYC's
traffic-light guidance (March 2026) whose **red tier bars AI from grading, discipline and IEP
development**; Katy ISD's K-6 ban; Columbia Law's 2026-27 policy.

🟢 **The opening this pass actually adds is a component, not a datum.**
[`chiptoe-svg/canvas-lms-mcp`](https://github.com/chiptoe-svg/canvas-lms-mcp) (**MIT**, payload-pinned
`p301`) ships **165 tools with a 117-read / 48-write split stated** and a **FERPA mode**. 🔵 **Match
that to NYC's red tier and the shape of a compliant build is explicit:** deploy the 117 read tools,
leave the 48 write tools unregistered, and the agent is architecturally incapable of the three things
the guidance prohibits. 🟢 **A read/write boundary that can be shown to a district counsel is worth
more than a policy memo** — and [`microsoft/agent-governance-toolkit`](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-governance-toolkit)
(**MIT**, pinned `p301`) is the permissive layer that documents it.

### EMEA

🟢 **Non-nil for the first time in twelve passes — all eight datums already carried.** European
AI-in-education at **`USD 2.64 bn` (2026) → `8.0 bn` (2030)**, CAGR **31.9 %**; Finland, Estonia and
the Netherlands leading K-12 integration; the UK's **£4 m** DfE investment in lesson-planning and
marking tools; UNESCO's generative-AI guidance; the **OECD 2026 Digital Education Outlook** and its
recommendation to move from general-purpose tools to **purpose-built educational AI with durable
learning gains**; Germany's **€20 bn** 2025-2030 AI commitment (general, not education).

🔴 **Kazakhstan is filed here**, following this base's enterprise-geography convention — and it is
where EMEA's one actionable new instrument sits (above): a **presidential decree of `2026-05-12`**,
**43 curriculum objectives**, **standards due `2026-09-01`**, **all secondary schools by 2029**, with
**personal-data protection** written into the plan.

🔵 **The EU opening is a timing argument, stated correctly.** Education's high-risk obligations bind
**`2027-12-02`**, not August 2026. 🟢 **That is roughly fourteen months of runway** — enough to build
conformity-ready assessment AI *before* the deadline rather than retrofitting it, and the honest
version of the pitch is the 2027 date, not an urgency that does not exist.

### APAC

🔴 **Zero new datums, and the region's instruments are the most education-specific anywhere.**
🟢 **Vietnam's Law on AI, in force `2026-03-01`, lists education — specifically automated assessment
and behavioural monitoring — as HIGH-RISK.** Korea's **AI Basic Act** in force **`2026-01-22`**, with
MSIT treating 2026 as a pilot period under limited enforcement. Taiwan's AI Basic Act and Japan's AI
Basic Plan impose few direct duties; Singapore's agentic-AI framework is voluntary; Australia's
National AI Plan leans on existing statute. China has the most binding regime (Labelling Measures,
anthropomorphic-service rules, 2025 and July 2026). **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026: `39 %` globally
would ban AI in schools**, with Australia and New Zealand higher than the Asian markets surveyed.

🔵 **The opening: Vietnam and Korea have both named the thing and given a grace period.** 🟢 An
engagement that can produce **evidence** for an automated-assessment system — not assurances —
arrives at the right moment, and [`UKGovernmentBEIS/inspect_ai`](https://github.com/UKGovernmentBEIS/inspect_ai)
(**MIT**, pinned `p301`, holder **UK AI Security Institute**) is the permissive evaluation framework
that produces it. ⚠️ **`p292`'s displacement trap stays armed and is not re-triggered here**: much of
the APAC education corpus is **corporate L&D**, not schooling.

### LATAM

🔴 **Zero new datums; the adoption-versus-governance pair is carried and is still this region's
defining fact.** UNESCO IESALC across **200 institutions in 19 countries**: **87 %** use AI somewhere,
**74 %** in teaching and learning, and only **26 %** have a formal AI strategy — **84 %** of private
non-profit universities against **68 %** public and **52 %** private for-profit. OECD **TALIS 2024**:
Brazil **56 %**, Chile **55 %**, Colombia **53 %**, Costa Rica **52 %** of secondary teachers had used
AI, against an OECD average of **36 %**. Uruguay's **Ceibal**: **75 %** of public-school teachers.
UNESCO's LAC AI-in-education **Observatory** launched **`2026-04-14`**; the Digital Education Council's
2026 LATAM survey covers **30 000+ responses across 29 institutions**; **ILIA**'s third edition reports
school-level AI education rising but still exceptional.

🟢 **The opening is unchanged and remains the clearest in this file: the gap between `87 %` doing it
and `26 %` governing it is the engagement.** 🔵 Nothing in that pitch needs a new model — it needs
policy, evaluation and audit, and both instruments are now payload-pinned and permissive
(`microsoft/agent-governance-toolkit`, `UKGovernmentBEIS/inspect_ai`). ⚠️ **The 87/74/26 triple remains
AGGREGATOR-ATTRIBUTED** — `www.iesalc.unesco.org` is unreachable at this session's resolver, so the
figures have never been read at the source. **Brief them with that attribution.**

### Global

🟡 **The 2026 market is between `USD 6.4 bn` and `USD 11.4 bn` depending on which of four firms is
asked — a `1.8×` spread.** North America holds the largest share (**36 %**); APAC grows fastest
(**35.3 %** CAGR). Teacher use roughly doubled in a year to about **60 %**; **86 %** of students across
16 countries and a reported **92 %** of university students use AI in their studies. 🔴 **Institutions
lag behaviour everywhere** — professional development, student training and district policy all trail
usage, which is the same shape as LATAM's 87/26 gap measured globally.

### 🔴 What this pass could NOT do

🔴 **No primary government or IGO source, TWENTIETH pass.** Both primary channels were attempted this
pass and both failed at the **resolver**: `primeminister.kz` (Republic of Kazakhstan — the Kazakh
decree's own publisher) and `www.iesalc.unesco.org` (the LATAM study's own publisher) returned
`getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`, while `github.com` and `raw.githubusercontent.com` answer normally. 🟢 **So
Kazakhstan's decree and LATAM's 87/74/26 triple are PRESS- and AGGREGATOR-ATTRIBUTED, and must be
briefed that way.**
🔴 **The DNS control sweep was REFUSED by the sandbox as exfil scouting — reproducing `p295` exactly —
and was NOT worked around.** Without it, *absent* still cannot be separated from *blocked*; every
unreachable host stays `EXISTENCE-UNMEASURED`.
🔴 **`make hooks` was DENIED** (*"Code from External"*), so `gate-dates`, `gate-absence` and
`inbound-claims` did not run against this file. The refuted August-2026 date was caught by reading,
not by the gate.
🔴 **The mandated regional phrasing was run a second time after `p300` recommended retiring it**, and
produced zero again. The recommendation is re-registered for `p302` with the sample now at eight runs.


## 🟢 Pass 300 — `p299`'S LOCALE-SYNDICATION LAW REPLICATED EXACTLY ON A SECOND RUN, LATAM SUPPLIED THE ADOPTION-VERSUS-GOVERNANCE GAP AS A MEASURED PAIR, AND THE UNWORKED REGION PRODUCED A LEARNING-CRISIS FIGURE THAT CUTS AGAINST THIS BASE'S OWN PITCH

⏱️ **Twenty-second pass of `2026-10-18`.** Evidence:
[`p300-websearch.2026-10-18-p300.tsv`](../evidence/p300-websearch.2026-10-18-p300.tsv).

**Ten legs: four mandated, four regional, two directed.** Four returned nils, each declared below
with its query.

### 🔴 The pass's most important measurement is about this base's own legs: 17 of 21 datums were ALREADY CARRIED, and the four mandated REGIONAL legs produced ZERO new between them

🟢 **Every datum every leg returned was checked against the COMMITTED state of this file**
(`git show HEAD:intel/market.md`) before being called new — mechanically, not by eye:

| leg | new / returned |
|---|---|

| **EMEA** *(mandated)* | 🔴 **0 / 0** — nil, the twelfth |
| **APAC** *(mandated)* | 🔴 **0 / 3** |
| **LATAM** *(mandated)* | 🔴 **0 / 7** |
| **trends** *(mandated)* | 🟡 **1 / 6** |
| 🇦🇴🇲🇿 **Lusophone Africa** *(DIRECTED, unworked region)* | 🟢 **3 / 7** |

**The four new datums, in full:** the **SACMEQ** Grade 6 Portuguese collapse (**62.7 % → 16 %**);
Mozambique's **Higher Education Strategic Plan 2026-2035** with Chapo's call on AI and data-science
research; Mozambique's **National Commission for AI**, reported `2026-05-10` *(aggregator-attributed)*;
and the **AI-tutor segment's 2024 base of USD 1.63 B** — whose 2030 endpoint (USD 7.99 B) this file

🔵 **So the four mandated regional legs produced NOTHING NEW between them, and one directed leg at an
unworked region produced three.** Sixth confirmation of a pattern `p294` first recorded, and the first
time it has been **measured against the committed file** rather than judged by reading.

🔴 **TWO corrections this measurement forced on this pass's own first draft, both recorded because the
error shapes are the instructive part:**

**(a)** The draft claimed *"five new datums"* for North America. **Zero were new** — the USD 169 M
refusal, the CMU/Gates USD 55 M, OpenAI's eight national partners, the 10 % guidelines figure, the
71 % teacher figure and Colorado/Texas were **all already on this file.** The claim had been written
from what the leg **returned** rather than from what this base **did not already hold**. 🔵 That is
`p299`'s locale finding one level up: **a result is not a finding, and a non-nil leg is not a
productive one.**

**(b)** 🔴 **The carried-check itself was wrong on its first run, and that is the sharper lesson.** It
reported LATAM's governance figure as **NEW** because it matched the exact string `30% of
universities`, while this file holds the same datum as **`~30%`**. 🔵 **An exact-string
carried-check is a LOWER BOUND on what is carried — never a measurement of what is new.** The loose
re-check produced the 17/4 above; the first run's 16/5 is recorded here as **wrong**, because a pass
that quietly replaces its own numbers teaches the next pass nothing.

### 🟢 `p299`'s locale-syndication law, replicated — the first time a law of this base's has been tested on a second independent run

`p299` measured the EMEA leg's nine slots, found **five were one CompTIA article at five locale
subpaths** (`en-za`, `en-eu`, `en-au`, `en`, `en-em`) plus **a Workday study originally from 2023**,
and generalised: *a query whose terms are REGIONAL rather than NATIONAL retrieves locale-replicated
syndication, and a search engine counts those as distinct results.*

🟢 **`p300` re-ran the identical query and got the identical corpus** — the same five CompTIA locale
subpaths, the same 2023-origin Workday study, the same total absence of schools, universities, edtech
vendors or national education policy. 🔵 **A law that predicts the next observation is worth more
than one that explains the last.** This one did, and it now carries the recommendation at the end of
this section rather than being filed as a complaint about a nil.

🔴 **EMEA's mandated phrasing: the TWELFTH consecutive nil for education**, declared with its query.

that appear ZERO times anywhere in this file before today.** The directed-beats-mandated pattern
`p287`-`p298` measured holds for a thirteenth pass, and this time the two new countries are **Poland

### 🔴 EMEA's mandated phrasing: an ELEVENTH nil, and the corpus artefact is now named

`AI education EMEA 2026 adoption regulation players` returned **nine slots**, of which 🔴 **five are
ONE CompTIA article served at five locale subpaths** — `en-za`, `en-eu`, `en-au`, `en`, `en-em` — and
one more is a **Workday study originally published in 2023**. 🔵 **This is the same artefact
`p299` measured on the ERP/CRM leg in [`verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) within the
same hour: six of nine slots there were one vendor's glossary page in six languages.**

🟢 **So the two dead channels have the same mechanism, and it is now worth stating as a law rather than
a complaint:** a query whose terms are *regional* rather than *national* retrieves **locale-replicated
syndication** — the same document, once per market — and a search engine counts those as distinct
results. 🔴 **Nine slots can be two documents.** Measuring slot-count as coverage is how an eleven-pass
nil went unexplained for nine of them.

`p296`-`p298` refused an unsourced *"USD 169 million committed to responsible AI in higher education,

and this is its fourth confirmation.** The figure is still not published on this file. 🔵 **A number
that survives four independent retrievals without ever acquiring a primary source is evidence about
the corpus, not about the money.**

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟢 **Material, and the governance gap is the sellable finding.** The leg returned, from secondary
roundups: 🔴 **only ~10% of institutions have formal AI guidelines**, and 🔴 **~71% of US teachers lack
AI training.** Regulation is still **state-piecemeal** — **Colorado** and **Texas** named as
introducing requirements — against no federal instrument. Named money: **Carnegie Mellon + the Gates
Foundation, USD 55M into AI courseware for gateway college courses**; **OpenAI launched a
country-level education programme with eight national partners in Q1 2026.**

🔵 **The opportunity is the inverse of the adoption story.** Where 10% of institutions have a policy
and 71% of teachers are untrained, the binding constraint is **not** a tutoring model — it is
**defensible process**. 🟢 **Lead with the governance artefact, as `p298` concluded for Morocco:** an
AI-use policy, an audit trail, and a human-decision boundary, built on the integrity substrate this
base now shelves — [`baoguangsheng/fast-detect-gpt`](https://github.com/baoguangsheng/fast-detect-gpt)
(MIT, ICLR 2024) with its limits stated, and
[`adlnet/lrs-conformance-test-suite`](https://github.com/adlnet/lrs-conformance-test-suite) (MIT) to
make the event trail provable rather than asserted. 🔴 **Do not price the USD 169M figure into a
pitch** — see above.

| schools in scope | **~8 000 primary + ~4 000 secondary ≈ 12 000**, against a national stock of ~14 000 primary and ~7 000 secondary |

| baseline *(IBE PIB, 2025)* | 🔴 **1% of schools had an AI lab; 27% had no Wi-Fi** |
| named principals | Education Minister **Barbara Nowacka**; PM **Donald Tusk** on "responsible AI use" |
| 🔴 what is NOT established | whether the labs were actually delivered before the **September 2026** school year — the corpus stops at the announcement |

🔵 **The opportunity, and it is a procurement shape rather than a product shape.** A state buying
**hardware for 12 000 schools off a 1%-penetration baseline** has bought rooms, not capability — and
the same corpus notes that **guidance on teacher training and data-privacy rules was still missing in
2025/26**. 🟢 **The gap between "lab installed" and "lab used" is the engagement**: teacher-facing
curriculum automation on
[`DaRL-GenAI/instructional_agents`](https://github.com/DaRL-GenAI/instructional_agents) (🟢 **MIT** —
🔴 **CORRECTED `p308` from `Apache-2.0`**; payload `LICENSE` **1 067 B** `ae280df5a726`, and
[`intel/trends.md`](trends.md) line 2815 already said *"`instructional_agents` is MIT, not Apache-2.0"*),
reached through the **LTI boundary** rather than by touching the ministry's stack, and an AI-use policy
artefact attached from day one. 🔴 **Verify delivery status against the Ministry of Education before
quoting the 12 000 figure to a client** — this base has recorded the announcement, not the rollout.

does in 15 languages against a national curriculum. 🔴 **The counterparty question `p298` raised
applies here too: the AI money sits with the IT ministry, the curriculum sits with education.** Qualify
which one holds the mandate before scoping.

institutional layer is half-adopted, which is exactly where this base's new substrate rows

policy, and the integration layer into the 66% not yet using AI administratively** — two distinct

## 🟢 Pass 298 — THE FOUR MANDATED LEGS PRODUCED NOT ONE NEW DATUM, TWO DIRECTED LEGS PRODUCED THREE NEW COUNTRIES, AND ONE OF THEM IS ALREADY RUNNING THE CAPABILITY THIS BASE HAS PERMISSIVE CODE FOR

⏱️ **Twentieth pass of `2026-10-18`.** Evidence:
[`p298-promotion-gap.2026-10-18-p298.tsv`](../evidence/p298-promotion-gap.2026-10-18-p298.tsv) ·
[`p298-channel-control.2026-10-18-p298.tsv`](../evidence/p298-channel-control.2026-10-18-p298.tsv).

| region | leg | result |
|---|---|---|
| **North America** | mandated | 🔴 **held, and NOTHING new.** Every datum already carried — and the figure `p293` downgraded was **re-served from the same unsourced blog** |
| **EMEA** | mandated | 🔴 **TENTH consecutive nil** on education-specific material. Enterprise AI only |
| **APAC** | mandated | 🔴 **nil on education for the third pass running.** Every education-adjacent hit was **corporate learning** again |
| **LATAM** | mandated | 🟡 **corroboration, not extension.** Strongest leg for the ninth time and **every datum already on this file** |
| **EMEA** | directed: 🇲🇦🇹🇳🇩🇿 **Francophone Maghreb** | 🟢 **NEW REGION — Morocco yields a national programme, a budgeted school target and an operating dropout-detection pilot.** 🔴 Tunisia and Algeria: declared nil |
| **EMEA** | directed: 🇲🇿🇦🇴 **Lusophone Africa** | 🟢 **NEW REGION — two countries, neither previously in this base, and the regulator is NOT the education ministry** |

🟢 **`p295`'s rule holds for a FIFTH consecutive pass and should now be read as the battery's design
principle, not a finding: yield tracks how UNWORKED a region is.** Two directed legs produced **three
countries this base had zero prior mentions of** (Morocco, Mozambique, Angola — `grep` count **0** in
`intel/` and `compose/` before this pass) plus **two honest nils** (Tunisia, Algeria). The four
mandated legs, run every pass for ten passes, produced **zero new datums.** 🔵 **The mandated
phrasings are now controls, not research.**

### 🔴 The methodological finding of the pass, and it is the negative twin of `p297`'s

`p297` introduced **cross-leg corroboration**: a global claim should appear in the legs of the
countries it names. `p298` supplies its counterpart, because the North American leg re-served the
**`USD 169M` "government commitment to responsible AI in higher education"** figure — the one
`p293` **DOWNGRADED** because *its source reports the figure without naming the government*, and
`p297` refused again.

🔴 **The same single blog returned it a third time, and a third appearance is not a third source.**
🟢 **The rule, stated so no later pass mistakes repetition for confirmation: RE-CIRCULATION IS NOT
CORROBORATION.** A figure that reappears because the same leg reaches the same page has gained
nothing. Corroboration requires an **independent** leg — ideally one aimed at the jurisdiction the
claim names, which is exactly what would resolve this one: **a leg naming the government.** 🔴 **The
refusal stands. Do not quote `USD 169M` to a client.**

### 🟢 Francophone Maghreb — a new region, and Morocco is the find

🔴 **Zero prior mentions of Morocco anywhere in this base.** The leg
(`intelligence artificielle éducation Maroc Tunisie Algérie 2026 ministère stratégie nationale`)
returned a dense national picture, and it is almost entirely Moroccan.

| datum | detail | grade |
|---|---|---|
| national strategy | AI named a cornerstone of **"Maroc Digital 2030"**; Ministry of Digital Transition announces tailored training tracks | 🟡 `ATTRIBUTED` — press, not primary text |
| institutional build-out | **Jazzari** regional technology institutes, to supply AI skills across **all 12 regions** | 🟡 `ATTRIBUTED` |
| 🟢 **school equipment target** | Ministry of Education: **12 000+ establishments** to receive digital solutions **by 2027** | 🟢 **budgeted and dated — briefing-grade** |
| 🟢 **programme scale** | *"Caravane pour tous"*: **74 880 beneficiaries (2023) → 625 000+ by 2027** | 🟢 **an 8.3× scale-up with both endpoints named** |
| 🟢 **an OPERATING pilot** | an **AI school-support system piloted in the Draâ-Tafilalet academy to identify students at risk of dropping out**, with positive results opening a path to **national extension** | 🟢 **the strongest single datum of the pass — see below** |
| 🔴 governance | a **parliamentary report** finds governance unclear and demands a **legal framework**; a parliamentary group has formally questioned the minister | 🔴 **the gap is on the record domestically, not inferred by this base** |
| 🔴 **Tunisia and Algeria** | 🔴 **DECLARED NIL.** The only document reached is a **UNESCO Maghreb ecosystem mapping from 2020** stating the Maghreb countries *"ne disposent pas jusqu'à aujourd'hui de stratégies nationales d'intelligence artificielle"* — **six years stale and explicitly not a 2026 reading** | 🔴 published with its query. Needs **Arabic-language** or ministry-site legs |

🟢 **THE BRIEFING-GRADE FINDING: Morocco is operating, at academy scale and with a stated national
extension path, the exact capability this base already holds permissive code for.** Dropout
early-warning is **two shelved MIT rows** —
[`cedanl/student-signal`](https://github.com/cedanl/student-signal) (precision/recall at multiple
invitation thresholds, Eegdeman invitation-rule methodology) and
[`datakind/student-success-tool`](https://github.com/datakind/student-success-tool) (**3 015
commits**, a named deployment outcome). 🔵 **This is the first time a named national pilot in a region
this base had NO rows for lines up one-to-one with a row this base already verified.** 🔴 **And the
parliamentary governance complaint is the other half of the engagement**: the instrument exists, the
legal framework is being demanded, and `inspect_ai` + `pedagogy-benchmark` (`P56`) is the evidence
artefact that answers it. See **`P61`** in [`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md).

connection* is an **offline-first** requirement, and the offline row has been on

- **EMEA** — `AI education EMEA 2026 adoption regulation players`: 🔴 **tenth consecutive nil.**
  Returned CompTIA on EMEA IT strategy 2026, a **2023** Workday study, a **2024** year-in-review, and
  a Bandwidth-Cavell communications report. 🔵 **Every EMEA finding this base holds arrived through a
  jurisdiction- or language-named leg — never this phrasing.** `p298` makes ten. It was run because
  the battery mandates it; the honest gain is a ten-point measurement of a dead channel, and the two
  directed legs run in the same hour produced three countries.
- **APAC** — `AI education APAC 2026 adoption regulation players`: 🔴 **nil on education for the
  third pass running** (`p292`, `p297`, `p298`). Every education-adjacent hit was **corporate
  learning**: a TCS–Pearson multi-year learning alliance, LearnUpon's Sydney HQ and *Create+* AI
  authoring, an Alteryx Academy relaunch. 🟡 Governance datums are real but **not education**:
  **OneTrust 2026 — 87% of organisations encourage AI-agent use while only 47% have governance,
  oversight and controls**; **Forrester** expects sovereignty to shape infrastructure choices for
  **~half** of APAC firms; **49%** cite insufficient real-time data infrastructure; OpenAI appointed
  an **Australia/New Zealand policy lead** as Canberra tightens AI governance and copyright;
  Singapore's consultations are **financial-sector**. 🔵 The **87% / 47%** split is the APAC analogue
  of the North American governance gap and is the one carryable line here.
- **LATAM** — `AI education LATAM 2026 adoption regulation players`: 🟡 **strongest leg for the ninth
  time, and extension is now nil.** It re-served the **UNESCO IESALC + UNU-IAS** working paper
  (**200 HEIs, 19 countries, fieldwork `2025-08`→`2025-10`**) already at `### LATAM` on this file; the
  **Ibero-American comparative study** of higher-education AI regulation across **Argentina, Brazil,
  Chile, Peru and Spain**; the **ILIA / CEPAL** regulation subdimension; and the **`$18.2M (2024) →
  $36M (2029)`** figure **this file already flags as subregional** — *Rest of Latin America*, i.e.
  excluding the two largest markets, which is why it must never be set beside this base's LATAM
  band. 🔵 **A ninth consecutive "strongest leg" that adds nothing is the signal to stop running it
  and start naming countries**, as `p298` did for the Maghreb and Lusophone Africa.

|---|---|---|
| **North America** | mandated | 🟡 **held.** Spread still too wide for a single figure; governance gap widened; **$55 M** CMU + Gates into gateway courses. 🔴 **One public-money figure REFUSED for want of a source** |
| **EMEA** | mandated | 🔴 **NINTH consecutive nil** on education-specific material — enterprise AI only |
| **EMEA** | directed: **🇳🇬 Hausa** | 🟢 **BROKEN IN THE SAME HOUR — five instruments, four new.** N-ATLAS, ATLAS Umoja AI, aprendIA, InkubaLM, a teacher-education framework |

| **LATAM** | mandated | 🟢 **strongest again (8th time), with the governance gap now DOUBLE-INSTRUMENTED** |

🟢 **`p295`'s prediction has now held FOUR times and should be promoted from a finding to a standing
rule: yield tracks how UNWORKED a region is, not its size, its wealth or its language.** The two
directed legs this pass (**Hausa**, **Central Asia**) produced **nine new datums** between them; the
two mandated regional legs that have been run every pass for nine passes produced **one** (a cross-leg
corroboration, below). 🔵 **The mandated EMEA and APAC phrasings should be run once as controls and
the budget spent on an unworked region** — unspent still: **Amharic** (native-language, against
`p295`'s English-language Ethiopia leg), **Lusophone Africa**, and now **the Caucasus** and
**Francophone Maghreb**.

🟢 **The pass's cheapest check, and it is new to this base: CROSS-LEG CORROBORATION.** North America's
leg reported *"OpenAI launched a country-level education programme with eight national partners in Q1
2026."* The Central Asia leg, run independently in the same pass, named **Kazakhstan in that inaugural
cohort with a seat count (165 000 ChatGPT Edu licences)**. **Two legs, neither aware of the other,
agreeing on one programme.** 🔵 A global claim should appear in the legs of the countries it names —
and when it does not, that absence is now a measurable signal rather than a silence.

🔴 **THE BRIEFING-GRADE FINDING OF THE PASS: one `region` value now contains two opposite national
postures, 2026, both state-driven.**

| | 🇳🇬 **Nigeria** | 🇰🇿 **Kazakhstan** |
|---|---|---|
| **what the state did** | **published an open multilingual model** (N-ATLAS) and, via **GSMA + 5 governments**, is scaling it as a **template** across the continent | **bought 165 000 proprietary ChatGPT Edu seats** and mandated AI in schools **by presidential decree** from `1 Sept 2026` |
| **the studio's opening** | build **on state-published weights**; the deployed teacher tool (`aprendIA`, 5 800 teachers) is **closed — that is the gap** | the **governance, evaluation and integration layer** around a platform the state **already owns** |
| **what it needs from these shelves** | language substrate + WhatsApp delivery ([`rumi-platform`](https://github.com/Orenda-Project/rumi-platform)) | 🆕 [`agent-governance-toolkit`](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-governance-toolkit) · 🆕 [`inspect_ai`](https://github.com/UKGovernmentBEIS/inspect_ai) |

🔵 **Both engagements are `EMEA` and neither brief transfers to the other.** This is the clearest case
this base has produced for why `region` alone cannot scope an engagement — **the field places the
opportunity; the national posture decides what you sell.** The nine substrate rows promoted onto
[`repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md) this pass are what the Kazakh column needs, and they
existed in this repository, unshelved, the whole time.

### 🔴 The market-size spread is still live, and still wide enough to disqualify a single figure

🔴 **$6.4 B (2025) · $8.3 B (2025) · $10.6 B (2026) · $11.4 B (2026)** — four vendor estimates, all
current this pass, differing by **78 %** at the top. 🔵 `p294`'s rule stands and is re-confirmed:
**a vendor market size quoted without its spread is a guess with a decimal point.**

🔴 **A new defect, recorded against a named property.** `marketsandmarkets`' *"Rest of Latin
America"* page is **internally inconsistent**: its prose reads **$18.2 M (2024) → $36 M (2029)**
while a snapshot on the same property reads **26.32 M**. Not a disagreement between two houses — one
house disagreeing with itself. 🔵 **Do not quote it in either direction.**

## 🟢 Pass 293 — ALL FOUR REGIONAL LEGS RAN, AND APAC'S FOUR-PASS "NO EDUCATION POLICY" VERDICT WAS REFUTED BY AN INDONESIAN LEG IN THE SAME HOUR

🔵 **The methodological result first, because it changes how every region row on this file should
be read.** Since `p287` this base has carried an inference: *a region-named English leg measures
English-language press coverage of a region, not the region*. Every proof was **sequential** — an
English leg failed in one pass, a language-named leg succeeded in the next. 🟢 `p293` ran the

|---|---|
| `AI education APAC 2026 adoption regulation players` | 🟡 **corporate L&D, 4 of 4**, and the leg's own summary says it found no education-specific APAC regulation |

🔴 **So `p292`'s APAC conclusion is withdrawn.** It read *"3 of 3 are corporate L&D"* as the
supply-side displacement *"corroborated from the demand side"*. The displacement was in the
**query**, and `p286`'s APAC "explicit nil" has the same explanation. 🟢 The inference is now a
controlled measurement.

### 🟡 North America — held, and one figure this base published is DOWNGRADED

| market, NA | ~**USD 951M** (2024) → ~**USD 2 303.2M** (2029), CAGR **15.9%** (MarketsandMarkets) |
| "AI for kids", NA | **USD 482M** (2024) → **USD 1 082M** (2034) — 🔴 a *different scope*; not comparable with the row above |
| adoption | NA leads at **36%** share; **10%** of institutions have formal AI guidelines; **71%** of US teachers lack AI training |

| 🔴 **"USD 169M government commitment to responsible AI in higher ed"** | 🔴 **DOWNGRADED. `p292` published this; its source reports the figure WITHOUT NAMING THE GOVERNMENT.** Not to be quoted to a client until a named instrument is found |
| regulation | 🔴 **relative regulatory vacuum** — no FDA-equivalent for edtech; Colorado and Texas piecemeal. 🟡 `ATTRIBUTED`; no primary regulatory text reached this pass |

### 🔴 EMEA — a SEVENTH consecutive nil on the mandated phrasing

🔴 Zero education results, again. The corpus is enterprise AI: CompTIA on EMEA IT strategy 2026

standing battery mandates the per-region leg, and the honest gain is a seven-point measurement of

### 🟢 APAC — the education-policy layer EXISTS, at regional scale, and it is new to this file

| element | state, as reported |
|---|---|
| the meeting | **14th ASEAN Education Ministers Meeting (ASED)**, **Singapore**, **`2026-07-22`** |
| the instrument | 🟢 **ASEAN Work Plan on Education 2026-2030**, launched at it |

education policy"* since `p286`. 🟢 It should read: **a five-year regional work plan naming AI
readiness and assessment, with a national compulsory-subject commitment gated on teacher and
infrastructure readiness.** That gate is procurable work — readiness assessment, teacher
training, curriculum and assessment redesign — and it is named in the instrument itself.

|---|---|

| **ILIA / Latin American AI Index** (CEPAL and partners) | a **regulation subdimension** inside governance; the 2023 chapter records experts calling for frameworks to *"protect people's rights, minimize algorithmic biases and ensure transparency and accountability"*, with explicit concern about **developer control held in the Global North** |
| a 2025 comparative study (`repositorio.uflo.edu.ar`) | AI-in-higher-education rules compared across **Argentina, Brazil, Chile, Peru and Spain** |
| 🔴 **MarketsandMarkets, "Rest of Latin America"** | **USD 18.2M** (2024) → **USD 36M** (2029) |

🟢 **Carry that last row precisely because it is embarrassing.** This file also carries **92% of
students** and **79% of faculty** actively engaging with AI in LATAM. **Near-universal adoption and
USD 18M of regional spend are both true**, and a studio should quote **the gap**, not one side of
it: the demand is real, the budget line is not there yet, and the engagement that sells into that
shape is small, fast and self-funding — not a platform programme.

🔴 **No code, no licence, no repository in any of the four regional legs**, for the fifth pass
running. 🔵 The regions generate **instruments**; the code arrives through directed legs.

### 🟡 Brazil — the Referencial is SHARPER, and a dead deadline was caught before publication

🟢 New to this file: the MEC *Referencial para Desenvolvimento e Uso Responsáveis de Inteligência
Artificial na Educação* is **241 pages**, and it shipped alongside a **regulatory sandbox built
with the AGU** (Advocacia-Geral da União). 🔵 Civil society — **Campanha Nacional pelo Direito à
Educação** — filed contributions arguing the text needed firmer normative grounding, which
corroborates this file's existing note that the Referencial is **self-declared non-normative**.

🔴 **The date correction, caught by a verification leg before anything was published.** The first
Brazilian leg returned a public consultation *"aberta na plataforma Brasil Participativo até 29 de
outubro"* **with no year attached**. A second leg places it in **October 2025** — the consultation
that *preceded* the March 2026 Referencial. 🔴 Published as live, this file would have handed a
studio a consultation deadline **eleven days out that closed a year ago**. 🔵 `p223`'s date gate
exists for exactly this shape, and the lesson is the cheap one: a deadline with no year is not a
deadline.

🔴 **Nil on code:** no Brazilian result — Referencial, sandbox, Projeto ConsciêncIA (a UFMS
extension project with SBC/EduComp and WEI papers), the *IA na prática docente* course on
Plataforma Mais Professores, or Paraná's public technical course in AI — states a repository, a
public code location or a licence. `gov.br/mec` is unreachable from here.

## 🟢 Pass 292 — ALL FOUR REGIONAL LEGS RAN, EMEA'S MANDATED PHRASING IS A SIX-POINT DEAD CHANNEL, AND THE ARABIC LEG BROKE IT ON THE FIRST ATTEMPT AGAIN

⏱️ **Fourteenth pass of `2026-10-18`.** Evidence:

indistinguishable from coverage.

🟢 **The pass's structural result, stated before the regions.** The mandated **region-named
English** legs produced **zero code addresses** for the fourth pass running, while a
**national-language directed** leg produced a named sovereign model, a deployed school AI
teacher and a permissive startup model family — on its first attempt, for the **third
consecutive pass** (🇻🇳 `p290`, 🇲🇽 `p291`, 🇪🇬 `p292`). 🔴 A region-named English leg measures
**English-language press coverage of a region**, which is not the same population as that
region's education sector.

### 🟡 North America — held, with the heaviest public money in the set and the widest governance gap

🟢 **Carried forward and re-confirmed this pass:** NA leads regional adoption at **~36 %**
share; **~$951 M (2024) → ~$2.3 B (2029)**, CAGR **15.9 %** (single publisher, secondary — a
direction, not a forecast). A separate *"AI for kids"* scope at **$482 M (2024), 12.5 % CAGR** is
🔴 **not comparable** — different scope, stated rather than blended.

🟢 **The Q1 2026 money, which is the region's distinguishing feature:** a **$169 M** government
commitment to responsible AI in higher education; an **OpenAI country-level education programme
with eight national partners**; and **$55 M** from Carnegie Mellon and the Gates Foundation into
AI courseware for **gateway college courses**. 🔴 The second remains the strategically dangerous
one for a studio: a **vendor contracting at national scale** closes a market to open-source
alternatives before an integrator is invited in.

🔴 **The governance gap is the widest on this shelf:** **~10 %** of institutions hold formal AI
guidelines and **71 %** of US teachers lack AI training. 🔴 **No sector regulator** — no
FDA-equivalent for edtech, adoption decided school-by-school, with **Colorado and Texas** adding
piecemeal state requirements. 🟡 ATTRIBUTED; no primary regulatory text was reached this pass.

🔵 **The engagement shape, unchanged and now better funded:** in NA you sell assurance to a
**buyer**, because there is no external regulator to point at. The client's own policy *is* the
compliance surface — which is precisely why
[`compose/code/p292-decision-evidence-store`](../compose/code/p292-decision-evidence-store/README.md)
is sellable here without a statute forcing it.

### 🟢 EMEA — a SIXTH consecutive nil on the mandated phrasing, and the Arabic leg answered immediately

🔴 **The nil, recorded exactly.** `AI education EMEA 2026 adoption regulation players` returned
enterprise-AI generalities only: CompTIA on EMEA IT strategy (AI fluency must reach
legal/compliance and frontline staff), Workday (EMEA finance teams ahead on adoption; the EU AI
Act as backdrop), a Bandwidth-Cavell report (`2026-02`) naming compliance complexity and data
governance as the barriers. **No schools, no universities, no edtech vendors, no national

re-running it; it was re-run because the standing battery mandates it, and the result is now a
**six-point** measurement instead of a five-point one.

🟢 **One Arabic directed leg broke it on the first attempt — the third national-language proof.**
🇪🇬 **Egypt's MCIT** launched, early 2026, a national LLM `كرنك` (**Karnak**) and a
secondary-school AI teacher `سيا` (**Sia**). Officials place Karnak **inside public digital
infrastructure** explicitly so startups can build applications on top; one secondary outlet
reports it open-sourced at **41 B parameters**, while MCIT's own pages do not mention weights.
Separately `مدرستك في مصر` (*Madrasatak fi Masr*), an MoE + MoFA platform with interactive
classes aligned to the Egyptian curriculum, framed by the ministry as **complementary to**
classroom teaching. 🔴 No licence is stated for any of it.

🟢 **And the leg surfaced the first permissive EMEA-origin model family this base has seen:**
**TokenAI** (Cairo) — `Horus 1.0-4B` reported **MIT**, `Horus Taleeq 0.2B Base` reported **MIT**
and **released with its training code and data sources** (Arabic-only, ~6 B Arabic tokens),
`Horus Lens 1.0` reported **Apache-2.0**. 🔴 **All press-reported, none read**: four verification
channels closed or inconclusive this pass. Posture **`CANDIDATE-ATTRIBUTED`** — detail on
[`agents/trending.md`](../agents/trending.md).

🔵 **The EMEA opportunity this reframes.** This shelf's EMEA story has been *"the AI Act makes
your system high-risk"*. 🟢 Egypt adds a second, non-EU EMEA shape: a **state that publishes a
sovereign model as public infrastructure and invites building on top**, with an
**Arabic-language** education surface already deployed. 🔴 The blocker is a licence nobody has
read — which is a scoping question with a known answer-procedure, not an unknown.

### 🟡 APAC — thin on education, and every education-adjacent hit was CORPORATE learning

🔴 **No national education policy, no ministry programme, no school or university adoption rate**
in the mandated leg — fourth pass that this leg has failed to reach an education institution.

🟢 **What it did return is a finding in its own right: three of three education-adjacent hits
are L&D.** TCS + Pearson's multi-year AI learning alliance aimed at **employer** skills gaps;
LearnUpon expanding with a Sydney HQ and *"Create+"* AI course authoring; Alteryx relaunching
its Academy with AI learning paths and credentials. 🔵 This is `T-p292b`: the same vocational
displacement `p291` measured on the **supply** side, arriving from the **demand** side
unprompted.

🟡 **Governance and infrastructure, carried as context:** one survey finds **87 %** of
organisations encourage AI agents while only **47 %** have governance, oversight and controls
for them; Forrester expects **sovereignty** to shape infrastructure choices for roughly **half**
of APAC firms; **49 %** of APAC businesses cite insufficient real-time data infrastructure as an
adoption barrier. Regulation moves **by market, not regionally** — Singapore consulting on AI in
financial institutions, OpenAI naming an ANZ policy lead as Canberra tightens AI governance and
copyright rules. 🔵 Consistent with `P30`: there is no APAC requirement to build against, only a
jurisdiction list.

### 🟢 LATAM — the strongest leg again, and the adoption-governance gap is now DOUBLE-quantified

🟢 **Two independent instruments this pass.** Digital Education Council, *AI in Higher Education
LATAM Survey 2026*: **>30 000 responses across 29 LATAM institutions**, **92 %** of students and
**79 %** of faculty actively engaging with AI (one framing: 92 % regularly, **67 %** at least
weekly). UNESCO **IESALC**, **200 HEIs across 19 countries**: teaching and learning **73.5 %**,
research **57.0 %**, administration **34.1 %**, community engagement **20.0 %**.

🔴 **Against that, only ~30 %** of LATAM universities have published AI-use policies (Tec de
Monterrey). 🔵 The gap is now measured by **two** instruments on the demand side and one on the
governance side, which is the firmest version of this shelf's longest-running LATAM finding.

🔴 **Regulation remains fragmented and sector-blind:** Chile has had a national AI policy since
2021 with a bill under discussion; Brazil and Colombia hold national strategies but **no
education-sector rules**; **Mexico has no binding instrument** — SEP, ANUIES and Observatorio IA
recommendations are explicitly non-binding. Regional bodies in play: **IDB** (AI regulatory
design) and **CEPAL/ILIA** (Latin American AI Index, 19 countries).

🔴 **No code, no licence, no repository in this leg** — and the Spanish-language Mexican **code**
leg (`p291` item 10) is now **discharged as a measured negative**: SaberesMx and MéxicoX ship as
**services behind a `Llave MX` identity gate**, not as repositories. 🔵 The LATAM supply gap this
shelf has tracked for dozens of passes is therefore unchanged: strong demand signal, published
policy, **no national code channel**.

## 🟢 Pass 289 — FOUR REGIONS RETURNED AGAIN, AND THE BINDING CONSTRAINT MOVED FROM *GUIDANCE* TO *NAMED HIGH-RISK SECTOR* IN A SECOND JURISDICTION

⏱️ `2026-10-18`, eleventh pass. Evidence:
[`p289-websearch.2026-10-18-p289.tsv`](../evidence/p289-websearch.2026-10-18-p289.tsv) ·
[`curriculum-authority-egress.2026-10-18-p289.tsv`](../evidence/curriculum-authority-egress.2026-10-18-p289.tsv).

🟢 **Four regional legs, four non-nil returns** — second consecutive pass with no region
declared empty. Every figure below is attributed; **vendor-sourced figures are marked and the
worst of them are NOT carried onto this shelf at all.**

🔴 **This pass's refusals, stated before its findings.** Three figures the legs offered and this
shelf declines to assert: the *"$12.3B AI-in-education market"* and *"42% improvement in
learning outcomes"* (vendor blog, no primary source), the *"$2.64B EMEA 2026 → $8.0B 2030 at
31.9% CAGR"* row (vendor-style estimate, single publisher), and *"North America = 36% of the
global market"* (aggregator, unverifiable). 🔵 `p271`-`p275` spent five passes proving this
shelf was checking one publisher against itself; the cost of that lesson is why these stay off.

### 🟢 The convergent finding: the regulatory question has split in two

`p288` found the regions splitting on *"is AI allowed to decide about a student?"*. `p289`
sharpens it: **two jurisdictions now name education a high-risk sector in binding law, and
both name ASSESSMENT specifically.**

| jurisdiction | instrument | education's status | the clock |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🇪🇺 EU | AI Act | **high-risk** where it evaluates learners or influences access to qualifications → conformity assessment + human oversight | 🔴 **sources conflict**: one guide says high-risk obligations apply from **Aug 2026**; the Commission's own milestones page lists **31 July 2026** entry into force and **27 July 2026** for the AI Omnibus. **Not resolved — the authoritative text is not reachable from here** |
| 🇻🇳 Vietnam | AI law | **one of six high-risk sectors**, explicitly including **automated assessment** and **behavioural monitoring** | compliance by **2027-03-01**; systems operating before **2026-08-15** get a further six months. 🔴 Effective-date reports vary (one says the law took effect 2026-03-01) — **carried as a conflict, not a fact** |

🔵 **Why that matters more than a market figure:** "AI that grades" is the single most common
thing a studio is asked to build in education, and it is now the named object of regulation in
the EU *and* in an APAC economy. The compliance surface is no longer a European peculiarity.

**🔴 What this pass could NOT place, and says so.**

**12 licensing-authority hosts across 10 jurisdictions were unreachable** — 2 independent
egress paths, **12/12 `POLICY-REFUSED`**. So every **curriculum-licence** statement on these
shelves, in every region, rests on a derivative publisher's restatement or on secondary press.
🔵 For a market map that is a *placement* limit worth stating: this base can tell a studio what
a national curriculum's **publisher** says its licence permits, and cannot yet tell it what the
**ministry** says.

## 🟢 Pass 288 — ALL FOUR REGIONS RETURNED, AND THE REGULATORY PICTURE NOW SPLITS ON A SINGLE QUESTION: IS AI ALLOWED TO DECIDE ABOUT A STUDENT?

⏱️ `2026-10-18`, tenth pass. Evidence:
[`p288-websearch.2026-10-18-p288.tsv`](../evidence/p288-websearch.2026-10-18-p288.tsv) ·
[`verified-repos.2026-10-18-p288.tsv`](../evidence/verified-repos.2026-10-18-p288.tsv).

🟢 **Four regional legs, four non-nil returns** — the first pass in this base's recent run
where no region had to be declared empty. Every figure below is attributed; **vendor-sourced
numbers are marked and carried because they are the only ones available, not because they are
reliable.**

### 🟢 The convergent finding across all four regions

🔵 **Every one of the four regions is regulating the same narrow thing: AI as the *sole* basis
for a decision about a student.** The instruments differ completely; the target does not.

| region | binding instrument | how it names the limit |
|---|---|---|
| North America | 🔴 **no comprehensive national law in any of the three economies** — US state statute instead | Oklahoma and Maryland require **human oversight** and bar AI from **high-stakes student decisions** |
| EMEA | **EU AI Act** | Admission decisions, student evaluation and exam scoring are **high-risk**; requires risk management, **human oversight**, conformity assessment before deployment |
| APAC | **Vietnam** AI law (in force `2026-03-01`); **South Korea** Framework Act (in force `2026-01-22`) | Vietnam lists **education** among six high-risk sectors, high-risk **only where AI output is the sole basis for a decision without meaningful human review** |
| LATAM | 🔴 **no 2026 instrument specific to AI in education** — general AI law plus institutional governance | UNESCO IESALC: **87%** of institutions use AI, **only 26%** have a formal AI strategy |

🟢 **What that means for a studio, in one line: the human-in-the-loop checkpoint is not a
feature, it is the regulatory interface in all four regions**, and a deliverable that cannot
*evidence* the human decision point is unshippable in EMEA and APAC and exposed in North
America. 🔵 This is why `p288`'s grading-agent reading matters more than its licence verdict:
[`arijit8972/agentic-automated-assignment-grading`](https://github.com/arijit8972/agentic-automated-assignment-grading)
ships a `needs_review` terminal state with review flags on low confidence and evaluator
disagreement. **That design is the compliance artefact.** Its code is not vendorable
(no grant, 66 vendored `node_modules` grants) — the *pattern* is, and `P42` in
[`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md) writes it out.

### 🔴 Two date sets this pass refuses to assert

- **EU AI Act timing.** Sources in the same leg gave: adoption 2024 with phasing through
  2026–27; an **AI Omnibus amendment** in force late July 2026; the Act's entry into force
  `2026-07-31`; and "high-risk provisions from Aug 2026". 🔴 **Carried as UNSETTLED.** An
  engagement must read the Commission's own timeline, not this shelf.
- **Vietnam's education compliance deadline.** One source says **September 2027**; another
  describes **March 2027 plus a six-month grace period** for systems already operating.
  🔴 **Carried as UNSETTLED.**

🔵 Market sizing remains irreconcilable between firms and is carried **DIRECTIONAL only**, as
every pass since `p287` has carried it. North America is reported at **~36%** of the global
AI-in-education market and Europe at **~$2.64B (2026)** — both third-party estimates, neither
load-bearing for any recommendation on this shelf.

## 🟢 Pass 287 — THE TWO REGION-NAMED NILS WERE BROKEN IN THE SAME HOUR BY DIRECTED LEGS, AND THE NEW GENERATIVE UNIT IS THE EDUCATION MINISTRY

⏱️ `2026-10-18`, ninth pass. Evidence:
[`p287-websearch.2026-10-18-p287.tsv`](../evidence/p287-websearch.2026-10-18-p287.tsv) ·
[`verified-repos.2026-10-18-p287.tsv`](../evidence/verified-repos.2026-10-18-p287.tsv).

🔵 **The battery ran once and briefly, per the standing instruction.** Two regional legs held,
**two returned nothing education-specific for the second consecutive pass** — and both of those
two were **broken by directed legs in the same hour**, which is the finding. Nine directed legs
ran; **six returned explicit nils and every nil has a different shape.** All recorded per region
below, nils included, because an undeclared nil is indistinguishable from coverage.

🟢 **The pass's structural result, stated before the regions:** `T-p286a` established that the
generative unit is the **exam board** and the **language of instruction**, not the region name.
🟢 **`p287` adds a third: the EDUCATION MINISTRY AND ITS POLICY INSTRUMENT.** A state that
**buys** rather than **builds** leaves a policy trail and no commit history, so a code-shaped
query returns nothing and reports it as absence. Turkey and Rwanda are both that shape, and
both were invisible to the region-named legs.

### 🟡 North America — held, with two NEW datapoints and a governance gap that keeps widening

🟢 **New this pass:** a **$169 M** government commitment to responsible AI in higher education
in **Q1 2026**, and an **OpenAI country-level education programme with eight national
partners**. 🔵 The second is the more strategically significant: a **vendor** is now
contracting at **national** scale, which is the procurement shape that closes a market to
open-source alternatives before a studio is invited in.

🟡 Carried, unchanged and still vendor-sourced: NA **~$951 M (2024) → ~$2.3 B (2029)**, CAGR
**15.9 %**; **~36 %** regional share; one independent house puts NA at **37.5 %** of 2025.
🔴 **The adoption-governance gap is the durable finding and it is wider than LATAM's:**
**86 %** of students across 16 countries using AI, against **~10 %** of institutions with
formal AI guidelines and **71 %** of US teachers without AI training.
🔴 **Regulation remains state-level and fragmented** — Colorado and Texas named, no federal
framework, and *no FDA-equivalent for educational technology*. For an engagement that means
**the compliance surface is the client's own policy**, because there is no external one to
point at.

### 🟢 EMEA — the English region-named leg returned NOTHING for a second pass, and a TURKISH leg broke it immediately

🔴 **The nil, recorded exactly:** the leg returned EU AI Act and enterprise-AI generalities
(Workday, CompTIA, Bandwidth-Cavell) — **no education-sector players, no 2026 education
adoption figures, no education-specific rules.** Second consecutive pass.

🟢 **One directed leg in Turkish refuted it on the first attempt.** Türkiye's **MEB** has a
national instrument — *Eğitimde Yapay Zeka Politika Belgesi ve Eylem Planı* — with a countable
structure: **4 goals, 15 policies, 40 action steps**, covering ethics rules, AI literacy and
teacher training, and **mandating a `Yapay Zeka Uygulamaları Etik Kurulu`** (AI Applications
Ethics Board). Alongside it, **ODTÜ + Kodluyoruz** run a free *"Herkes için Yapay Zeka"*
programme for high-school students on ODTÜ's Bilgeİş platform.

🔵 **Why this is an opportunity and not a trivium:** a ministry that has **already published an
ethics board and a 40-step action plan** has pre-committed to a compliance vocabulary. An
engagement that arrives speaking it is reading from the client's own document — and this is
also the first EMEA state on this shelf whose AI-in-education posture is **documented and not
inferred from the AI Act**.

🔴 **No MEB repository exists**, and the licence of the "open source" SDKs announced with its
earlier software-training programme is **unstated**. Code nil, policy grant.

### 🟢 APAC — the English region-named leg returned NOTHING for a second pass, and FIVE code rows came from four directed legs

🔴 **The nil:** Pearson+TCS's AI learning alliance, LearnUpon's Sydney HQ and Create+ authoring,
Alteryx's Academy relaunch, OpenAI's ANZ policy lead. **All corporate learning.** Nothing on
education ministries, curriculum policy or school adoption rates. Second consecutive pass.
🟡 One governance figure worth carrying because it generalises: **87 %** of organisations
encourage AI agents, **47 %** have governance for them — the same adoption-governance shape the
education figures show, in the enterprise.

🟢 **The directed legs produced the pass's code, all of it:** 🇮🇳 `avps82/mentar` (AGPL-3.0, 839
files) and `somenssarkar/gurukul-ai` (NCERT/CBSE, no grant); 🇵🇰 `saif2012004/PrepifyAI` (FBISE,
389 files, no grant); 🇨🇳 `heronerar/OpenGaokao` (**source-available, says so itself**); 🇮🇩
`sira313/rapkumer` (**total commercial prohibition**, now readable).

🔴 **The licence posture of APAC's education code, measured rather than characterised: five rows,
ONE usable grant, and that one is AGPL.** For a studio deliverable that is the hardest regional
position on this shelf — harder than West Africa's, because **West Africa's rows are unlicensed
(which an ask can fix) while APAC's are deliberately restricted (which it cannot).**
🔵 **That distinction is the one a regional proposal has to get right**, and this base had the
two cases filed under one heading until now.

### 🟢 LATAM — the battery's strongest leg, and it CORROBORATES `p286` rather than extending it

🟢 **Independently re-derived this pass**, which is worth more than a new figure: Digital
Education Council LATAM 2026 — **92 % of students, 79 % of faculty** actively engaging with AI,
**30 000+ responses, 29 institutions**. UNESCO IESALC across **200 institutions in 19
countries** gives the adoption *shape*: teaching and learning **73.5 %**, research **57.0 %**,
administration **34.1 %**, community engagement **20.0 %**.

🔴 **Against ~30 % of LATAM universities with a published AI policy.** 🔵 **The engagement read
is in the UNESCO split, not the headline:** administration at **34.1 %** against teaching at
**73.5 %** means the back office is the **under-served** half — and it is also the half where
the deliverable is **not** bounded by a curriculum licence (see `T-p287a`). **The quiet
opportunity in LATAM is administrative, not pedagogical.**

🟡 **Regulation, re-confirmed:** no binding regional framework. **Chile** leads with a National
AI Policy since 2021 and a law under discussion; **Brazil** and **Colombia** have national
strategies with **no education-sector rules**; **Mexico's** SEP/ANUIES recommendations are
**non-binding**. The **ILIA** index (19 countries, CEPAL among publishers) is the readiness
benchmark to quote.

🟡 **Code:** one LATAM-origin row, `guedesindev/ai_tutor_forge` (🇧🇷 **GPL-3.0**, 7 files).
🔴 **And the ENEM-specific gap is now named:** the closest work this base can find is a **2024
USP MBA thesis** proposing a GenAI ENEM tutor for low-income students — **a PDF, no code, no
licence.** 🔵 For the region with the strongest *demand* evidence on this shelf, that is the
sharpest supply gap in the base: **92 % student adoption and no permissively licensed
ENEM-aligned code at all.**

## 🟢 Pass 286 — THE FOUR REGIONAL LEGS PRODUCED NO CODE ADDRESS AND TWO EXPLICIT NILS, AND THE EXAM-BOARD LEGS PRODUCED EIGHTEEN

⏱️ `2026-10-18`, eighth pass. Evidence:

🔵 **The battery was run once and briefly, as `p285` item 6 instructed, and the result is the
clearest version yet of why.** Four regional legs: **two held with figures, two returned nothing
about education at all.** Three directed legs addressed to **exam boards and languages** returned
**18 code addresses and 6 first-hand grants**. Recorded per region below, nils included — because
an undeclared nil is indistinguishable from coverage.

### 🟡 North America — held, and two figures worth carrying

- **$951 M (2024) → $2.3 B (2029), 15.9 % CAGR** for the regional AI-in-education market
  *(MarketsandMarkets, vendor-sourced — the same house whose regional pages `p271`–`p275` showed
  are one upstream publisher re-sold; quote it as a **direction**, not a number)*.
- 🔴 **The procurement-relevant pair: only ~10 % of institutions have formal AI guidance, and
  ~71 % of US teachers report no AI training.** ⚠️ Secondary, one statistics roundup.
- 🟢 **New and concrete: Carnegie Mellon + the Gates Foundation, $55 M into AI courseware for

  high-failure-rate introductory courses**, not institution-wide agents. ⚠️ Single secondary source.
- 🔴 Regulation is **fragmented by state** (Colorado, Texas named) with **no federal education-AI
  instrument**. That is the opposite of EMEA and it changes the sale: there is no single compliance

🔴 **The leg returned enterprise AI only: not one education vendor, ministry, institution or
programme.** Its regulatory content was generic GDPR-and-AI-Act commentary. 🟢 **This base already
carries the primary fact the leg could not reach — `Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`, Council
`2026-06-29`, in force `2026-07-27`** — so the nil costs nothing except the budget it consumed.
🔵 **Stated as a property of the CHANNEL, not of the world** (`T-p284b`): *"AI education EMEA"* as
a query reaches consultancy trend-blogging. The leg that worked in EMEA was `p285`'s, addressed to
a **vendor and a plugin ecosystem** (eLeDia), not to a region.

### 🔴 APAC — an EXPLICIT NIL for education; the only hits are CORPORATE learning

🔴 No education-specific adoption or regulation returned. The closest results were **TCS + Pearson's
multi-year AI learning alliance** and **LearnUpon's Sydney HQ with AI course authoring** — both
**workforce** rather than schooling — plus Singapore's **National AI Strategy 2.0** and Australia's

— so the APAC nil is a **query** nil, and the remedy is already demonstrated.

### 🟢 LATAM — the strongest battery leg, and the adoption-governance gap is now quantified twice

- 🟢 **Digital Education Council, *AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026*: 92 % of students and
  79 % of faculty actively engaging with AI**, from **30 000+ responses across 29 institutions**.
  Partners named: **Tecnológico de Monterrey** and its Institute for the Future of Education.
- 🟢 **UNESCO IESALC, 200 institutions across 19 countries**, by function: **teaching and learning
  73.5 %**, research **57.0 %**, administration **34.1 %**, community engagement **20.0 %**.
- 🔴 **And the governance half: only ~30 % of LATAM universities have published an AI-use policy.**
  🔵 **That pairing — 92 % of students using it, 30 % of institutions governing it — is the single
  most sellable number in this file**, and it is now corroborated by two independent publishers
  (a council survey and a UN agency), which is more than most figures on this shelf can claim.
- 🔴 **Regulation stays heterogeneous and non-binding for education.** **Chile** leads with a
  National AI Policy since 2021 and a bill under discussion; **Brazil** and **Colombia** have
  national AI strategies with **no education-sector instrument**; in **Mexico**, **SEP** and
  **ANUIES** have issued recommendations that **lack binding force**. Regional indices: the **IDB**

- 🟡 Carried, unmoved: Brazil's `2026-10-13` CNE amendment (assistive essay correction **permitted**

  prohibition) is **still not homologated** in any source found this pass.

### 🟢 Africa — the regional claim on this shelf was a WEST AFRICAN sample, and East Africa refutes it

🔴 This base has published, for 17 passes, that there is **no permissively licensed African-origin
education agent**, and two passes ago that **"five African code rows, five unlicensed — the gap is
LICENSING, not supply"**. 🟢 **An East African leg refuted it on its first attempt:**
[`Tanzania-AI-Community/twiga`](https://github.com/Tanzania-AI-Community/twiga) is **MIT** (1 073 B,
read first-hand off the **`development`** ref), **230 files**, RAG over **Tanzania Institute of
Education** curriculum, with tests, migrations, monitoring and CI; `vincent-laizer/NECTA-API` is
**MIT** on the national-results channel.
🔵 **The correction is geographic, and it matters for how an engagement is scoped:** the unlicensed
cluster is **🇬🇭 Ghana and 🇳🇬 Nigeria** (five rows, five without a grant), while **🇹🇿 Tanzania has
permissive, production-shaped code with an active community organisation behind it.** A studio
proposal that treats "Africa" as one market gets the licence posture wrong in both directions.
🔴 **Kenya is the third shape:** [`gabrielmahia/shule-ai`](https://github.com/gabrielmahia/shule-ai)
(KCSE/CBC, Kiswahili + English) is listed as *open-source* on GitHub Topics and is in fact
**CC BY-NC-ND 4.0** — **non-commercial and no-derivatives**, i.e. unusable for client work twice
over. 🟡 Carried and unchanged: the **content** layer stays closed (NaCCA all-rights-reserved, WAEC
past questions under no public licence).

HolonIQ, Ginia, Vetto, Google.org/RPi and the IDB study are **all already carried**. 🟢 **Only `Elevify` is new**, single-channel secondary. 🔴 **"Never quote a single LATAM figure" STANDS: three publishers span 5.1× at a common year** |

🔴 **The pattern across two consecutive passes is now worth stating as a rule for `p286`:** the
mandated battery **corroborates** and the **gap-directed leg extends**. `p284`'s Africa leg and
`p285`'s exam-board and Kuali legs produced every new row in both passes; eight verbatim regional
legs across the two produced **one widened spread and one seed round**.

### 🔴 Africa — the content-licence constraint, read first-hand, and it reshapes the engagement

🟢 **New this pass, and it is not a policy finding — it is a payload one.** `Angel-builds/edubot-africa`
states in its own README that **NaCCA's curriculum PDFs are free to download but marked
*all rights reserved*, and that WAEC past questions carry *no public licence***. Its remedy is the
reusable part: it uses **the curriculum as taxonomy, not as content** — NaCCA's `B7.1.1.1`
annotation scheme becomes a **skill graph**, and explanatory prose is grounded on **openly licensed
sources** instead. See [`../compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md) `P17` (curriculum that cannot be licensed) and `P19` (regional supply that serves no grant), both **extended this pass**.

🔴 **This generalises well beyond Ghana.** National curricula and examination boards are the two
assets every education engagement wants to ground on, and in this region **both are closed**. The
constraint is therefore **architectural, not legal-review**: a design that stores curriculum text
cannot ship, and a design that stores curriculum *structure* can.

## 🟢 Pass 284 — THE GAP-DIRECTED LEG `p283` ORDERED IN PLACE OF A VERBATIM ONE BROKE AFRICA OPEN ON ITS FIRST RUN, AND BRAZIL'S PARECER MOVED IN THE DIRECTION THIS SHELF HAD NOT RECORDED

⏱️ `2026-10-18`, sixth pass. Evidence:
[`p284-websearch.2026-10-18-p284.tsv`](../evidence/p284-websearch.2026-10-18-p284.tsv).

🔵 `p283` ended with an instruction rather than a backlog item: *"`p284` is told **not** to re-run
the four regional legs verbatim, and to spend the budget on a gap-directed leg instead."* 🟢 **It
was followed, and it is the reason this pass has two regional findings instead of an eight-for-eight
nil.** Three consecutive passes ran the mandated battery verbatim and returned nothing; one leg
aimed at the declared gap returned a shelf's worth on the first attempt.

### 🟢 Africa — the four-pass "empty" is REFUTED, and it was a query defect, not an absence

🔴 `p283` published *"Africa re-confirmed empty for the fourth time … **No open-source education AI
project for Africa or the Middle East** was returned, again"*, on the strength of the EMEA leg's
Africa content. 🟢 **A leg addressed to Africa directly returned the following, none of it on this
shelf before.** The pattern is the one `p254` named — *the base inherits its own conclusions about
what is impossible* — and the remedy was one differently-worded search.

| fact | detail | grade |
|---|---|---|
| **AU Continental Education Strategy `2026–2035`** | Treats **digitalization and AI as one of three megatrends** education systems must adapt to — the continental frame this shelf had no entry for | institutional |
| **AU Continental AI Strategy (2024)** | Places education at the centre of inclusive digital futures | institutional |
| **Kenya National AI Strategy `2025–2030`** | Plans to **embed AI literacy across education** and pilot **Digital Innovation Hubs** as curriculum anchors | institutional |
| **South Africa — DBE coding & robotics** | Curricula under development for **Grades R–9** | reported |
| **South Africa — North-West University** | Reported as the country's **first official university AI policy**, **January 2026** | reported |
| **Pan-African ethical AI curriculum** | `arXiv 2604.27708` — culturally contextualized framework for primary/secondary, **age-banded progression**, **Ubuntu-based ethics** | preprint |
| **LLMs for career guidance, 10 African countries** | `arXiv 2510.18902` — recommends African universities **prioritise open-source LLMs**; open models (Llama) outscored a proprietary one on the task. ⚠️ **One evaluation, one task** — not a model ranking | preprint |
| **African higher-education constraints** | 2026 exploratory study (ICHEI): **every** digitalization/AI constraint rated **≥ 3.5 of 5**, outdated faculty capability and infrastructure among them | study |

🔵 **The studio-relevant reading, and it is not "Africa is now covered".** What the leg returned is
**policy and curriculum frames, plus two preprints** — it still returned **no production open-source
education agent built for an African deployment**. 🟢 So the gap narrows and changes shape: this base
now holds the **regulatory and curricular context** for an African engagement and still holds **no
shelf-ready code artefact** for one. The two African-context repositories this base already carries
([`muskvdc/EduTwin-Africa`](https://github.com/muskvdc/EduTwin-Africa), pre-licence, and
[`Valexxy/edunaija-os`](https://github.com/Valexxy/edunaija-os), no grant) remain the only code, and
both still serve no usable licence.

🔴 **A NAME COLLISION a studio will hit, and this base has been burned by this exact shape twice.**
Reporting names an AI robot tutor called **"Iris"**, launched in **South Africa, August 2025**. This
KB already carries **`Iris`** as the user-facing name of the TUM tutor that
[`ls1intum/Pyris`](https://github.com/ls1intum/Pyris) powers — the row `p282` withdrew as an alias.
**Two unrelated artefacts, one name**, which is precisely the `EduNaija` double-artefact trap already
recorded in `intel/trends.md`. 🔵 Cite "Iris" with its origin attached, always.

### 🟢 LATAM — the ranked homologation watch MOVED, and the movement is the opposite of what this shelf recorded

🔴 **What this file published at `## Opportunities by region → LATAM` until this pass:** that a
`2026-10-13` amendment *"bars AI tools from determining a pupil's approval or failure — marks are
given only by teachers"*. 🟢 **That half is confirmed and stands.** 🔴 **What was missing is the
other half, and it inverts the engagement:** the same amendment **PERMITS** AI in essay correction,
which the CNE's September text had **prohibited outright**.

| element of `Parecer CNE/CP nº 15/2026` | state as of `2026-10-18` |
|---|---|
| CNE approval | **`2026-09-01`**; súmula in the **DOU `2026-10-06`** (Ed. 189, Seç. 1, p. 18) |
| 🟢 **AI in essay/dissertation correction** | **PERMITTED as a `primeiro filtro` — a first pass that assists the teacher.** The CNE's original text banned it; **MEC changed this before homologation**, judging that a total ban *"impedia um recurso que facilita a rotina docente sem substituir o professor"* |
| 🔴 **final mark, approval and failure** | **PROHIBITED to automated systems.** The teacher assigns the grade and decides progression |
| 🔴 **`educação infantil` (early childhood)** | **USE TOTALLY FORBIDDEN** at this stage; later stages carry progressive rules |
| ⏱️ **adequacy window** | institutions have **one year** to comply — 🔴 **but the PROHIBITIONS apply from publication of the parecer**, not after the window |
| 🔴 **homologation** | **STILL not confirmed in any source found.** Reporting of `2026-10-13`/`2026-10-14` says minister **Leonardo Barchini** would homologate that week. Under **art. 2 of Lei nº 9.131/1995 there is no normative force until the homologating act is published** |

🔵 **Why this is the most actionable regulatory cell in this file.** The pair of rules is not a
restriction plus a restriction; it is **a design brief with a named architecture**: assistive
grading is *expressly permitted*, autonomous decision-making is *expressly forbidden*, and the
boundary is the **assignment of the mark**. A studio can build essay-support tooling for Brazilian
institutions **today** and must put the teacher on the mark. 🔴 And the two-speed commencement is a
trap for a delivery plan: a team that reads *"one year to adapt"* and ships an autonomous grader in
month three has shipped into a prohibition that was already in force.

⚠️ **Grade: secondary-source, two outlets, consistent.** `T61` still blocks the primary texts —
`mixvale.com.br` and `semesp.org.br` both failed DNS resolution from this sandbox this pass, so the
DOU and the parecer itself remain unread. A second outlet (`apufsc.org.br`, `2026-10-13`) carries
the same amendment detail, so this is **two-sourced rather than single-sourced**, and the
pre-amendment September reporting (*"CNE proíbe uso de IA para corrigir redações e provas"*) is
**superseded, not contradictory** — it describes the text before MEC changed it.

### 🟡 APAC — held, with ONE new figure

🟢 Every APAC fact this base holds was returned again and **matched to the digit**: China compulsory

The 2026 baseline is a **band of $8.7–11.4B — 31.0% wide, not the "narrow" one this sentence
claimed until pass 273** (the sentence was written before Grand View's $11.4B row was added to the table above it, and was
never re-read against it) — and the long-range forecasts differ by roughly **4×**. Nobody disagrees about the size of the market today; they disagree about
whether it is a $32B market in 2030 or a $137B market in 2035. **Quote the baseline, never the
horizon** — the horizon tells a client which vendor's blog you read. Technavio is the only one
of the five that explains its number structurally: a shift *"from experimental applications of
generative AI to the strategic deployment of autonomous agentic workflows"*, with grading and
scheduling as the named automation targets. That framing is worth more than the figure.

**Read these as direction, not as magnitude.** Every market-size figure located this pass
traces back to a vendor or aggregator blog; none traces to a primary research house
document we could open. Prior versions of this file published a five-row market-size table
with named research houses — those attributions did not survive re-checking and have been
removed rather than recycled.

a sound figure on the strength of noise is the opposite error. **For a client: give the EMEA
range ($2.1–2.6B, 2026) and say it is a two-publisher range.** A single EMEA number is the one
