#### 🔴 `p295` — THE MANDATED LEG SERVED A CLAIM THIS BASE HAS ALREADY REFUTED, AND THE STANDING GATE CAUGHT IT BEFORE PUBLICATION

🔴 **A North-America-framed source told this pass that *"the EU AI Act, which classifies education AI
as high-risk, takes full effect in August 2026."* `p295` wrote it down. 🟢 `make hooks` rejected the
line, and it was repaired before it ever reached a shelf.**

🔵 **The correction, which is the row that matters:** **Annex III point 3 — education as high-risk —
applies from `2027-12-02`, not August 2026.** What *is* live on **`2026-08-02`** is **Article 50
transparency**. 🔴 The stale claim is wrong by **fifteen months in the expensive direction**: it
tells an EMEA client they are bound *now*, and a readiness programme sold on it is sold on a date
that does not exist.

🔵 **Why this is worth a row rather than a silent edit.** [`p223-selfclaim-date-gate`](../compose/code/p223-selfclaim-date-gate/README.md)
exists because this base **published that error itself**, in this very file. It is now a standing
check — and this pass is the first time it has fired on **incoming** research rather than on legacy
prose. 🟢 **The gate is doing the job it was built for**, and the SEO/market-research corpus feeding
the mandated regional legs is confirmed to still be circulating the refuted date.

🔵 Pairs with `p294`'s **Article 6(3)** derogation citation, which remains the sharper EMEA
instrument for a client conversation.

#### 🔴 Pass 291 (`2026-10-18`) — the mandated phrasing is a MEASURED DEAD CHANNEL, and the high-risk category is wider than this shelf has been treating it

not say "schools". Limb (a) reaches access, admission or assignment to educational **and
vocational** training institutions; limb (b) the evaluation of learning outcomes including
where they steer a learner's path; **one limb suffices**. 🟢 **So corporate L&D, professional
certification and continuing-education systems are in the same high-risk bucket as a university
admissions tool** — and measured against the one MIT corpus this base has read in depth, they
are the *least* guarded part of it (1 of 6 learning agents outside the education-labelled
segments carries any human-decision guard, against 23 % inside them).

**vocational training and professional-certification** operators, not at schools. HolonIQ's
2026 Europe EdTech 200 cohort is already *weighted to workforce learning* (`p290`) — the
regulated population and the commercial population are the same population.

EUR-Lex unreachable from here (`p289`: 12 authority hosts `POLICY-REFUSED`, 2 egress paths).

schools**. Capital is down a quarter while it concentrates in workforce and marketplace models.
🟡 An EMEA engagement pitched at a ministry is pitched at the segment with the least private
capital behind it; **corporate L&D and apprenticeship delivery is where the money actually
is**, and the EU AI Act's high-risk tier applies to those just as much once their output
influences access to a qualification.

#### 🟡 Pass 288 — the EU AI Act is the binding frame and it reaches NON-EU VENDORS, but this leg again returned REGULATORS AND NO VENDORS

🟢 **The classification that governs an engagement:** the EU AI Act places **admission
decisions, student evaluation and exam scoring** in the **high-risk** tier, which requires risk
management, **human oversight** and **conformity assessment before deployment**. 🔴 **It reaches
non-EU vendors whose outputs affect EU-located students** — a LATAM- or US-built tutor sold
into an EU institution is in scope.

🔴 **Dates are UNSETTLED in this leg and are not asserted here.** The same set of sources gave
adoption in 2024 with phasing through 2026–27, an **AI Omnibus amendment** in force late July
2026, entry into force `2026-07-31`, and high-risk provisions "from Aug 2026". An engagement
reads the Commission's own timeline.

🟡 **Adoption signals (third-party, directional):** European AI-in-education market **~$2.64B
(2026)**; **Finland, Estonia and the Netherlands** cited as K-12 integration leaders; the UK
reported at **£4M** for lesson-planning and marking tools. 🔴 **Only ~10% of institutions have
formal AI-use guidelines** — adoption is running well ahead of governance.

🔴 **Declared gap, second pass running: this leg named the European Commission, UNESCO and the
OECD (Digital Education Outlook 2026) and NOT ONE EMEA ed-tech vendor.** That is a real hole in
this shelf's EMEA coverage and it is written down rather than papered over — an informed gap is
information; silence looks exactly like coverage.

🟢 **The opportunity, placed:** the high-risk tier turns **conformity evidence into the
deliverable**. The gap between *"adoption ahead of governance"* (~10% with guidelines) and a
binding high-risk regime is the single largest EMEA engagement surface on this shelf: an
institution that already deploys AI in assessment needs **documented human oversight, risk
management and conformity artefacts retrofitted onto tools it already runs**. 🔵 The authorization
posture worth copying is Open edX's MCP plugin — **re-check the platform's own staff/superuser
flags live on every request, never cache a role** — because an audit trail of *who was allowed
to ask* is half of the oversight evidence.

> **🟢 `p287` (2026-10-18) — the region-named leg returned NOTHING education-specific for a

failed, response 403"*).

**ministry with a delivery deadline**, not a university with a pilot, and the deliverable is
**curriculum-aligned teacher enablement** rather than a student-facing tutor. 🔴 It also lands
against this base's standing constraint from `p281` — China, Brazil and Singapore all restrict

> preprint**, and the leg still returned **no production open-source education agent built for an

> about what is impossible* — and `p283` is the pass that broke it, by ordering a gap-directed leg
> instead of a fourth verbatim one.

> the user-facing name of the TUM tutor [`ls1intum/Pyris`](https://github.com/ls1intum/Pyris)
> powers — the row `p282` withdrew as a GitHub alias. **Two unrelated artefacts, one name**, the
> same trap as the two `EduNaija` artefacts. Cite "Iris" with its origin attached.

(`T61`, now measured on two independent clients). 🟢 **What is settled is the status**: `p279` carried
the Omnibus with the caveat *"the proposal requires approval"*, and it is **law**.

🔴 **The actionable date is six weeks out, not fourteen months.** Every engagement conversation in
this region has been framed around `2027-12-02`; **Article 50's marking grace expires `2026-12-02`**,
and it applies to systems **already deployed**. A tutoring or content-generation deployment live in
the EU today has a **marking and disclosure** obligation landing first, and it is the cheaper of the
two to retrofit badly.


**Pass 277 — the UK's supplier-facing instrument was revised THIS YEAR, and it adds four standards
that bite on exactly what an education agent does.** `p276` ended the nine-pass EMEA nil on the EU AI
Act; this leg adds the **other** EMEA jurisdiction a studio sells into, and the finding is that England
regulates education AI **through procurement rather than statute**.

**DfE "Generative AI: product safety standards"** — retitled from *"product safety expectations"* —
first published **January 2025**, **revised 19 January 2026**. 🔴 **The revision added four standards:
cognitive development, emotional and social development, mental health, and protection against
manipulation.** The stated drivers were **changes to the Online Safety Act** and emerging generative-AI
risk.

🔵 **Why this matters more than its non-binding status suggests.** The standards are aimed at
**suppliers of learner-facing products** — which is what Globant *builds* on this shelf. They are
**not law**, but they function as the **procurement checklist** an English school or college applies,
so failing them does not attract a penalty: it loses the deal. 🔴 **And the four new standards are the
hardest to retrofit of any requirement in this KB** — "cognitive development" and "protection against
manipulation" are claims about *pedagogical design and interaction patterns*, not features that can be
added at the end. A sector commentator's read, logged as opinion rather than finding, is that **many
tools currently deployed in schools would not yet meet them**.

**Three more dated instruments from the same jurisdiction:**

- **DfE staff AI training modules updated 19 May 2026** — four modules, with Module 3 (safe use) named
  as essential for all users regardless of experience. 🔵 **A client-side training asset that already
  exists**, so an engagement should map onto it rather than author one.
- **KCSIE 2026** — statutory safeguarding guidance — now treats **AI-generated imagery including
  deepfakes** as a safeguarding matter, and filtering/monitoring reviews are expected to cover AI.
- **DfE × i.AI AI-tutoring trials from the autumn term 2026**, with tools intended to be **available in
  schools by end-2027**. 🟢 **That is a dated public-sector pipeline** — the nearest thing to a
  published buying window on this shelf.

⚠️ **One scope limit worth writing down before it is misquoted:** one supplier reads the standards as
applying **only to learner-facing platforms**, excluding teacher-facing tools. 🔵 **If that reading
holds, the compliance-cheapest first deliverable in England is teacher-facing** — which is also where
Japan put 100 of its 161 funded pilot municipalities. **Two jurisdictions, unrelated instruments, same
conclusion about where to start.**

⚠️ **GRADE: SECONDARY-CONVERGENT, PRIMARY UNREAD.** 🔴 **`www.gov.uk` is DNS-unresolvable from this
sandbox** (`getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`, reproduced twice, via two different tools), so neither the standards
nor KCSIE was read first-hand. **Binding status conflicts across secondary sources** — some describe the
standards as mandatory; DfE's own pages are reported as non-binding, and that is the reading published
here. **Same posture as `p276` took on `eur-lex`: the regulation is marked as unread, not dressed as
read.**


**Pass 276 — THE NINE-PASS EMEA NIL ENDS, and it ended by querying the INSTRUMENT instead of the
region, exactly as `p275` ranked it.** `p275` diagnosed its own EMEA blanks as *"narrow-query blanks,
not quiet regions"* and ranked one instruction for this pass: *"query the instrument, not the region."*
🟢 **Run once, it returned the single most decision-relevant fact on this shelf for an EMEA engagement.**

🔴 **The AI Act deadline an EMEA education engagement was planning around has MOVED.**

| obligation | was | **now** |
|---|---|---|
| **Annex III high-risk** — incl. **education admissions**, **assessing learning outcomes**, determining a person's level of education | `2026-08-02` | 🔴 **`2027-12-02`** |
| **Annex I** — AI embedded in regulated products | `2027-08-02` | **`2028-08-02`** |
| **Article 50** transparency duties | `2026-08-02` | 🟢 **UNCHANGED** *(machine-readable marking: grace to `2026-12-02` for systems already on the market)* |

The instrument is the **Digital Omnibus**, **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** — Council final approval
`2026-06-29`, published in the Official Journal `2026-07-24`, **in force `2026-07-27`**.

🟢 **And what did NOT move is what an education engagement actually trips over first:**
- The **prohibition on inferring emotions in education settings** has applied since **February 2025**.
  It is a ban, not a deferred high-risk duty — proctoring and engagement-detection features are
  affected **today**.
- The **Article 4 AI-literacy duty** likewise dates from that period.
- **Provider vs deployer** splits the work: a studio *building* education AI is a **provider** (risk
  management, data governance, technical documentation, conformity assessment, EU-database
  registration); the institution *using* it is a **deployer** (follow the provider's instructions,
  keep human oversight, log incidents). 🔵 **A Globant engagement is almost always the provider**, which
  is the heavier half.
- **Public institutions owe a Fundamental Rights Impact Assessment before first use.**

⚠️ **GRADE: SECONDARY-CONVERGENT, PRIMARY UNREAD.** Reg (EU) 2026/1744 and the `2027-12-02` date are
corroborated independently by **four** legal publishers (Gibson Dunn, DLA Piper, Cooley, Cloud Security
Alliance). 🔴 **`eur-lex.europa.eu` is CONNECT 403 from this environment on all three URL forms tried**
(`/eli/reg/2026/1744/oj`, `OJ:L_202601744`, `CELEX:32026R1744`), so **the Official Journal text was not
read first-hand.** This base does not publish a licence it has not read; it marks a regulation it could
not read either. **`p277` must read the OJ text before an engagement quotes a date off this shelf.**

🔵 **The commercial reading:** the deferral is **16 months of runway, not a reprieve.** The duties that
bite first (emotion-inference ban, AI literacy, Article 50 transparency) are in force now, and the
heavy provider obligations land `2027-12-02` — which is inside the lifetime of a platform built in
2026. **Build to Annex III now; the delay changes the audit date, not the architecture.**

🔴 **One EMEA supply finding, from this pass's enumeration rather than its searches:** the European
Commission's own **European Digital Credentials** reference implementation
([`european-commission-empl/european-digital-credentials`](https://github.com/european-commission-empl/european-digital-credentials))
serves **no licence grant across `2 970` enumerated paths** and no `license` element in its `pom.xml`.
**An EU-credentialing engagement cannot treat it as open source**, and that is now an enumerated
absence rather than a ladder's silence.

**Pass 272 — the EMEA leg reproduced this base's AI Act rows exactly, added one institutional
player, and returned an informative NIL on MEA.**

🟢 **Every AI Act datum the leg returned was already shelved and already correct**: Annex III

the **Art. 5** emotion-inference prohibition in education institutions from the same date. 🔵 **The
EMEA regulatory shelf is saturated; re-running this leg will not pay again.**

- 🟢 **New: Utrecht University has adopted an AI policy that classifies AI systems by risk level,
  aligned to the AI Act.** 🔵 **That is an institution which has already done the Annex III triage a
  studio engagement would otherwise have to perform with the client** — roughly two years before the

  completes a pattern across three regions: **North-West University (ZA)**, **SUNY (US, 64
  campuses)** and **Utrecht (NL)**. ⚠️ **Universities are ahead of enforcement everywhere this base

**Pass 270 — the "Finland / Estonia / Netherlands are the K-12 leaders" claim does not survive
re-verification as a group, and the three split three ways.**

- 🟢 **Estonia is substantiated, and it is the strongest EMEA adoption datum this base holds.**
  Education and Research Minister **Kristina Kallas** told *Politico* in **May 2026** that
  **about half of the country's 20,000 upper-secondary students** were already using an
  **OpenAI-partnered** education platform, with the remainder expected over that summer. A
  **Tallinn University** study of a **2024 national survey (n=15,631, grades 6-12)** describes
  Estonia as among the **first countries in Europe to put AI literacy into the national
  curriculum**. 🔵 **Estonia is therefore the one EMEA jurisdiction where national-scale
  deployment is a measured fact rather than a plan** — and note what is deployed: a
  **proprietary US platform**, in the region whose own permissive supply this file documents
  immediately above. *(secondary-source grade.)*
- 🟢 **Finland is substantiated but is a different shape, and the difference matters for scoping.**
  Finland has built **no standalone AI subject**; AI literacy is woven into existing subjects via
  **"phenomenon-based learning"**, with the **Ministry of Education and Culture** naming AI skills
  a national priority across all education levels, with teacher support and safe use as focus
  areas. 🔵 **A Finnish engagement is therefore a cross-curricular integration problem, not a
  course-delivery one** — the deliverable attaches to existing subject materials, so
  curriculum-mapping assets (`DECK6/korean-elementary-learning-map`-shaped) matter more than
  course generators.
- 🔴 **The Netherlands is NOT substantiated, and is now an informed gap rather than a carried
  claim.** The only Dutch trace located is its inclusion among the **14 countries** of a European
  teacher survey. The three-country "K-12 leaders" grouping itself traces **only to an aggregator
  citing no source**. 🔵 **This base will stop asserting Dutch leadership.** The named next step,
  costed: **Dutch-language queries against the Ministerie van OCW and SLO**, which this pass did
  not run.

**Pass 270 — the Sanoma survey finally reaches this file, and its second number is the one that
sells education-specific tooling.** The **Sanoma Learning 2026 European Teacher Survey**
(**>20,000 teachers across 14 countries**) reports **teacher AI use at 63% across Europe** — and
**only 16% of teachers believing that GENERAL-PURPOSE AI improves learning outcomes**.
🟢 **That 47-point gap between use and believed efficacy is the single most useful EMEA sentence
in this file for a studio conversation:** European teachers have already adopted AI and have
already concluded that the general-purpose tools are not working for them. **The pitch is not
"adopt AI"; it is "replace the general-purpose tool you are already using with something built
for your curriculum and your assessment rules."** *(vendor-published survey — Sanoma sells
education materials, so the finding flatters the publisher. Graded accordingly, and carried
because the direction is corroborated by the 63%/16% split being self-damaging to general-purpose
vendors rather than to Sanoma's own category.)*
🔴 **Why it was missing:** `agents/trending.md` states, of this very survey, *"Recorded in
`intel/market.md` and `intel/trends.md`."* **It was in neither.** See the pass-270 gap addendum.

**Pass 266 addition — EMEA is where the permissive supply actually comes from, which inverts the
usual framing.** Both assessment engines verified this pass are EMEA-origin and permissive:
**Artemis** (MIT, TU Munich, 819★, self-hosted by universities in several countries) and
**Numbas** (Apache-2.0, Newcastle University, 215★). Beside Richie/Marsha (MIT, France
Université Numérique) and OpenOLAT (Apache-2.0, Swiss), the EMEA engagement is **not** an import
of US tooling — the regionally-built, regionally-governed, permissively-licensed stack exists.
In a market where procurement asks about provenance and sovereignty, that is a differentiator
rather than a footnote.

**Numbas answers a data-residency objection structurally.** Its assessments execute **entirely
in the browser** — a Python 3 compiler emitting a JavaScript runtime — so a student submission
**need never reach a server**. Under GDPR that is a far stronger position than a processing
agreement over a cloud marking service, and it is the kind of architecture-level answer that
shortens a procurement cycle.

⚠️ **Governance is the gating constraint, not appetite:** one 2026 roundup reports **only ~10%
of 450+ surveyed institutions have formal AI-use guidelines** (secondary-source grade).
🟢 **`p274` reconciles this against the "nearly two-thirds of European education systems" figure
further down this section, which a reader coming down the page reads as a contradiction. It is not
one.** The two-thirds counts **national education systems** holding a strategy, guidance or
framework; the ~10% counts **institutions** holding formal AI-use guidelines of their own.
**Different populations, different predicates** — and read together they *are* the EMEA pitch: the
ministry has a policy and the institution has nothing with which to comply with it. 🔵 **And the
predicate travels: `p274`'s LATAM leg returns UNESCO's regional Observatory at FEWER THAN 10% of
institutions** with formal guidelines *and* sufficient capacity. **Two regions, two instruments, the
same ~10% against the same predicate** — the first cross-regional corroboration on this shelf that
is not a re-publication. The
**OECD 2026 Digital Education Outlook** recommends moving beyond general-purpose assistants
toward **purpose-built educational AI** — which is an argument, from a body ministries cite, for
exactly the Artemis/Iris-style deployment over a general chatbot rollout.

The clearest regulatory calendar in the world, and a **14-month window** before it closes.

- **The window is the offer.** High-risk obligations for education now bite on
  **2027-12-02**, not August 2026. Clients who read the delay as a reprieve will arrive late;
  the engagement is a compliance-by-design build *during* the window — classification of
  in-scope systems, human-oversight design, documentation.
- **Transparency is already live (2026-08-02).** Disclosure that a learner is talking to an
  AI, and deepfake disclosure, are obligations **today**. This is a short, concrete,
  immediately billable remediation scope.
- **Provenance as a procurement requirement.** `lineage-skill` (Apache-2.0) keeps generated
  material tied to its sources — the shape of evidence an AI Act file needs.
- **Sovereign deployment.** Moodle's AI subsystem pointed at local Ollama/vLLM means no
  student prompt leaves the member state. Pair with EMEA-origin, permissive bases:
  **OpenOLAT** (Apache-2.0, CH), **Artemis** (MIT, DE), **Richie/Marsha** (MIT, FR).
- **The market is ready**: nearly **two-thirds of European education systems** already have
  an AI strategy, guidance or policy framework, and two European Commission reports
  (2026-09-22) describe generative AI as a **mainstream** tool in EU education.
- **Two obligations the 2027 delay does *not* touch, and both are sellable now.** Emotion
  inference in schools is **in principle prohibited** — a prohibition, not a high-risk duty, so
  no deadline relief applies; any engagement touching attention-tracking, affect detection or
  "engagement scoring" from camera or voice must be audited against it **today**. And the
  **AI-literacy duty** on deploying organisations is already live. A prohibition audit plus a
  staff AI-literacy programme is a short, defensible first engagement that does not wait for
  December 2027.
- **Consent is a design constraint in the European Schools network.** Schola Europaea's
  guidelines mean **pupils cannot be required to use genAI** unless the tool is approved. Any
  learner-facing design needs a fully equivalent non-AI path — not a degraded fallback. Build
  it in from the first sprint; retrofitting an opt-out into an agentic flow is a rewrite.
- **Where EMEA clients self-host, they already reach for open weights.** 2026 deployment
  guidance describes districts with data-residency requirements self-hosting open-weight
  models while the mainstream uses hosted APIs. That is the exact architecture this KB
  recommends (Moodle AI subsystem or OpenOLAT against local Ollama/vLLM), which makes it an
  easier sale than it was a year ago.

**Pass 267 — a ministry's production code is now MIT, and the public-sector platform gap closes.**

- 🟢 **The UK Department for Education is an upstream maintainer under MIT.**
  [`DFE-Digital/apply-for-teacher-training`](https://github.com/DFE-Digital/apply-for-teacher-training)
  — **MIT under Crown Copyright, 34,414 commits**, a live service at
  `apply-for-teacher-training.service.gov.uk`; beside it
  [`DFE-Digital/teaching-vacancies`](https://github.com/DFE-Digital/teaching-vacancies) (MIT).
  **A studio can reuse a national ministry's production service code with zero copyleft
  obligation.** For EMEA public-sector bids this converts "we will build you something like a
  government service" into "we will extend the pattern a government already runs and publishes" —
  and MIT means the client's deliverable stays theirs.
- 🟢 **A European public-sector school platform exists:** [`hpi-schul-cloud/schulcloud-server`](https://github.com/hpi-schul-cloud/schulcloud-server)
  (**AGPL-3.0**, 16,192 commits) with its Nuxt front end — the EMEA counterpart to Sunbird (APAC)
  and i-Educar (LATAM), built around data-protection-first school IT, which is the posture EMEA
  procurement actually tests. ⚠️ **Provenance is org-handle grade only** — the README names no
  ministry, funder or Land, so do **not** brief it as a federal German programme. AGPL's network
  clause prices any hosted deployment.
- 🟡 **High-stakes testing has an EMEA-origin platform, at a GPL-2.0 price.**
  [`oat-sa/package-tao`](https://github.com/oat-sa/package-tao) — TAO, *"created by the University
  of Luxembourg"*, maintained by Open Assessment Technologies. This is the layer above coursework
  assessment where Artemis and Numbas stop. **GPL-2.0 is the most restrictive licence on this
  shelf** and is not AGPL-compatible: the integration boundary is the delivery API, never the
  engine. ⚠️ Secondary sources tie TAO to famous international assessment programmes; **its README
  claims none and this KB asserts none** — verify with OAT before it reaches a deck.
- 🟡 **Permissive assessment remains the EMEA strength:** `Artemis` (MIT, TUM) and `Numbas`
  (Apache-2.0, Newcastle, browser-only execution). Pass 267 adds the **offline assessment** half —
  [`Tangerine`](https://github.com/Tangerine-Community/Tangerine) (GPL-3.0, 8,064 commits), whose
  README claims 5M+ assessments across 65 countries — relevant to EMEA development-funded work in
  Africa and the Middle East, where connectivity is the binding constraint.
- ⚠️ **An informed gap, recorded rather than hidden:** the required regional search leg for EMEA
  (*"AI education EMEA 2026 adoption regulation players"*) returned **nothing education-specific** —
  only enterprise-AI adoption and compliance material (EU AI Act as a general driver, compliance
  complexity as the stated barrier, the UK/DE/FR/NL/Nordics as leading adopters, UAE and Saudi
  Arabia treating AI as national priority). **No education-sector player list, adoption rate or
  ministry programme surfaced.** This base's existing EU AI Act dates (including the
  **2027-12-02** resolution) remain more precise than anything this pass's searches returned, and
  were left untouched.

spending searches on primary retrieval. No ministry PDF was read.

🟢 **The regional shape is the inverse of LATAM's, and that changes the pitch.** In LATAM this base
measured **adoption outrunning governance** (92% of students, 79% of faculty, against ~30% of
universities with published policies) — so the sellable work there is **governance retrofit**
(`P13`). In MEA the **state programme arrives first**: ministries, national frameworks, funded
rollouts, enrolment counts and teacher-training targets — **and the permissive supply is absent at
the payload** (see [`repos/trending.md`](../repos/trending.md) and
[`agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md)). 🔵 **The demand is already funded, named and dated, and nothing
off-the-shelf satisfies it. That is a better starting position than a market that has already bought
something.**

🟢 **Egypt is the most engageable of the four, for a structural reason.** It is the only one of the
four whose 2026 instrument is a **teacher-competency framework** rather than a device rollout or a
curriculum line — which means the deliverable it implies is **teacher-facing workflow and training
content**, the exact shape that permissive supply *does* exist for (`Instructional Agents`, MIT,
EACL'26; `Claw-ED`, MIT, local-first; `lineage-skill`, Apache-2.0, provenance-tracked). ⚠️ **But do
not name its delivery platform:** reporting splits between **QUREO**, the Japanese **Kiryo** and a
**Sprix** contract approved in January 2026. Three names, one slot — brief the framework, not the
vendor.

🟡 **South Africa is the one to approach at institution level, not ministry level.** Its universities
are ahead of its schools (NWU's policy, USAf's register) while the national policy has been
**withdrawn** and the schools curriculum is still being written. **An institutional engagement has a
policy to comply with; a schools engagement has no national instrument to anchor to.** That is the
reverse of Kenya and Egypt, where the national programme is the anchor.

🔴 **Declared holes in this subsection, so it is not read as complete.** There is **no MEA education
market sizing of any kind in this base** — no spend figure, no vendor share, no CAGR for Africa or
the Middle East surfaced on any of the four legs. **The region now has policy and programme rows and
still has no number.** Nothing was found for the Gulf states, the Maghreb, or any sub-Saharan country
other than Kenya, Nigeria and South Africa; the four-query instruction covered four countries, and
the rest of MEA remains exactly as unexamined as before.



#### Pass 271 — the Netherlands retraction is OVERTURNED by a Dutch-language leg, and the instrument is a dated future mandate

`p270` retracted the Netherlands and wrote the remedy down without running it: *"Costed next step, NOT
run: Dutch-language queries against the **Ministerie van OCW** and **SLO**."* **Pass 271 ran it, and
there is a national instrument.**

🟢 **The instrument: `kerndoelen digitale geletterdheid` (core objectives for digital literacy), with
`AI-geletterdheid` explicitly included.**

| property | value |
|---|---|
| Scope | **primair onderwijs** + **onderbouw vo** + **(voortgezet) speciaal onderwijs** |
| Status today | 🟡 **CONCEPT** — "definitief concept" (Jul 2025); functional kerndoelen (Nov 2025) |
| Legally official | 🔴 **2027-08-01** |
| Fully introduced + **Inspectie** oversight | **2031** |
| Structure | three domains; the practical domain includes **AI exploration** — pupils learn to recognise AI in their surroundings, how it works, and its social and ethical consequences |

🔵 **So `p270` was half right, and the half it got wrong is the instructive half.** The *aggregator's*
claim — the Netherlands **"leading in K-12 AI integration"**, present tense — remains **unsupported**:
nothing located shows Dutch deployment at Estonia's scale. But `p270` concluded *"no national Dutch
instrument or programme was located"*, and that is now **false**. 🟢 **The lesson: retract the CLAIM,
not the COUNTRY.** `NOT_ESTABLISHED` was carrying two different meanings — *"the assertion is wrong"* and
*"nothing is there"* — and the second was an artefact of **searching in English**. New trend **`T35`**.

🟢 **Commercially this is better than the claim it replaces.** A present-tense "leader" is a market you
have already lost. **A concept curriculum that becomes law on 2027-08-01 and is inspected from 2031 is a
procurement runway with a date on it** — and the band is **primary + lower secondary**, which is where
`Gap 367` says permissive supply does not exist. See new pattern **`P21`**.

🟢 **Two further OCW findings, both second-sourced to parliamentary documents:**
- **Kamerbrief on `digitale autonomie` (2026-07-02)** names responsible AI use as part of public digital
  provision for `scholen`, `mbo`, `hogescholen` and `universiteiten`, and states that Dutch education is
  **"in grote mate afhankelijk van niet-Europese techbedrijven"** — *largely dependent on non-European
  tech companies*. 🔵 **This is a ministry writing down the sovereignty problem in its own words**, in
  the region whose permissive supply this base documents. Pair it with `p270`'s Estonia finding (national
  deployment on an OpenAI-partnered proprietary platform): **the EMEA pitch is sovereignty, and two
  governments have now supplied the argument themselves.**
- **Beleidsbrief 2026-2030 (Apr 2026)** requires institutions in `vervolgonderwijs` to respond to
  generative AI; OCW runs a **"Programma AI"** (*Stand van de Uitvoering*, Jun 2026).
- ⚠️ Minister **Rianne Letschert** / staatssecretaris **Tielen** — **Wikipedia-grade**, not confirmed
  against a primary channel.
- ⚠️ **One secondary source (go21) expected nationwide introduction in 2026 and is OUTDATED.** The
  August-2027 date is given consistently by Kennisnet and the Expertisepunt.

🟡 **Also returned, and shelved as context not as education policy:** UK **£4M** for AI lesson-planning
and marking tools; Germany's **€20B** AI commitment 2025–2030 (**general AI, not education** — do not
cite it as an education figure); EU enterprise AI adoption **19.95%** against an OECD **20.2%**.

🔴 **The open web still carries stale EU AI Act dates** — one source in this pass's leg still gives
**February 2025** as the enforcement start. This base's correction, made nineteen passes ago, holds.

#### Pass 279 — the live obligation is a TRANSPARENCY duty, and the high-risk date is still fifteen months out

🔴 **This is the claim most likely to be got wrong in an EMEA proposal, and an inbound source got it
wrong again this pass** *(fifth consecutive — `T66`)*:

| obligation | applies | status |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **Article 50 transparency** — users must be told they are dealing with an AI system | **2026-08-02** | 🟢 **LIVE NOW** |
| 🔴 **Annex III point 3 — education as high-risk** | **2027-12-02** | 🔴 **NOT yet in force** |
| Annex I | **2028-08-02** | not in force |

🔵 Instrument: **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744**, OJ **2026-07-24**, in force **2026-07-27**; Parliament
**2026-06-16**, Council **2026-06-29**. ⚠️ Still unverified against EUR-Lex, which is unreachable here
*(`ACTION ND`)* — so quote it as **reported** in a deck, and say so in the meeting.

🟢 **What a studio can sell TODAY against a 2027 deadline:** the duties that are already live and the
ones that take longest to build. **Article 26** makes a school using a purchased tool responsible for
monitoring it and for informing students, parents and teachers; **Article 27** requires a
**Fundamental Rights Impact Assessment** from deployers *governed by public law* — **which a state
school or state university is** — before deploying an Annex III point 3 system. 🔵 **An FRIA is a
document with a method, not a feature**, and a 2027 deadline is a 2026 engagement because the
assessment has to precede the deployment. 🔴 Emotion inference in educational institutions is
**prohibited in principle**, which rules out a whole category of "engagement analytics".

🟡 **Market, for sizing only:** Europe ~**$512.6M** (2024), UK / Germany / France the largest
contributors; **64%** of Europeans expect AI literacy to be necessary by 2030 while only **37%** in
Germany use AI for work or study. Named commercial players: **LEGO Education, Oxford University Press,
Seppo, Cornelsen, Lalilo** — 🔴 **none of them open source**, so none is a build-on starting point.


#### Pass 289 (`2026-10-18`) — EMEA

🟢 **The clock is the story, and the member-state layer is where the buildable work is.**

- **EU AI Act** high-risk classification for learner evaluation and qualification access
  (dates conflicting — see the table above)
- **OECD Digital Education Outlook 2026**, by member state: 🇨🇿 **Czechia** embeds generative
  AI in **mandatory** digital competence; 🇫🇷 **France** teaches AI literacy through the **Pix**
  platform; 🇱🇹 **Lithuania** is preparing a **national register of approved AI tools** plus
  teacher competence frameworks; 🇫🇮 **Finland**'s national agency has issued GenAI
  recommendations
- 🇬🇧 **England**: the national curriculum is **ceded as data** — Oak National Academy publishes
  it under **OGL v3.0** with commercial use explicit (read first-hand this pass)

🔵 **The opportunity, placed:** **Lithuania's approved-tool register is a procurement gate, and
a gate is a specification.** An agent that can produce a conformity file — model provenance,
oversight path, data boundaries — is selling into a named list rather than a vague compliance
mood. And England is the one jurisdiction in this base where the **curriculum itself** is
licensed for commercial reuse, which makes it the natural pilot for curriculum-aligned work.

🟡 **The EMEA VENDOR gap `p288` declared is STILL OPEN.** Three passes have now returned only
regulators and standards bodies — Commission, OECD, UNESCO, national agencies — and **not one
EMEA ed-tech vendor**. 🔴 A declared hole, not a silence, and it stays ranked.

category but **files it under `EMEA`** (see the Central Asia block there), so a reader comparing
APAC-only will not see it.

🔴 **The standing APAC problem is now explicit: ANZ is not part of the compliance market and the
corporate-learning corpus crowds out the education one.** A directed, country-named leg
(`p291`'s Indonesia, `p293`'s ASEAN) has broken this nil **every time it has been tried**. The
mandated regional phrasing has broken it **never**.

#### 🟢 `p296` · `2026-10-18` — THREE BINDING NATIONAL AI LAWS ARE NOW IN FORCE IN THIS REGION, AND ONE HAS AN EDUCATION-SPECIFIC GRACE PERIOD

🔴 **APAC is no longer the "voluntary guidance" region this base has been briefing.** It is split, and
the split is now between **statute** and **guidance** rather than between countries:

| jurisdiction | instrument | in force | what it means for an education engagement |
|---|---|---|---|
| **South Korea** | **Framework Act on AI** — a *general* AI law | 🔴 **22 January 2026** | the region's first horizontal statute. An EdTech deployment is in scope as an AI system, not via a sector rule |
| **Vietnam** | **law on AI** | 🔴 **1 March 2026** | ⚠️ **education has its own staggered deadline**: systems in **education, healthcare and finance already operating before 15 August 2026** get an **additional six-month grace period** — i.e. a migration window, and it is the only education-named compliance date in the region |
| **China** | service-specific regulation | in force | regulates *specific services* rather than AI generally — classify the service, not the model |
| **Japan** | promotion-oriented | — | promotes rather than penalises; no penalty regime to design against |
| **Singapore, India, Australia** | voluntary guidance + existing sector/data law | — | the obligation arrives through data protection and sector rules, not an AI act |

🔵 **The Vietnam grace period is a dated, nameable engagement** and `P12` already exists for it —
re-check that pattern's dates against **1 March 2026 / 15 August 2026 + 6 months**.

**Public sentiment, which decides whether a student-facing pilot is sellable.** The **Ipsos Education
Monitor 2026** finds **lower support for banning AI in schools across the Asian markets surveyed** —
🔴 **with Australia and New Zealand as the explicit exception, recording *higher* support for a ban.**
**ANZ is therefore not an "APAC" market for this purpose**: the same student-facing pilot that sells
in Southeast Asia is the one that draws opposition in Australia. Place ANZ separately, as this base
already learned to do for Africa (`T-p286a`).

⚠️ **A regional caution the leg produced on itself.** A Digital in Asia summary argues adoption
**breadth** across the region is extraordinary while **depth remains shallow** — which is the pattern
that makes headline adoption percentages useless for scoping. Ask what is in production, not what is
adopted.

🟢 **This region gained three permissive agent rows this pass, all from the orphan list, and APAC is
now the best-supplied region on the agents shelf:**
[`THU-MAIC/OpenMAIC`](https://github.com/THU-MAIC/OpenMAIC) (**MIT, 41.0k★**, Tsinghua),
[`Orenda-Project/rumi-platform`](https://github.com/Orenda-Project/rumi-platform) (**Apache-2.0**,
847 commits, Pakistan/Taleemabad, national curriculum, 15 languages, **WhatsApp delivery**) and
[`microsoft/Shiksha-Copilot`](https://github.com/microsoft/Shiksha-Copilot) (**MIT**, MSR India +

**Named commercial players** (market-research sourced, ⚠️ unverified): Riiid (KR), Squirrel AI (CN),

#### 🟢 Pass 288 — TWO BINDING AI LAWS ARE NOW IN FORCE, and Vietnam names EDUCATION a high-risk sector outright

🟢 **South Korea — Framework Act on AI (the "AI Basic Act"), in force since `2026-01-22`.** The
region's broadest general AI law and the closest analogue to the EU AI Act, though shorter. Its
generative-AI provisions require that **users be told they are dealing with AI** and that
**generated content be marked**.

🟢 **Vietnam — AI law in force `2026-03-01`**, and a **July 2026 decision lists EDUCATION among
six high-risk sectors**, with automated assessment and behavioural monitoring named as
examples. 🔵 **The limiting clause is the one that matters commercially:** many listed systems
are high-risk **only where the AI output is the sole basis for a decision without meaningful
human review**. A deliverable with an evidenced human checkpoint can sit outside the high-risk
tier. 🔴 **The compliance deadline is UNSETTLED** — September 2027 per one source, March 2027
plus a six-month grace period for systems already operating per another. Not asserted here.

🟡 **The rest of the region, by posture:** Taiwan has a principles-based **AI Basic Act** with a
national AI committee from **March 2026**; **Japan** promotes AI without penalties; **China**
regulates by service type; **Singapore, India and Australia** rely on voluntary guidance plus
existing privacy and sector rules.

🔴 **Public sentiment runs against classroom AI, which is a product constraint and not a
footnote.** Ipsos Education Monitor 2026: support for **banning AI in schools** ranges from
**23% (Indonesia)** to **31% (South Korea)**, with Australia and New Zealand higher still. A
APAC deployment is selling into measurable parental scepticism.

🔴 **Declared gap:** policy data for **South Asia and parts of Southeast Asia is thin**, and
**Indonesia's AI presidential regulations were still pending as of mid-2026**.

🟢 **The opportunity, placed:** Vietnam's sole-basis clause and Korea's disclosure-and-marking
duties are **the same engineering requirement wearing two legal labels** — provenance marking
on every generated artefact plus a recorded human decision point. Build that once and the
deliverable clears both, and clears the EMEA high-risk tier besides. 🔵 For the **disclosure**
half specifically, Korea's marking duty is the strictest concrete requirement this base has
recorded in any region, so it is the one to design to.

> **🟢 `p287` (2026-10-18) — region-named leg NIL for a second pass (corporate learning only);
> four directed legs produced FIVE code rows.** 🇮🇳 `avps82/mentar` (AGPL-3.0, 839 files),
> 🇮🇳 `somenssarkar/gurukul-ai` (NCERT/CBSE, no grant), 🇵🇰 `saif2012004/PrepifyAI` (FBISE, 389
> files, no grant), 🇨🇳 `heronerar/OpenGaokao` (source-available by its own text), 🇮🇩
> `sira313/rapkumer` (total commercial prohibition).
> 🔴 **Measured posture: five rows, ONE usable grant, and it is AGPL.** 🔵 **And the distinction
> a regional proposal must not blur:** West Africa's rows are **unlicensed**, which a written

| **China** | **"AI Plus Education" action plan** + the May 2025 *General AI Education Guide* and *Generative AI Use Guide* | Plan **April 2026**, running **to 2030** | **The largest mandated surface in the region.** Dedicated AI courses, integration into local curricula nationwide, national platforms carrying AI courses to rural and remote schools, a **national teacher AI-literacy standard**, and **AI content added to teacher qualification exams**. Curriculum by an expert committee chaired by **Prof. Karthik Raman (IIT Madras)**. Built on an existing base of **18,839 CBSE schools** already offering AI as a skill subject from Class 6. 🔴 **The binding scope is CBSE, not every board or state** — do not brief a national mandate. 🔴 **The constraint is teachers, not curriculum:** MoE itself names orienting **over one crore (10 million) teachers** as the challenge |

FY2026 pilot is **149 municipalities / 478 schools**, split **10** in-class, **100** teacher-administrative, **51** teaching-material. 🔵 **The split is the signal: the funded majority is teacher workload, not student-facing tutoring.** Draft reform targets practical AI skills for **every high-school graduate by 2030** |
| **Singapore** | **MOE Teacher AI Literacy Professional Learning Roadmap**, plus Authoring Copilot and Learning Assistant | Roadmap **Aug 2026**; tools rolled out **Jan 2026**; Module 1 due **end-2026** | **The only jurisdiction here that has made teacher AI training time-bound and compulsory.** Module 1 (basics, ethics, safety, responsible use) **must be completed by end-2026**; Modules 2–3 (age-appropriate use, effects on student thinking, AI for deeper learning) encouraged by **end-2027**. "**Four Learns**" at COS 2026: learn *about*, *to use*, *with*, and *beyond* AI — the last is the stated emphasis. 🔴 **AI tools deliberately withheld from lower primary** to protect foundational skills, and MOE is formally studying over-reliance. Teacher workload measured flat at **~53 h/week**. ⚠️ Whether Modules 2–3 are mandatory **conflicts across sources** |
| **South Korea** | **AI digital textbooks** — and this is the cell a studio must read before any other | Rollout **Mar 2025** → status **revoked Aug 2025** → voluntary; court rulings **Jul and Aug 2026** | 🔴 **The only national AI-in-education programme in this KB that was built, deployed and then UNWOUND.** See below |

#### 🔴 The most decision-relevant APAC fact is a failure, not an adoption curve

**South Korea stripped its AI digital textbooks of official textbook status four months into the
rollout.** The National Assembly revoked the status in **August 2025**, leaving adoption to each
school. The measured consequences:

| | |
|---|---|
| adoption, before → after | 🔴 **37% → 19%** of classes |
| data-protection violations found by the privacy regulator | **2** (May 2025) |
| the state auditor's finding | the textbooks were **brought into schools without a pilot** |
| publisher suits against the reversal | **dismissed**, Seoul Administrative Court, **Jul and Aug 2026** |
| MoE's 2026 posture | voluntary school-by-school adoption; **review process for 2026-year products halted** |
| 🟢 **`p284`: what the state did NEXT** | a **USD 960 million** plan to build **AI talent centres and vocational high schools through 2030**. ⚠️ Secondary-source. 🔵 **Korea did not retreat from AI in education — it moved the money from COURSEWARE to CAPACITY**, which is the single most useful data point in this table for sequencing an engagement |

**The cited causes were not model quality.** They were **factual errors in generated content, privacy
risk, increased screen time, and heavier teacher workload** — every one of them an *implementation and
governance* failure, and every one of them inside a delivery partner's scope.

🟢 **What a studio does with this.** This is the strongest available argument for the **pilot-gated,
teacher-workload-first** sequencing this KB already recommends, and it is now evidence rather than
opinion. Korea is the reference case for three propositions: that **a procurement mandate is not
adoption** (37% → 19% happened *with* the mandate in place), that **a privacy finding can end a
programme faster than any accuracy benchmark**, and that **skipping the pilot is the single
highest-severity programme risk** — the state auditor named exactly that. 🔵 **Pair it with Japan's
funded split** (100 municipalities on teacher administration vs 10 in class): the two jurisdictions
disagree about almost everything except *where to start*, and both start away from the student.

⚠️ **GRADE: SECONDARY-CONVERGENT, PRIMARY UNREAD — all five jurisdictions.** No ministry source was
read first-hand. **`mext.go.jp`, `moe.gov.sg`, `moe.go.kr`, `cbse.gov.in`, `pib.gov.in` and
`www.gov.uk` are all DNS-unresolvable from this sandbox** (`getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`, reproduced); only
`github.com` and `raw.githubusercontent.com` resolve. **This base does not publish a licence it has not
read, and it marks a regulation it could not read either.** `p278` with egress should read, in priority
order: **CBSE Circular Acad-15/2026** (the one binding instrument here), **MEXT Ver. 2.0**, and the
**Korean MoE's own account of the reversal**.

🔴 **One claim was offered and is NOT shelved:** a single wire report of an *expansion to 300 secondary
schools* in Korea, which the ministry has not confirmed and which contradicts the halted review
process. **Left out deliberately.**


**Pass 276 — the tenth consecutive weak APAC leg, and the regional phrasing is again the cause.**
Published with the query that produced it (`AI education APAC 2026 adoption regulation players`), because
an informed gap is information and silence looks exactly like coverage.

🔴 **Zero school- or university-level policy results.** Everything returned was general APAC enterprise
AI: Singapore's financial-sector consultations as a *template* for guardrails, a prediction that APAC
governments will converge on shared principles and localise them, Forrester on CEO ownership of AI
strategy, and "AI sovereignty" as the 2026 regional theme. **None of it is education.**

🟢 **Three named players did come through, and they are the commercial surface:**
- **TCS × Pearson** — a multi-year AI learning alliance aimed at employer skills gaps.
- **LearnUpon** — APAC expansion, Sydney HQ, AI course authoring.
- **OpenAI** — a dedicated **Australia/New Zealand policy lead**, as Canberra tightens AI governance
  and copyright rules.

🔵 **Diagnosis, and the instruction for `p277`: this is a QUERY defect, and `p276` has the proof.** The
EMEA nil ran nine passes and broke on the **first** instrument-targeted query. APAC must be queried the
same way — **by instrument, per jurisdiction** (Japan's MEXT guidelines, Singapore MOE, India's NEP/CBSE
mandate, China's MoE generative-AI rules, Korea's AI textbooks) **rather than by the word "APAC"**, which
no education ministry uses to describe itself.

the instrument that closes it was on this base's own trending shelf the whole time.**

🔴 **This file has carried Vietnam as `UNRECONCILED` since `p269`: `Decision No. 33/2026/QD-TTg`
(compliance **2027-03-01**) against `Decree 142/2026/ND-CP` (effective **2026-04-30**), *"two
instruments, two dates, one jurisdiction and one function"*. `p273`'s backlog gave it an ultimatum:
establish which governs or record it unresolvable and stop spending legs on it.**

🟢 **`p274`'s APAC leg returns a THIRD instrument — and three instruments at three altitudes is not a
contradiction. It is a legislative scheme.**

| altitude | instrument | date | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Law** | **Law on AI** | **in force 2026-03-01** | The enabling statute. **Names education among six high-risk sectors**; National AI Database pre-registration, conformity assessment, human oversight, **72-hour** incident reporting |
| **Decree** | `Decree 142/2026/ND-CP` | effective **2026-04-30** | Implementing instrument under the Law |
| **Decision** | `Decision No. 33/2026/QD-TTg` | issued **July 2026**; compliance **2027-03-01** | The high-risk sector list for education: *automated assessment* and *behavioural monitoring*, **+6 months** for systems live before **2026-08-15** |

🔴 **And the finding behind the finding: this base already held the Law.** `agents/trending.md` has
carried *"Vietnam's Law on AI, in force 2026-03-01, naming education among six high-risk sectors"* —
with the pre-registration, conformity-assessment and 72-hour reporting detail — **in three separate
places**, while this file and `intel/open-gaps.md` declared the reconciliation impossible. 🔵 **The
eighth cross-shelf defect in nine passes, and the species is `T34`'s: a shelf declared something
unestablished that another shelf had established.** The register asked for a search; **a `grep` was
the answer.**

🟢 **The ruling, stated so it is falsifiable:** the **education compliance deadline is `2027-03-01`**
(+6 months for systems operating before `2026-08-15`); the **2026 dates are the framework commencing**,
not the education obligation biting. ⚠️ **Secondary-source grade, and the falsification is named: if
`Decree 142/2026/ND-CP` is shown to impose education obligations of its own from `2026-04-30`, the
hierarchy read fails and the conflict returns.** 🟢 **Until then this base has one Vietnamese
deadline, not two, and may quote it.**

- 🟢 **Japan: the four-pass gap NARROWS, and the reason it was empty may be that Japan is empty by
  choice.** **ABC News (2026-05-31)** characterises Japan's posture as **"AI last, not first"** —
  explicitly contrasted with its neighbours — and **Edmentum** reports Japan moving toward **official
  digital textbooks**. ⚠️ **This is still not an adoption figure and still not a mandate**, so the
  declared gap (*no school-level Japan adoption or policy data*) **stands**. 🔵 **But its
  INTERPRETATION changes.** Four passes read the silence as a measurement failure. **Read beside
  `T22` — Korea mandated AI textbooks, hit under 30% adoption, and the legislature stripped their
  official status — "AI last" looks like a policy choice with a worked example behind it.** For a
  Japanese engagement that is the opposite brief: not *"catch up"*, but *"be the implementation that
  does not get withdrawn."*
- 🟢 **`EdTech Hub` (2026-03-30), on Southeast Asia: education-AI initiatives there "remain
  short-term and project-based."** 🔵 **A named research organisation saying the market is stuck in
  pilots is the clearest productionisation opening on this shelf** — and it is corroborated from the
  other side of the world by `p274`'s trends leg (GovTech/Bridges 2026: few districts and colleges
  have agents in day-to-day operations). **Two independent sources, two regions, one finding: the
  pilots are not becoming operations.**
- 🟡 **Ipsos ban-support reproduced and still not shelved as adoption data** (India **26%**,
  Indonesia **23%** — **down from 30%**, Singapore **28%**, Japan/Malaysia **29%**, Korea **31%**).
  🔵 Indonesia's **7-point fall** is the only movement, and it is in the permissive direction.



**Pass 270 — the APAC regional leg returned thin education material and solid regulatory shape,
and the thinness is recorded rather than dressed up.**

- 🟢 **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026 — the only education-specific APAC source the leg returned, and
  it measures the thing clients actually ask about: public appetite for a ban.** Support for
  **banning AI in schools**: **Indonesia 23%**, **Singapore 28%**, **Japan 29%**, **Malaysia 29%**,
  **South Korea 31%**. 🔵 **Every figure is below one third.** The "parents will not accept it"
  objection is not supported in APAC by this measure, and South Korea — the jurisdiction with this
  base's best-documented *failed* rollout (`T22`) — has the region's **highest** ban support, which
  is consistent: the backlash is against a specific botched deployment, not against AI in school.
- 🟢 **Three jurisdictions, three regulatory postures, none of them hard law for education:**
  **Singapore — voluntary.** Five encouraged principles (transparency, fairness, ethics, human
  oversight, accountability); the **AI Verify** toolkit is **optional**.
  **Japan — promotion-led.** **AI Basic Plan adopted 2025-12-23**; the **2025 AI Promotion Act
  carries no explicit penalties**, with reliance on voluntary guidelines backed by existing law.
  **Indonesia — pending.** **Two AI Presidential Regulations were still unissued as of mid-2026.**
  🔵 **For the studio: in all three, the binding constraints on an education deliverable are
  procurement, data-residency and curriculum-alignment — not an AI statute.** That is the opposite
  of the EMEA posture and should change which questions discovery asks first. *(secondary-source.)*
- ⚠️ **Enterprise scaling, as context rather than education data:** Singapore **56%** and Indonesia
  **51%** of enterprises report progress toward scaled AI adoption — the two regional leaders.
- 🔵 **Zero new named edtech players.** The sovereign-model theme the leg returned — **Sahabat AI**
  (Indonesia), **SEA-LION** (Singapore), **NTT Sarashina** (Japan) — is **already held on this
  base**, and is infrastructure rather than education supply. **An informed nil return.**

**Pass 266 addition — the APAC base is national, and it is MIT.** **Sunbird**
([`Sunbird-Ed/SunbirdEd-portal`](https://github.com/Sunbird-Ed/SunbirdEd-portal), MIT, 38,046
commits) is the stack beneath **DIKSHA**, India's national school platform under NCERT. For an
Indian public-sector engagement this reverses the default approach: the platform, the content
pipeline and the political precedent already exist, so the work is the **agent layer over a
national deployment** (see Pattern G in [`verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md)),
not a migration. It also lands directly on India's **AI-from-Class-3 mandate for AY 2026-27**,
where the delivery vehicle is already Sunbird-based.

**Vietnam is the most specific regulatory design brief in this file.** Its July 2026 decision
names **automated assessment** and **behavioural monitoring** as high-risk education uses, but
only *"where the AI output is the sole basis for a decision without meaningful human review."*
⚠️ (secondary-source grade). **Build the human review in and the system is not high-risk** —
Artemis's human-in-the-loop feedback model and config-gated AI are a direct fit. The deadline is
**2027-03-01**, with six extra months for systems already live before **2026-08-15**, so systems
being designed now are deciding their own classification.

**China constrains product shape, not paperwork:** anthropomorphic-AI rules bar virtual
intimate-relationship services for minors and require **parental consent under 14** ⚠️. A
companion-persona tutor — a common 2026 product instinct — is the wrong shape for that market;
a task-scoped tutor like Iris is not.

The largest mandated demand anywhere, and the thinnest open-source supply.

- **India is the single biggest curriculum event in the world**: AI and computational
  thinking compulsory **from Class 3 in every school from AY 2026-27**. That is a national
  content, teacher-training and assessment problem with a legislated deadline.
  `instructional_agents` (MIT) for materials at multilingual scale; `pykt-toolkit` for
  measuring whether any of it lands.
- **Teacher capacity is the bottleneck, not the models.** India is standing up curriculum
  through IIT Madras; Singapore is training every teacher including trainees by 2026. Teacher
  co-pilots (`Claw-ED`, `instructional_agents`) sell better here than student-facing tutors.
- **China's unevenness is the opportunity.** Beijing mandates 8 hours/year; the national
  rollout varies by province. Provincial-grade delivery and measurement is an unmet need,
  with the obvious caveat that China's AI rules are the region's most binding (registration,
  content controls, localisation).
- **Singapore as the regional reference deployment.** Highest adoption in APAC, a
  government platform (SLS) built on teacher supervision, and voluntary frameworks
  (AI Verify) that make piloting cheap. Win here, cite everywhere.
- **Korea is now the region's compliance market, and the clock is explicit.** The **AI Basic
  Act and its Enforcement Decree are in effect** with a grace period of **at least a year**
  before fines — the only APAC jurisdiction where an EU-style compliance-by-design engagement
  has a statutory hook. Korea is also 4th in regional adoption (40.6%). Pair the EMEA
  compliance playbook with Korean localisation and the offer is ready-made.
- **Vietnam has a national AI law in force** and 27.9% adoption — ahead of Japan. It is absent
  from most APAC education briefs, including this KB's earlier passes.
- **Correction to the supply story:** APAC is **not** supply-poor. The most-adopted education
  agent in open source, `HKUDS/DeepTutor` (41.5k, Apache-2.0), comes from the **Data
  Intelligence Lab at the University of Hong Kong**, and the learner-modelling layer below it
  is substantially Chinese-academic (`bigdata-ustc/EduData`, `HFUT-LEC/EduStudio`). What APAC
  has not produced is a **platform**. The engagement shape is therefore *integration of
  APAC-origin agents onto non-APAC platforms* — and in a Hong Kong, Singapore or mainland
  pitch, the headline component being local research is an asset, not a footnote.
- **Gap, stated plainly:** no APAC-origin open-source education **platform** surfaced. The
  earlier blanket claim ("no platform *or agent*") was wrong and is retracted in
  [`agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md).

**Pass 267 — demand and supply are now both verified in India, and Korea shows the failure mode.**

- 🟢 **India's mandate has a date, a scope and a budget line.** AI and computational thinking become
  compulsory from **Class 3**; the CT&AI curriculum for Classes 3–8 was **launched 2026-04-01** by
  the Education Minister, implementation begins in the **2026–27 session**, Classes 9–10 follow in
  **2027–28**, teacher training runs through **NISHTHA**, and a **₹500 crore Centre of Excellence
  in AI for Education** was announced in the 2025–26 budget. ⚠️ Secondary-source (news and
  education-sector coverage); dates are decision-usable, figures directional.
- 🟢 **And the delivery platform for that mandate is MIT.** Sunbird/DIKSHA
  ([`Sunbird-Ed/SunbirdEd-portal`](https://github.com/Sunbird-Ed/SunbirdEd-portal), **38,046
  commits**, re-measured and reproduced exactly this pass) is the national stack under NCERT.
  **This is the only region in this file where a verified mandate, a named teacher-training
  programme and a permissively-licensed national platform all line up** — see pattern **P16**. The
  engagement is curriculum-to-classroom delivery *on the client's own stack*, with no platform
  displacement and no copyleft.
- 🔴 **Korea is the cautionary case, and it is recent.** Seoul mandated AI digital textbooks from
  2025; adoption sat **below 30%** by March and in **August 2025 the National Assembly stripped
  them of official textbook status** after unions argued the pace had outrun teacher preparation.
  **A mandate that lands on content distribution without teacher readiness can be withdrawn by the
  legislature.** Lead APAC mandate conversations with enablement, not content volume.
- 🟡 **China:** one secondary source reports AI as a compulsory subject from age six as of September
  2025, and names China and the UAE as the only countries running compulsory national AI curricula
  since 2025-26. ⚠️ **Unconfirmed against any primary source** — carried as a lead, not a fact. The
  minors' constraints this base already records (no virtual intimate-relationship services for
  minors; parental consent under 14) remain the binding design limit on companion-persona tutors.
- ⚠️ **Japan: the gap persists and the instrument is now named.** No MEXT generative-AI guidance for
  schools surfaced in either the regional leg or a targeted ministry-level search. This is the third
  consecutive pass to record it. Primary-source reading is **not possible from this environment**
  (no non-GitHub host resolves by DNS on either instrument), so this gap cannot be closed here —
  it needs a session with open egress, not another search.


#### Pass 269 — Korea's statute is in force and effectively unenforced, which changes the deadline story

🟢 This base carries South Korea's **AI Basic Act, in force 22 January 2026**, as the one APAC
jurisdiction with a statute actually operating. Pass 269's APAC leg sharpens it: **MSIT has signalled
that 2026 functions as a pilot period with limited enforcement, with a one-year grace on penalties**
*(⚠️ secondary-source grade)*.

🔵 **Why that matters commercially rather than legally.** Korea is already this base's documented
**failure case** on the demand side — AI digital textbooks mandated from 2025, **under 30%
adoption**, and in **August 2025 the National Assembly stripped them of official textbook status**
(`T22`). Put the two together and the picture is a jurisdiction where **the obligation is real, the
enforcement is deferred, and the last mandate failed on adoption rather than on compliance.** 🟢 **So
a Korean engagement should be scoped on adoption risk, not on penalty risk** — teacher workflow,
change management and measured uptake — and a proposal that leads with compliance is answering the
question the client has already learned is not the binding one.


#### Pass 271 — Vietnam's second instrument is corroborated from a second channel, and the reconciliation is still open

🟢 **`p269` flagged Vietnam as carrying TWO instruments and refused to merge them. Pass 271 found a
second, independent description of one of them — and it matches the base to the day:**

> a **July 2026** decision lists education, including **automated assessment** and **behavioural
> monitoring**, among high-risk sectors; those systems must comply by **1 March 2027**, except those in
> healthcare, education and finance **already in operation before 15 August 2026**, which get an
> additional **six-month grace period**

🔵 **That is `Decision No. 33/2026/QD-TTg` as this base already records it**, and it adds one datum the
base did not have: **the decision's issuance month (July 2026)**. 🔴 **It does NOT reconcile anything.**
The other instrument — `Decree 142/2026/ND-CP`, effective **2026-04-30**, under which this base grades
Vietnam `GATED` — is untouched by this leg. **A second source for one of two conflicting instruments is
not a ruling on which governs.** The `UNRECONCILED` flag stands; **do not quote either Vietnamese
deadline alone.**

🟢 **The APAC market figure is new to this base, and it is derived rather than published** — **~$2.85B
(2026)**, the residual of the ResourcERA regional decomposition, corroborated to within 2.5% by Grand
View's **~$2.28B (2025) @ 28.1%**. Grand View's regional horizon is **~$18.2B by 2033**. SNS Insider
gives **31.33%** and Mordor **44.20%** for the region — a 16-point CAGR spread, so **quote the baseline.**

🔵 **No new APAC players were shelved.** A paid-report marketing page named Google, Microsoft, IBM,
Pearson and Byju's; that is a vendor list behind a paywall, not a market map, and it was refused.
Korea's **AI Framework Act (eff. 2026-01-22)** and Taiwan's **AI Basic Act (Dec 2025)** were already held.

#### Pass 279 — the hardest mandate in the region was LEGISLATED AWAY, and the nearest deadline is a teacher-training one

🔴 **Korea — a mandate reversal, and a cautionary tale worth more than an adoption curve.** See `T62`.
AI digital textbooks were **reclassified from "textbooks" to "educational materials"** by amendment to
the Elementary and Secondary Education Act (reported **August 2025**), which **removes the adoption
mandate**. Korean and home economics are excluded, social studies and science are **delayed to 2027**,
the review for 2026-year products was **halted**, and **~10 publishers sued the government in June
2026 for roughly KRW 200bn**. 🟢 **Procurement thesis: price against what a school may CHOOSE, never
against what a ministry has ANNOUNCED. In Korea the buyer is now the school and the district.**

🟢 **Singapore — the nearest dated obligation anywhere on these shelves.** See `T63`. **Every**
primary, secondary and JC teacher must complete MOE AI modules by **end-2027**, with **module 1 due by
the end of 2026**. 🔴 **The student channel is closed** — delivery runs on MOE's own tools on the
Singapore Student Learning Space, from **Primary 4** only, with P1–P3 deliberately print-first. 🟢 **So
the opportunity is teacher enablement, completion evidence and institutional integration**, not a
classroom product.

🟢 **India — dated, scoped, and cross-curricular.** See `T64`. **Circular Acad-15/2026 (2026-04-01)**,
Classes **III–VIII**, session **2026-27**, with **Acad-18/2026** covering resource books and a stated
aim of *"AI-literate learners by 2030"*. 🔴 **AI is NOT a standalone subject** — computational thinking
is woven into maths, science, languages and social studies, reportedly with **no board exam**. 🟢 The
sellable artefact is **cross-curricular material, teacher enablement and project assessment**.

🟡 **Japan — a date correction.** MEXT's Ver. 2.0 guideline is dated **2024-12-26**, not 2026: the PDF
filename carries `20260324` while the document itself shows 2024. A revision is underway and
**unconfirmed as published**. 🟢 **DX High School adopted 1,249 schools in FY2026** — a funding
programme, not a rule.

🟡 **Region-wide, for sizing only:** APAC ~**$591.6M** (2024) at a reported **20.9% CAGR** to 2029,
described by two houses as the fastest-growing region. 🔴 **Regulation remains genuinely fragmented** —
Singapore has mature responsible-AI guidance, China legislates against algorithmic misconduct, the
ASEAN AI governance guide is early-stage — and **none of that is education-specific**.

🔵 **An instructive nil, recorded rather than hidden:** the mandated region-wide APAC query named **no
education-specific ministry, policy or vendor at all**. Four jurisdiction-targeted retries produced
four dated instruments. **`T53` demonstrated a third time: the region query's silence is the query's,
not the region's.**

structure, not classrooms. An informed gap: this region's *rules* are legible and its

🔵 **The opportunity, placed:** Vietnam's grace structure is a **dated delivery window** —

### LATAM

appears as a licence holder on any grant these shelves carry.** 🟢 **What `p298` adds for LATAM is not
a market datum but a tool: the integration boundary is now permissive in six runtimes** (see

#### 🟢 `p297` · `2026-10-18` — the strongest leg AGAIN, and the governance gap is now measured by TWO independent instruments that must not be blended

🟢 **LATAM out-returned all three other regions for the eighth time, and it did so with named
instruments rather than vendor estimates.**

**Adoption — two surveys, both large:**

- **Digital Education Council, *AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026*:** **92% of students** and
  **79% of faculty** actively engaging with AI, from **30 000+ responses across 29 institutions**, run
  with **Tecnológico de Monterrey** and its **Institute for the Future of Education**.
- **UNESCO IESALC + UNU-IAS working paper:** **200 institutions across 19 countries**, with use split
  **teaching and learning 73.5% · research 57.0% · administration 34.1% · community engagement 20.0%**.

🔴 **Governance — and here `p297` makes a correction to how this file reports the gap.** This leg
returned *"only **30%** of universities in Latin America have published AI policies."* This base
already carries IESALC's *"**26%** with a strategy"* (`p281`, `p293`). 🔵 **These are TWO DIFFERENT
INSTRUMENTS measuring two different things — a *published policy* and a *strategy* — and they must
not be averaged, merged, or quoted as a range as if one number had moved.** They agree on the order of
magnitude, which is the claim that survives: **adoption is near-universal and governance sits around
a quarter to a third.** That is the gap, and it is now **independently double-sourced** rather than
doubled by restatement.

🟡 **Regulation remains heterogeneous, with no unified regional framework:**

| country | instrument | binding? |
|---|---|---|
| **Chile** | **National AI Policy since 2021**, AI law **under discussion** | 🟢 most advanced in the region |
| **Brazil** | national AI strategy, **no education-sector rules** | 🟡 |
| **Colombia** | national AI strategy, **no education-sector rules** | 🟡 |
| **Mexico** | **SEP**, **ANUIES** and **Observatorio IA** recommendations | 🔴 **explicitly lack binding force** |

🔵 **Institutions and instruments to name in a LATAM proposal:** **Tec de Monterrey**, **UNAM** (its
own AI-in-education project), **UPC Peru** (**weekly** teacher training — the only named recurring
cadence in the region), **Pontificia Universidad Católica de Chile**; the **IDB**'s study on
regulatory models for the region; and the **ILIA 2025** index, produced with **CEPAL**, which tracks

⚠️ **Channel limitation, stated because a silent one reads as a clean run.** `unu.edu` remains

#### 🔴 `p296` · `2026-10-18` — 87% ADOPTION AGAINST 26% STRATEGY: the region's engagement is GOVERNANCE RETROFIT, measured on 200 institutions

🟢 **The strongest primary instrument this leg has ever returned for this region.** A **UNESCO
IESALC** study (with UNU-IAS) of **200 higher-education institutions across 19 countries** finds:

| measurement | value | what it means |
|---|---|---|
| institutions using AI in **at least one area** | 🔴 **87%** | adoption is already done; selling adoption is selling into a solved problem |
| institutions with a **formal AI strategy** | 🔴 **26%** | **a 61-point gap** |
| what drives adoption | **individual academics, researchers and students** | 🔴 **shadow AI at institutional scale** — the deployment exists and nobody owns it |

🔵 **This single pair of numbers is the LATAM engagement thesis, and it is the opposite of the
pitch used elsewhere.** In a region where 87% have already adopted and 74% have no strategy, the
billable work is **not** a tutoring pilot — it is **inventory, policy, data boundaries and an
evaluation regime wrapped around AI use that is already happening**. `P13` (governance retrofit for a
LATAM university that already adopted AI) is the pattern, and this study is now its evidence base.
UNESCO's own framing agrees: *governance frameworks and institutional strategies are struggling to
keep pace.*

**Governance instruments, by country.**

| country | instrument | note |
|---|---|---|
| **Uruguay** | 🟢 **first in Latin America to sign the Council of Europe Framework Convention on AI** (2025) | the one country with a binding external commitment to point at |
| **Colombia** | **CONPES 4144** (Feb 2025) | policy-first route, no statute |
| **Mexico** | federal AI bill proposed in the Senate (2024) | still a bill |
| regional pattern | lawmakers converging on **risk-based taxonomies** | i.e. EU-shaped, which makes EU-conformant design reusable here |

**Institutions to work with, not around.** **UNESCO launched the Observatory on AI in Education for
Latin America and the Caribbean on 14 April 2026 in Santiago, Chile**, supporting policy, teacher
training and pilots — including one in **Mexico with CONALEP and DGETI**. The **Digital Education
Council** published a **2026 LATAM survey with 30 000+ responses across 29 institutions**. **OEI and
ProFuturo** have published regional reports.

🟢 **The region gained its first permissive *evaluation* instrument this pass:**
[`AI-for-Education/pedagogy-benchmark`](https://github.com/AI-for-Education/pedagogy-benchmark) —
**MIT**, benchmarks of pedagogical knowledge built from **teacher-qualification exams supplied by
Chile's Ministry of Education** (*Agencia de la Calidad de la Educación* and **CPEIP**),
arXiv:2506.18710. 🔵 **A governance retrofit needs a measurement to put in the policy, and this is a
LATAM-sourced one.** ⚠️ **5 commits — a benchmark release, not a maintained harness**, and scoring
well on Chilean certification items is not evidence of teaching a Chilean child well.

🔴 **The declared gap, so the silence is informed.** **School-level (K-12) adoption is not covered by
the UNESCO study**, which is higher-education only. The 2025 **Latin American AI Index (ILIA)** finds
school-level AI education and postgraduate programmes **exist but remain exceptions**. An older
OEI/ProFuturo report describing the region as *"very limited"* **predates this data and should not be
cited as current.** 🔴 **No LATAM education-specific AI regulation was found** — education sits inside
general AI and data-protection frameworks. ⚠️ Sources give **inconsistent dates** for the UNESCO study
and Observatory launch; most are press releases. Read the primary reports before quoting figures.

#### 🟡 `p295` (`2026-10-18`) — the strongest mandated regional leg again, and a vendor property that contradicts itself

across 19 LAC countries**, surveyed late 2025 — remains the best single source on this shelf.
🔴 `unu.edu` is **`EXISTENCE-UNMEASURED`** from this session (see the DNS control below), so it stays

🟢 **New this pass:** the regional AI index **ILIA 2025** carries a **regulation subdimension** inside
its governance section — the first named regional *regulatory* instrument this base has placed in
LATAM rather than inferred.

🔴 **New, and recorded as a DEFECT against a named property:** `marketsandmarkets`' *"Rest of Latin
America"* is **internally inconsistent** — **$18.2 M (2024) → $36 M (2029)** in prose against a

🔵 **The structural read, unchanged and worth repeating to a client:** regional commentary describes

(**719★ / 550 forks**, GPL-2.0, on the federal Portal do Software Público since 2008) over anything
that needs a ministry to stay in office.

🟢 **New, and it is the most citable LATAM source on this shelf:** a **UNESCO IESALC + UNU-IAS**

down.

supply gap is therefore **unchanged, not closed**: strong demand, published policy, **no
national code channel**.
