#### 🟢 Pass 288 — the strongest PRIMARY source of the four regions, and it says adoption is INDIVIDUAL while governance is absent

🟢 **UNESCO IESALC + UNU-IAS surveyed 200 institutions across 19 countries** — the best-sourced
regional datum on this shelf this pass, and institutional rather than vendor-supplied:

- **87%** of institutions use AI in **at least one area** of their activity.
- 🔴 **Only 26%** have a **formal AI strategy**.
- **74%** use it in **teaching and learning**.
- 🔵 **Adoption is driven by individual academics, researchers and students — not by
  institution-wide strategy.**

🟢 **Institutional scaffolding is arriving:** UNESCO launched the **Observatory on AI in
Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** on **`2026-04-14` at ECLAC, Santiago**, with a
remit covering ethical and regulatory framework development and the training of teachers and
public policymakers. The **Digital Education Council**'s LATAM higher-education survey gathered
**30 000+ responses across 29 institutions**. Mexico runs a **UNESCO pilot with CONALEP and
DGETI** to co-build Centres of Excellence.

🟡 **Regulation is general AI law, not education law.** **Uruguay** was first in the region to
sign the Council of Europe's AI framework convention (2025); **Colombia** adopted **CONPES
4144** (Feb 2025); **Mexico**'s federal bill mirrors the risk-based approach and proposes an
oversight authority; **Peru**'s law adds **regulatory sandboxes** with a phased schedule for
smaller firms. 🔴 **This leg found NO 2026 instrument specific to AI in schools or
universities.** At school level the ILIA index reports progress but says the gains **"remain
exceptions"**.

🟢 **The opportunity, placed, and it follows directly from the 87/26 split:** the regional
bottleneck is **not adoption and not tooling — it is institutional governance**. The sellable
unit is the **AI strategy and governance layer** wrapped around usage that is *already
happening bottom-up*: inventory what individual academics already run, place the human decision
points, produce the policy artefacts. 🔵 **74% already use it in teaching and learning, so a
governance engagement is a retrofit, never a greenfield** — and because Mexico's bill and
Peru's sandboxes point at a risk-based regime, that retrofit is the thing that will be audited.

🟡 **A code-level LATAM note from this pass's verified rows:** the only Spanish-language
artefact found was **inside a US-domestic repository** —
[`hdronamraju/sped-agent`](https://github.com/hdronamraju/sped-agent)'s
`src/agent/notices.ts` generates **bilingual EN/es statutory parent notices** (full
`ES_DAYS`/`ES_MONTHS` tables, *"de {hora} a {hora}"* time phrasing) for California IEP
meetings. 🔴 It is **all rights reserved**, so it is a specification and not a dependency — but
it is the clearest existing model of Spanish-language regulated-notice generation this base has
located, and that capability is reusable far beyond California.

> **🟢 `p287` (2026-10-18) — independently re-derived, and it CORROBORATES `p286`.** Digital
> Education Council LATAM 2026: **92 % of students, 79 % of faculty**, 30 000+ responses, 29
> institutions. UNESCO IESALC, **200 institutions / 19 countries**, gives the shape: teaching
> and learning **73.5 %**, research **57.0 %**, administration **34.1 %**, community engagement
> **20.0 %** — against **~30 %** of universities with a published AI policy.
> 🔵 **The engagement read is in the split, not the headline: administration at 34.1 % against
> teaching at 73.5 % makes the back office the UNDER-SERVED half — and it is also the half
> whose deliverable is NOT bounded by a curriculum licence (`T-p287a`). The quiet LATAM

> 🟡 Regulation re-confirmed: no binding regional framework; **Chile** leads (National AI Policy

**Pass 280 — the ranked homologation watch MOVED, and the text that becomes binding is not the text
this base has read.** 🟢 **LATAM was the one region whose region-wide query returned real material**:
a **UNESCO IESALC × UNU-IAS working paper dated September 2026**, surveying **200 higher-education
institutions across 19 countries** (fieldwork late 2025) on teaching, research, administration and
governance — the largest institution-level instrument on this shelf for the region — alongside
**ILIA 2025** with **CEPAL** and a 2025 comparative study of HE AI rules in **Argentina, Brazil,
Chile, Peru and Spain**.

| Brazil — `Parecer CNE/CP nº 15/2026` | state as of `2026-10-18` |
|---|---|
| approved by **CNE** | **`2026-09-01`** |
| DOU summary published | **`2026-10-06`** |
| 🔴 **homologated?** | 🔴 **NO — not in any source found.** Under **art. 2 of Lei nº 9.131/1995 it has no normative force until the homologating act is published** |
| 🟢 **reported imminent, WITH CHANGES** | **`2026-10-13`** reporting: minister **Leonardo Barchini** would homologate that week, and one amendment **bars AI tools from determining a pupil's approval or failure — marks are given only by teachers** |
| 🟢 **`p284`: the OTHER half of that amendment, and it runs the other way** | **AI in essay/dissertation correction is PERMITTED as a `primeiro filtro` that assists the teacher.** The CNE's September text **banned it outright**; MEC changed that before homologation, judging a total ban *"impedia um recurso que facilita a rotina docente sem substituir o professor"*. 🔵 So the rule is **not** *"no AI in grading"* — it is **assistive grading allowed, the MARK stays human**. Two outlets, `2026-10-13`/`2026-10-14` |
| 🔴 **`p284`: `educação infantil`** | **AI use TOTALLY FORBIDDEN** at the early-childhood stage; later stages carry **progressive** rules |
| 🔴 **`p284`: the window does NOT defer the bans** | institutions get **one year to adapt**, 🔴 **but the prohibitions apply from PUBLICATION of the parecer**. A delivery plan that reads *"12 months"* and ships an autonomous grader in month three ships into a prohibition already in force |
| 🟢 **new substance** | the text **classifies AI uses by RISK LEVEL**, gives institutions a **12-month adequacy window from homologation**, and **prohibits autonomous AI use up to the 5th year** on UNESCO's **age-13** parameter |
| official text | `gov.br/mec/pt-br/cne/2026/setembro-2026/pcp015_26.pdf` *(not read — `T61`)* |

🔴 **The engagement consequence is sharper than "watch it".** The amendments mean the binding text
**differs from the approved one**, so any curriculum or assessment mapping built against the
September text has to be re-checked on publication — and the **12-month clock starts at that
publication**, which makes the homologating act a **dated engagement trigger** rather than news.

| Colombia — `Ley 2626 de 2026` | this pass |
|---|---|
| 🟢 **scope, which `p279` ranked for an egress-enabled run** | 🟢 **RESOLVED from a secondary source:** it modernises the **Tecnología e Informática** subject, phasing in **computational thinking, programming, AI, data science and digital citizenship**. **Binding on official public institutions; private establishments MAY adopt the curricular model** — so a private-school client has **no statutory obligation**, which is the commercially decisive half |
| 🟡 **a contested attribution** | this shelf says the *lineamientos* are owed by **MEN jointly with MinTIC and MinCiencias**; this pass's source names **MEN with a `Comisión Nacional de Educación Digital`**, which must review curricular guidelines, competency standards and pedagogical orientations **at least every three years**. 🔴 **Both secondary. Recorded as a conflict, not resolved** |
| 🟢 **new** | conscience-freedom for pupils and teachers, and the **parents' preferential right to choose** their children's education, are written into the statute |
| 🔴 **a pending amendment to the statute itself** | **`PL 418/2026`** proposes adding an **`art. 3A`** on the ethical and responsible use of AI and social media; **`PL 568/2026`** would make digital transformation and AI **transversal axes** of education. 🔴 **Projects, not law** — but the instrument this base calls LATAM's first binding one **has a moving spec** |
| 🔵 **the discrimination worth keeping** | a September 2026 source stating *"Colombia has no approved AI law"* is about **general** AI regulation and **says nothing about `Ley 2626`**. Two different instruments; conflating them loses the one statute that binds |

🔵 **The regional motion is now dated end-to-end:** Colombia has a **statute without guidance**
(implementation engagement, no spec), Brazil has **guidance about to become a rule with a 12-month
clock**, and the UNESCO IESALC survey gives the first region-wide institutional baseline to sell
governance against.


**Pass 278 — the region was carried on ONE regional query for 277 passes, and five jurisdictions
answer with NAMED, DATED instruments the moment they are asked separately.** `p275` diagnosed the
EMEA blanks as *"narrow-query blanks, not quiet regions"*; `p277` ended a ten-pass APAC nil by
querying **MEXT, CBSE, China's MoE, Singapore's MOE and Korea's MoE** instead of the word *"APAC"*.
`p277` then ranked the same move for LATAM and North America (`T53`). 🟢 **Run once, it returned the
first BINDING LATAM education-AI statute on this shelf.**

| jurisdiction | instrument | binding? | what it forces |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **Colombia** | **Ley 2626 de 2026** (*Ley de Educación Digital*), sanctioned **Aug 2026** | 🔴 **YES — a statute** | Phases **computational thinking, programming, AI, data science and digital citizenship** into basic and secondary education. **Binding on official PUBLIC schools; private establishments MAY adopt the curricular model.** MEN must act jointly with **MinTIC** and **MinCiencias**, so implementing *lineamientos* are **still owed** — the law exists, its guidance does not |
| **Brazil** | MEC orientador ***"Inteligência Artificial na Educação Básica"***, launched **8–9 Apr 2026** | 🟡 orientation | **7 diretrizes and 12 aprendizagens** for teaching *about* AI, with the **BNCC as the stated starting point**. 🔴 **LGPD (Lei 13.709/2018) and the ECA Digital (Lei 15.211/2025) are transversal and MANDATORY** — the data-protection floor is statutory even though the pedagogy is guidance. Teacher course *"IA na prática docente"* on **Plataforma Mais Professores**, under the **Enec** strategy |
| **Mexico** | **SEP's 10 recommendations** for ethical and critical generative-AI use, **15 Apr 2026** | 🟡 recommendations, **HE-scoped** | Derived from the **Encuesta Nacional sobre IAG en educación superior** — applied to **1.5M students and 166k+ teachers**, with **>60% of both** reporting daily use. Asks institutions for their own *lineamientos*, teacher training, curriculum updates and **rethought assessment**. A national HE AI policy is **being designed, not issued**. Basic education: a **consultation opened Jul 2026** on phones, technology and AI; **>1M teachers** named as the constraint |
| **Chile** | MINEDUC guide ***"PotencIA el Aprendizaje"*** (**Mar 2025**), after the **2023 ChatGPT guide** | 🟡 guide | Generative AI for teachers with a critical/ethical/creative framing, **distributed to every establishment in the country**. A **Plan de Formación en IA en Educación** runs with **Fundación Chile** and **CENIA**. 🔴 **UNESCO's country profile states there is NO law or policy specific to AI in the education SYSTEM** — both halves are shelved, because a studio needs to know the guide exists *and* that nothing binds |
| **Argentina** | **Paideia** (via **educ.ar**) + a **Permanent Observatory of AI in Education** approved by the **Consejo Federal de Educación** | 🟡 programme | An official AI-in-education programme with computational-thinking and age-staged AI objectives; the Observatory's remit is **monitoring impact and developing ethical/regulatory frameworks**. **CABA** published a critical-AI-use guide for schools in **2025** |
| **Peru** | **Minedu** rural AI deployment for **2026** | 🟡 programme | AI for learning planning across **205 rural schools / 27,000 students** |

🟢 **What this changes for an engagement.** The regional story this shelf has told — *"adoption has
outrun governance"*, 87% using AI against 26% with a strategy — **is still true at the university
layer, and is now wrong at the school layer in Colombia**, where a statute arrived ahead of the
guidance. 🔵 **That inverts the sales motion in exactly one jurisdiction:** in Colombia the question
a client asks is *"how do we comply with Ley 2626 before MEN publishes the lineamientos"*, which is
an implementation engagement with a deadline attached. Everywhere else in the region the question is
still *"we have adopted, help us govern it"*.

🔴 **GRADE: SECONDARY-CONVERGENT, PRIMARY UNREAD — and the reason is now MEASURED rather than
assumed.** Primary addresses were located for Brazil (`gov.br` MEC PDF), Mexico
(`educacionsuperior.sep.gob.mx`) and Colombia (`mineducacion.gov.co`, `mintic.gov.co`) and **none is
reachable from this sandbox**: the policy hosts are DNS-unresolvable or proxy-403 while
`raw.githubusercontent.com`, `git ls-remote` and `registry.npmjs.org` all answer.
**Full map:** [`egress-map.2026-10-17-p278.tsv`](../evidence/egress-map.2026-10-17-p278.tsv).
⚠️ **One structural caveat, published rather than smoothed:** Brazilian sources **disagree on the
MEC document's own structure** (one says four pillars, another five chapters), so the structure is
**not** shelved — only the two statutes and the curriculum counts are.
⚠️ **And Chile was nearly published as a blank:** the combined `Chile … Argentina` query returned
Argentina and Peru and nothing on Chile. A **jurisdiction-targeted retry** found two ministerial

[`p278-websearch.2026-10-17-p278.tsv`](../evidence/p278-websearch.2026-10-17-p278.tsv), legs 1–5.


**Pass 277 — the direct instrument gains its denominator, and the teacher layer is where LATAM leads
the OECD.** `p275` and `p276` established that UNESCO-IESALC (200 institutions, 19 countries) is the
instrument to quote for regional university governance, and corrected a *perception* figure that had
been standing in for a policy count. This leg adds the number that makes the governance gap legible:

| UNESCO-IESALC (2026), regional universities | |
|---|---|
| **already use AI** | 🟢 **87%** |
| have a **formal strategy** | 🔴 **26%** |
| **evaluate** implementation | 🔴 **9%** |

🔵 **Stated as one sentence: 87% are doing it, 26% have decided how, and 9% check whether it worked.**
That is the engagement, and the 9% is the part a studio is uniquely able to sell — evaluation is
billable, unclaimed, and the precondition for every renewal. Student-side: the **Digital Education
Council** reports **92% of students across 29 regional institutions** using AI.

🟢 **And the schools layer, where LATAM is ahead of the OECD rather than behind it.** Secondary teachers
reporting AI use in the prior year: **Brazil 56%**, **Colombia 53%**, against an **OECD average of
36%**. 🔴 **But only 22% of Brazilian teachers report that their school has guidelines on its use.**
**The same shape as higher education, one level down: adoption without governance** — and the gap is
wider in schools than in universities.

**Named instruments and money, placed:**

- **Colombia — CONPES 4144 (February 2025)**, a national AI policy adopted *before* a national AI law.
  🔵 **The inverse of Brazil's order** and the reason Colombia is the easier public-sector entry: there
  is a document to comply with.
- **Brazil — PL 2.338/2023** passed the **Senate on 10 December 2024**, carrying transparency and
  supervisory provisions. ⚠️ **Whether it has since become law was NOT established this pass** — do not
  brief it as enacted.
- **Mexico — UNESCO × CONALEP × DGETI** pilot to co-build a model of centres of excellence, **18 August
  2026**. 🔵 **Technical and vocational education**, which is the under-served segment in this KB's
  LATAM coverage and the one closest to employability outcomes.
- **Google.org — $4.6M** to scale Raspberry Pi's **Experience AI** across **Argentina, Brazil, Chile,
  Colombia, the Dominican Republic, El Salvador, Mexico, Peru and Uruguay**, targeting **1.25M students
  by 2028**. 🔵 **Nine countries, one curriculum, external funding already committed** — the clearest
  partner-adjacent surface on this shelf.

🔴 **The regional verdict this shelf should carry verbatim, because it is the counter-argument an
engagement will meet.** The ProFuturo/OEI study concludes the landscape *"reveals more an intention than
a policy"* — uncoordinated actors, unsustained efforts, and developments **still far from rural
classrooms or vulnerable contexts**. 🔵 **Read with Korea's reversal above, that is the same warning
from the opposite hemisphere:** the programmes that fail are not the ones with weak models, they are the
ones with no delivery capacity underneath.

⚠️ **GRADE: SECONDARY-CONVERGENT.** UNESCO, IADB and ProFuturo/OEI agree and are independent of each
other; none was read at primary source here. The **56% / 53% / 36%** teacher figures are a **2024
vintage** — quote them with the year attached.


**Pass 276 — the strongest leg again, and it corrects the predicate under this shelf's own headline
governance number.**

🔴 **The "~30% of LATAM universities have published AI-use policies" figure is a PERCEPTION measure, not
a policy-coverage measure.** The Digital Education Council / Tec de Monterrey LATAM survey reports that
**only ~30% of students and faculty consider that their university integrates AI effectively or provides
sufficient resources to use it well.** 🔵 **That is a satisfaction question. It is not a count of
published policies**, and the two are not interchangeable: an institution can have a policy nobody rates
as effective, and vice versa. **Retired as a policy figure.**

🟢 **The policy question has a direct instrument, and it is already on this shelf — UNESCO-IESALC, 200
institutions across 19 countries (2025):**

| predicate | value |
|---|---|
| teaching and learning use | **73.5%** |
| has a **formal AI strategy** | **26.0%** |
| has **institution-wide policies** | 🔴 **18.5%** |
| has a **dedicated budget** | 🔴 **8.0%** |
| has **formal evaluation mechanisms** | 🔴 **9.0%** |

🔴 **So this base has carried three different governance gaps, and they are three different questions:**
92 − 30 = **62 points** (use vs *perceived effectiveness*), 92 − 18.5 = **73.5 points** (use vs
*institution-wide policy*), and `p275`'s *"fewer than 10%"* ≈ **83–84 points** (use vs *policy **and**
capacity*, which matches IESALC's 8.0% budget and 9.0% evaluation). 🔵 **All three are real; none is
interchangeable with the others.** **An engagement should quote the 18.5% institution-wide-policy figure
for a governance-retrofit pitch** — it is the direct measure of the thing being sold — and cite the
8.0%/9.0% pair when the pitch is *capacity* rather than *policy*.

🟢 **Regulatory map, newly sourced and placed:** Universidad de los Andes' observatory documents **716
regulatory instruments — binding norms and draft bills — across 23 countries and territories** in the
region. **Peru** has a general AI law (**Ley N.º 31814**, 2023). **Chile** has had a national AI policy
since **2021** with a general law still in discussion. **Brazil** and **Colombia** have national
strategies but **no education-sector AI regulation**. 🔴 **Mexico's SEP and ANUIES proposals are
explicitly NON-BINDING.** 🔵 **The commercial consequence: in LATAM the binding constraint is almost
never a national education-AI statute — it is institutional policy and data-protection law.** An
engagement that waits for sector regulation waits indefinitely; one that lands institutional governance
can start now.

⚠️ **Two quantities on this shelf are UNRECONCILED and must not be quoted until they are.** This file
records the DEC LATAM survey as **"92% of students and 79% of faculty, 30,000+ responses across 29
institutions"**; this pass's sourcing gives the LATAM survey as **29 institutions / 22,941 students**,
and the **GLOBAL** 2026 report as **88% of students and 77% of faculty, 45,398 responses across 35
countries**. 🟢 **The 92% is confirmed LATAM — `p275`'s regional attribution was RIGHT** — but the
**faculty percentage and the response count are not yet reconciled**, and `digitaleducationcouncil.com`
is CONNECT 403 from here.

🔴 **And a conflation to defend against, found in the wild this pass:** an aggregator publishes the 92%
as **global** student AI use ("66% in 2024 → 92% in 2025"). **It is a LATAM figure**, and the global
number is 88%. 🔵 **DEC itself invites the error** by comparing its LATAM 92% against its own
**global-2024** 86% as a *"+6% increase"* — a cross-base comparison, `T38`/`T42`'s species appearing in
a primary publisher rather than a reseller. `T51`.

**Pass 270 — the Linux Foundation LATAM datum is discharged, and the register's NAME for it does
not exist.**

- 🔴 **There is no "Linux Foundation LATAM AI workforce report".** Cited as the register wrote it,

  employers plan to upskill their own workforce** to meet tech-talent demand, and **8–14% of Latin
  American jobs could see significant productivity increases through AI augmentation**.
  🔵 **Both numbers point the same way and it is the way a studio wants:** the regional constraint
  is **skills**, not jobs, and employers have already decided to pay for upskilling. **That is a
  budget line that exists before the engagement does.**
  ⚠️ **Do not conflate it with the *2026 State of Tech Talent Report*, which is GLOBAL**
  (n=**400** hiring/training IT leaders), not LATAM-specific. Its capability-gap ranking — **AI
  security and risk management 57%, AI operations and monitoring 57%, cost optimisation 54%, AI
  infrastructure expertise 45%** — is frequently quoted as though it were regional. It is not.
- 🟢 **The academic finding this base did not have: LATAM higher education has no unified AI
  framework, and universities are deciding in isolation.** A Universidad Autónoma de Madrid
  analysis of the Latin American context finds **no unified regulatory framework for AI in higher
  education**, with **most universities operating without clear internal rules** and making
  isolated decisions. 🔵 **This is the institution-level inverse of the school-level pattern** this
  file already documents for LATAM, and it is directly monetisable: **the first deliverable for a
  LATAM university client is frequently the policy and the governance scaffold, not the tutor.**
- 🟢 **National instruments, as the leg returned them:** **Colombia** adopted its **Política
  Nacional de IA in February 2025**, six objectives spanning ethical governance through
  digital-talent formation. **Brazil's `PL 2338/2023`** was **approved by the Senate in December
  2024** and **remains pending in the Câmara dos Deputados** — still not law; the **ILIA** regional
  index nonetheless rates Brazil the region's **governance leader**. **Chile's** national AI policy
  dates to **2021** and its **AI bill remains in trámite** before the futures/science committees of
  both chambers.
- 🔴 **One datum was REFUSED rather than shelved, and the refusal is the finding.** A study blog
  claims **Chile approved a dedicated AI *education* framework in 2025** and that Brazil and Mexico
  hold only non-binding guidance. **That claim appears in none of the academic or policy sources
  located**, which describe Chile via its 2021 policy and its pending bill. 🔵 Recorded as
  **UNVERIFIED-AND-CONTRADICTED and NOT carried.** A Chilean compliance slide built on it would
  assert a framework that the policy record does not show.

**Pass 266 addition — the governance deficit is the offer.** The quantified section above is the
argument: **87% of surveyed LATAM higher-ed institutions use AI, 26% have a formal AI strategy**
⚠️, and Brazilian, Chilean, Colombian and Costa Rican teachers use AI **16–20 points above the
OECD average**. The engagement that gap implies — AI-use policy, model inventory, human-review
design, assessment-integrity controls, procurement criteria, staff training — **requires no
platform displacement**, which removes the usual public-sector blocker. It also pre-positions
the client for `PL 2338`, which would classify education-related selection as high-risk.

**Route in:** UNESCO's **Observatory on AI in Education for LAC** (launched 2026-04-14, Santiago,
at ECLAC), UNESCO IESALC, **Ceibal** (Uruguay) and the **ILIA** index are convening bodies, not
just citations — and the public/private split (**68%** public vs **84%** private non-profit ⚠️)
says the public sector is both the larger gap and the harder sale.

**On supply, stated precisely:** LATAM's open-source education output remains the **platform**
layer and it is **copylefted** — `portabilis/i-educar` (GPL-2.0, 34,948 commits) and `i-diario`
(AGPL-3.0). Pass 266 did verify a Portuguese-language *agent*
([`guedesindev/ai_tutor_forge`](https://github.com/guedesindev/ai_tutor_forge), GPL-3.0), but at
18★ and 21 commits it is a personal project — **do not brief it as regional capability.** For a
Brazilian municipal network the honest stack is i-Educar as the installed base plus a permissive
agent layer on top, with i-Diário's AGPL network clause priced before the SOW.

The region where the policy direction most favours what open source actually is.

- **Brazil's digital-sovereignty framing is a direct mandate for open source.** MEC/SEGAPE's
  March 2026 *Referencial* explicitly calls for national solutions that reduce dependence on
  global platforms. A self-hosted Moodle or Chamilo with local-model inference is not a
  cost compromise there — it is the stated policy preference.
- **Move before the resolution lands.** MEC is reported (2026-10-15) to be preparing a
  national resolution on AI in teaching based on CNE guidelines, not yet in the Diário
  Oficial. Institutions that build to the existing *Referencial* now will be compliant by
  construction; this is the moment to be in the room.
- **Teach-about-AI before teach-with-AI.** Brazil's MEC guidance orders it that way
  deliberately. Sell AI **literacy** curriculum and teacher formation first; student-facing
  tutors second. Pitching it in the other order misreads the buyer.
- **Mexico's gap is quantified and it is teacher training.** SEP's April 2026 survey:
  **76% of professors make materials with AI but only 36% teach students to use it**, and
  **9 in 10 want training**. There is **no public national AI competency standard for
  teachers**. MXN 284.1M is allocated to teacher professional development in 2026, and SEP
  announced three national encuentros on technology, phones and AI in schools (July 2026).
  A teacher-formation programme with a measurable competency framework is the highest-fit
  offer in the region.
- **Build on the installed base.** Chamilo has the deepest LATAM public-sector footprint;
  Moodle dominates the large public universities. Neither needs replacing — both need an
  agentic layer, delivered through an LTI tool so the platform licence stays untouched.
- **Adoption is not the obstacle.** Brazil and Mexico lead regional AI adoption (reported
  76% / 70%, August 2026) and 92% of LATAM university students already use AI. Governance
  and teacher capability are the obstacles, and both are services work.
- **The region publishes the platform layer, and this KB had missed it.**
  [`portabilis/i-educar`](https://github.com/portabilis/i-educar) (GPL-2.0, 719★, **34,948
  commits**, last commit **2026-10-14**) is a Brazilian school-management system that began in
  the *prefeitura de Itajaí (SC)* and was published on the federal **Portal do Software Público
  in 2008**; `i-diario` (AGPL-3.0, last commit **2026-10-09**) is its teacher portal. For a
  municipal or state network this is the installed base, with sovereignty provenance that no
  imported platform can match. Build the AI layer outside both, across an HTTP/LTI boundary,
  and the copyleft never reaches studio code.
- **Thirteen of nineteen LAC countries studied do not teach early AI adoption in schools**
  (CENIA, reported 2026). Read against Brazil's and Mexico's explicit teacher-formation
  programmes, this is the regional asymmetry: two markets are buying, most of the rest have not
  started. Pitch teacher formation in BR/MX and **AI-literacy curriculum** everywhere else —
  and note that the second is cheaper to deliver and politically easier to approve.
- **The funding is already placed, which tells you who to talk to.** **Google.org has committed
  $4.6M to AI-literacy rollout across nine LATAM countries including Brazil and Mexico,
  targeting 1.25M students by 2028.** UNESCO runs a regional **AI-in-education Observatory for
  LAC** with a Mexican pilot through **CONALEP and DGETI** — the federal technical-education
  systems, not universities. The IDB has reviewed **193 regional AI initiatives** and found
  **59% using generative AI and 27% using language models**. Multilateral and philanthropic
  money is funding literacy and teacher capability; implementation partners are what it lacks.
- **Brazil is converging on the EU, so EMEA work ports.** Brazil signed an **AI and
  tech-regulation agreement with the EU (June 2026)**, and `PL 2338` would classify
  **education-related selection** — admissions, placement — as **high-risk**, the same surface
  the EU's Annex III names. An AI-Act-shaped compliance artefact built for an EMEA client is
  substantially reusable in Brazil. Say this to clients operating in both; it is the cheapest
  credible differentiator available in the region.
- **Agentic infrastructure is the gap, not AI interest.** HolonIQ's 2026 LATAM EdTech 100 shows
  AI positioning concentrated in **teacher-facing** tools, with learning platforms losing more
  ground than any other category. Brazil's **Teachy** has taken that niche regionally and
  expanded into Asia. Competing on consumer tutoring in LATAM means competing with a funded
  incumbent in the one category the market has already picked; the open lane is the
  institutional plumbing — SIS-grade data, dropout prediction, compliance artefacts — that
  Teachy does not sell.

**Pass 267 — a second independent survey, a sharper governance breakdown, and a verified negative.**

- 🟢 **A second, larger survey corroborates the governance deficit this base built its LATAM thesis
  on.** The **Digital Education Council's AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026** reports
  **92% of students and 79% of faculty actively engaging with AI**, from **30,000+ responses across
  29 institutions**, with Tecnológico de Monterrey and UNAM among its partners. Separately, a
  Tec-de-Monterrey-linked figure puts **only ~30% of LATAM universities** as having published AI-use
  🔴 **[CORRECTED `p276`: this is a PERCEPTION figure — ~30% of students and faculty consider their
  university integrates AI *effectively* or provides *sufficient resources*. It is not a count of
  published policies. The direct measure is UNESCO-IESALC's **18.5% institution-wide policies**.
  See `## Opportunities by region → LATAM`.]**
  policies. **Two independent instruments, the same shape: near-universal use, policy coverage
  around a quarter to a third.** The 61-point gap this base priced its governance-retrofit
  engagement on is not an artefact of one study.
- 🟢 **The UNESCO IESALC breakdown is now four numbers, not one.** Of 200 institutions across 19
  countries: **teaching and learning 73.5%**, **research 57.0%**, **administration 34.1%**,
  **community engagement 20.0%**. This base had carried "74% in teaching and learning"; the figure
  is **73.5%**, and the three additional areas matter commercially — **administration at 34.1% is
  less than half of teaching**, so the back-office is the under-served half of a LATAM university
  engagement, not the classroom.
- 🔴 **Uruguay's Plan Ceibal publishes no code — verified, not assumed.** The `ceibal` GitHub
  organisation exists and states *"This organization has no public repositories"*, with no public
  members. For LATAM's most-cited national edtech programme the posture is **integration, not
  adoption**, and briefing it as an open-source national stack (as Sunbird genuinely is) would be
  wrong. **Brazil remains the only LATAM country in this KB with published education source code**
  — `i-educar` (GPL-2.0, 34,948 commits) and `i-diario` (AGPL-3.0) — and it arrives copylefted.
- 🟡 **Regulatory picture, unchanged in shape and now sourced twice:** no unified regional
  framework. Chile leads with a National AI Policy (2021) and a bill under discussion; Brazil and
  Colombia have national strategies **without education-specific rules**; in Mexico the SEP/ANUIES
  and AI-Observatory recommendations are **non-binding**. **That absence is the commercial opening**
  — governance work that needs no regulator to force it, sold against institutional risk.
- ⚠️ **Every percentage in this section is secondary-source and will stay that way from this
  environment.** `ess.iesalc.unesco.org`, `unu.edu` and `www.digitaleducationcouncil.com` all
  **failed DNS resolution on both instruments** this pass (curl and WebFetch alike), as did
  `ve.scielo.org` and `repositorio.cepal.org`. The figures are carried because they are
  decision-relevant and mutually corroborating, **not** because a primary text was read.
- 🔴 **Pass 268 — four LATAM national programmes have now been probed by hand, and the supply
  picture is settled enough to brief: outside Brazil, the region's national education platforms do
  not publish code.** Pass 266 left a standing instruction to probe national-programme names
  directly rather than by star-sorted search; passes 267-268 have done it:

  | country | programme | what was found |
  |---------|-----------|----------------|
  | 🇺🇾 Uruguay | **Plan Ceibal** | GitHub organisation exists with **zero public repositories**, no public members *(pass 267)* |
  | 🇦🇷 Argentina | **Juana Manso** (Educ.ar S.E., Plan Federal, 2020) | **Claimed open, unlocated.** An official is quoted *"todo lo que llevamos adelante es de código abierto y de patrimonio del Estado Nacional"* and a third-party resource page classes it as open source — but **no forge, no organisation and no source tree was found**, and every source located dates to 2020-21 with nothing on 2026 operational status |
  | 🇲🇽 Mexico | **SEP / "Educación Superior 2026"** | **Not software.** A catalogue that redirects applicants to each institution's own registration site. No ministry repository located |
  | 🇨🇱 Chile | **educarchile** (MINEDUC + Fundación Chile) | Portal, **no public code located** |
  | 🇨🇴 Colombia | — | **Nothing located** |

  🟢 **Brazil remains the only LATAM country in this KB publishing education source** —
  [`portabilis/i-educar`](https://github.com/portabilis/i-educar) (GPL-2.0, 34,948 commits) and
  `i-diario` (AGPL-3.0) — **and it arrives copylefted.** 🔵 **What this means for a LATAM
  engagement, stated commercially:** outside Brazil there is **no national base to adopt**, so the
  work is *integration against a closed or hosted national platform*, and in Brazil it is
  *integration across a copyleft boundary*. Neither is the "fork the national stack" story that
  India's Sunbird (MIT) supports in APAC. **The probe list is recorded so later passes do not spend
  the budget re-establishing it.**
- ⚠️ **An official's "it's all open source" is not a licence, and this pass is the clearest case
  yet.** Argentina's Juana Manso is publicly described as open source by a government official and
  by a third-party directory, and **no code was locatable on any channel tried.** Treat
  programme-level open-source claims as **CLAIMED-OPEN, UNLOCATED** until a repository is read —
  the same grade this base gives a README licence claim with no payload.

these is education-specific** — this pass's leg confirms that explicitly. The binding constraints on an
education deliverable in LATAM remain **data protection, procurement and institutional policy**, which is

#### Pass 279 — the governance research base is now citable, and the figures still are not

🟢 **Two sources worth naming in a proposal**, both carried already and both re-confirmed this pass:
the **UNESCO IESALC working paper (September 2026)**, built on a survey of **200 higher education
institutions across 19 LAC countries** between August and October 2025 and covering teaching,
research, administration and governance; and **ILIA 2025 (CEPAL)**, whose governance dimension carries
a regulation sub-dimension. 🟢 A comparative study of AI regulation in higher education across
**Argentina, Brazil, Chile, Peru and Spain** gives a five-jurisdiction frame a regional deck can cite
directly.

🟢 **Brazil — a second, firmer instrument.** Beyond MEC's **"IA na Educação Básica: Caminhos para o
Currículo e a Prática Docente"** *(launched **2026-04-09**, with UNESCO cooperation)*, **Parecer
CNE/CP nº 15/2026** was approved **2026-09-01** with its summary published in the **DOU on
2026-10-06**, and **becomes binding on ministerial homologation** — which had not happened as of this
pass. 🔵 **The CNE opinion holds that AI must stay subordinate to educational purposes and must not
replace education professionals**, which is a constraint on solution design, not merely on
procurement. 🔴 **Watch the homologation**: it is the moment Brazil moves from orientation to binding
rule, and it is the single highest-value LATAM trigger to monitor.

⚠️ **The figures remain the weakest part of this region's page.** The *"Rest of Latin America"* series
(**$18.2M 2024 → $36M 2029**) is one vendor's page, covers an undefined subset of the region, and
**cannot be reconciled** with the LATAM figures elsewhere in this file. 🔵 **Equity is the stated
regional risk** in the regional expert forum material — adoption deepening inequality between and
within countries — which is a reason to scope delivery around access, not only capability.


#### Pass 289 (`2026-10-18`) — LATAM

🟢 **The strongest ADOPTION data of any region this pass, and the governance gap is now
measured at institutional scale.**

**UNESCO IESALC** (with UNU-IAS), **200 higher-education institutions across 19 countries**:

| measure | value |
|---|---|
| using AI in at least one area | **87%** |
| using AI in teaching and learning | **74%** |
| 🔴 holding a **formal AI strategy** | **26%** |
| students reporting confusion about appropriate use | **1 in 2** |

**TALIS 2024**, secondary teachers reporting AI use: 🇧🇷 Brazil **56%**, 🇨🇱 Chile **55%**,
🇨🇴 Colombia **53%**, 🇨🇷 Costa Rica **52%** — against an **OECD average of 36%**.
🔵 **LATAM is above the OECD average on teacher adoption.** 🇺🇾 **Ceibal**: **75%** of Uruguayan
public-school teachers report using these tools. **Digital Education Council** LATAM survey
2026: **30 000+ responses across 29 institutions**. **CENIA + ECLAC**: regional AI investment
averages **six times lower** than the global one.

🔴 **No binding national or regional AI-in-education rule found.** Governance is voluntary and
institutional, with UNESCO's 2021 ethics recommendation the main reference.

🔵 **The opportunity, placed, and it is the cleanest in this file:** **87% adoption against 26%
strategy, with investment 6x below global.** The gap is not a modelling gap — it is a
**governance-artefact** gap, and governance artefacts are cheap to produce relative to
platforms. An institutional AI policy, an assessment-integrity position, a staff training path
and a disclosure standard are sellable **now**, to institutions that have already adopted the
tools and know they are exposed. 🟡 And the 6x investment figure argues for **adapting open
substrates over licensing vendor platforms** — which is this base's whole thesis, and the one
region where the economics state it outright.


### Global

#### 🟢 `p296` · `2026-10-18` — the market-wide move is EXPERIMENTATION → GOVERNANCE, and buyers have started asking for evidence

**The four cross-region findings, from the most credible sources the leg returned (1EdTech, HolonIQ,
EC/OECD, UNESCO) rather than from the statistics round-ups:**

| finding | source | consequence for a studio |
|---|---|---|
| **Experimentation → governance.** Institutions are moving to AI strategies with defined governance, compliance and evaluation processes | 1EdTech | the deliverable increasingly includes a policy and an eval regime, not just a system. This is what the LATAM 87%/26% gap looks like worldwide |
| **Workflow-first is where the gains are real**: course-design support, teacher productivity, administrative workflows | HolonIQ | 🔵 teacher- and admin-facing beats student-facing on measurable return **and** on regulatory exposure — the same conclusion the NYC moratorium forces |
| **Purpose-built education platforms are displacing generic chatbots** — tools built around learning objectives and curriculum | TeachBetter | the **curriculum binding** is the moat. `microsoft/Shiksha-Copilot` (grade/subject/chapter) is the shelf's clearest permissive example |
| **Buyers want evidence** that products improve learning, persistence, well-being or job-relevant skills | 1EdTech | 🔴 an engagement without a measurement plan is becoming unsellable — and this base's evaluation gap is a *commercial* gap, not just a technical one |

**Also recorded:** **digital credentials** are being treated as a core currency for skills-based
hiring and learning (1EdTech), and **autonomous agents that plan lessons and draft assessments** plus
real-time adaptive personalisation are named as 2026's defining development (Educativa).

🟢 **A permissive, hardware-grounded runtime row arrived this pass and it is region-agnostic by
design:** [`open-edge-platform/education-ai-suite`](https://github.com/open-edge-platform/education-ai-suite)
— **Apache-2.0**, Intel, 450 commits: reference education AI pipelines (*Smart Classroom*, *Teaching
Assistant*) **plus benchmarking tools to decide which hardware a use case needs**, on Intel CPU/iGPU/NPU
via OpenVINO. 🔵 **It is the first answer this base has to the sizing question every sovereign and
offline pattern raises** (`P7`, `P9`, `P12`). ⚠️ **0★ and hardware-coupled** — a vendor reference
stack; price the lock-in.

⚠️ **Figures this file will NOT publish as fact.** A **$12.3B** global market projection attributed to
HolonIQ, an EDUCAUSE-attributed **83% planning AI teaching-assistant deployment**, and **~54% of
students using AI daily or weekly** (DemandSage). All three come from vendor or low-rigour blogs
citing sources the leg could not reach. 🔴 **The market-size figures this base has carried in earlier
passes — `$9.58B → $42.48B by 2030 at 41.5% CAGR` — come from the same class of source and have never
been verified against a primary instrument.** Treat the whole market-size family as vendor-sourced.
🔵 **The credible sources point the other way from the hype**: cautious, governed, workflow-focused
adoption.

#### 🔴 `p295` (`2026-10-18`) — four live market estimates, 78 % apart, and an existence channel that is now doubly closed

| estimate | year | house |
|---|---|---|
| **USD 6.4 B** | 2025 | IMARC-derived |
| **USD 8.3 B** | 2025 | second house |
| **USD 10.6 B** | 2026 | third house |
| **USD 11.4 B** | 2026 | second house's own forward figure |

🔵 **Carry the spread or carry nothing.** Adoption figures are firmer and still secondary: student AI
use **66 % (2024) → 92 % (2025)**; **86 %** of higher-education students using AI as their primary
research and brainstorming partner at the start of 2026; sector maturity rated **LOW** — most
institutions still in **pilot**; **language learning** named the fastest-growing segment.

🔴 **The existence channel, now measured twice and closed both times.** `p295` discharged `p294`'s
ranked item 1 — a DNS probe off the proxy path — **by refuting its premise**:

| host | class | `getaddrinfo` |
|---|---|---|
| `github.com` | known-real, **allowlisted** | 🟢 **RESOLVES** `140.82.113.4` |
| `huggingface.co` | known-real, not allowlisted | 🔴 `EAI_NONAME` |
| `unu.edu` | known-real, not allowlisted | 🔴 `EAI_NONAME` |
| `nm-aist.ac.tz` | known-real, not allowlisted | 🔴 `EAI_NONAME` |
| `this-host-does-not-exist-p295.example` | **known-FAKE** | 🔴 `EAI_NONAME` |

🔵 **The resolver is the same allowlist layer as the gateway**, so a name that fails to resolve is
**still** `EXISTENCE-UNMEASURED` — exactly as a `000` from `curl` is. 🔴 **Bound, stated:** a broader
host-by-host probe was **refused by the sandbox as exfil scouting** and was **not** worked around;
this verdict rests on the single five-host probe that ran, which already carries **both** controls.

#### 🔴 Pass 294 (`2026-10-18`) — the market-size spread widened AGAIN, and the student-adoption figure that gets quoted everywhere is second-hand

| source class | 2025 / 2026 base | endpoint | CAGR |
|---|---|---|---|
| IMARC-based summary | **$6.4 B (2025)** | **$79.6 B (2034)** | — |
| The Business Research Company | **$7.52 B (2025)** | **$10.6 B (2026)** | — |
| third source | — | — | **36 %** |
| fourth source | — | — | **~28 % through 2036** |

🔵 **The warning this shelf has carried since `p291` is reinforced, not revised:** these are vendor
and market-research pages with different definitions of the market. **Carried as a direction.**

🔴 **Two widely-quoted adoption figures, both downgraded on inspection:**
- *"global student AI usage surged from **66 % (2024)** to **92 % (2025)**"* — 🔴 the source states

- **K-12 = 45.62 %** of total adoption, and **language learning** the fastest-growing segment —
  🟡 single vendor, two-decimal precision on a share figure that no method is given for. 🔴 **The
  precision is the tell**; carried as a ranking, not a measurement.

🟡 **The one figure worth a pitch:** market maturity scored **35 / 100**, i.e. *"most institutions
are still in pilot mode"*, against intense competition. 🔵 It agrees with North America's **10 %**
formal-guidelines figure from an unrelated publisher — **two independent channels, same conclusion:
the buying is ahead of the governing**, which is the gap a studio's assurance layer sells into.

#### 🟡 Pass 291 (`2026-10-18`) — the market-size spread WIDENED, which strengthens the warning rather than resolving it

🔴 **Four vendor reports, four different numbers, and the spread is now wider than when this
shelf first warned about it**: **$6.4 B (2025)**, **$10.6 B (2026)**, **$11.4 B (2026)**, with
endpoints from **$57.2 B (2033)** to **$79.6 B (2034)** and CAGRs from **~31 %** upward.
🔵 This shelf carries no single figure as authoritative and that posture is now **measured**,
not cautious: two of these publishers disagree by **78 %** on the *current* year.

🟢 **One genuinely cross-region corroboration.** An analyst rates institutional AI maturity
**low — most institutions still in pilot**, from an instrument independent of **UNESCO
IESALC**'s LATAM survey, which found **87 % use against 26 % with a strategy**. Two unrelated
methods, same shape: **consumption is universal and governance is not**. 🔵 That gap is the one
finding on this shelf that has held in all four regions, across six passes, from four
independent sources — and it is the commercial premise under every pattern in
[`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md).

🔴 **Declined again:** the *"66 % → 92 % student use"* progression (vendor summary, recurring
for three passes) and *"North America = 36 % of the global market"* (aggregator, unsourced).

> 🔵 **Why this subsection exists (`p289`).** The region vocabulary is closed and has **five**
> values; this block carried four for roughly 170 passes, so `p383-region-heading-gate`
> reported `P383-MISSING-REGION: Global` on every run and no pass discharged it. It is not a
> formality: an opportunity that is real in all four regions and *placeable in none of them*
> was previously written into whichever region the pass happened to be looking at.

#### 🟢 Pass 290 (`2026-10-18`) — the cross-region opportunity: the human checkpoint is free and the EVIDENCE STORE is not

🟢 **The genuinely global finding this pass**, and it is a change in what a studio has to
build. `p288` and `p289` established that all four regions regulate the same narrow act — **AI
as the sole basis for a decision about a student** — and that the portable requirement is a
recorded, evidenceable human decision point plus provenance marking.

🟢 **`p290` found the first half shipping under MIT**, in three sibling agents across two market
segments: `application-reader-agent` *("never issue a final admit or deny decision
unilaterally")*, `special-education-agent` *("never make eligibility determinations")* and
`retention-agent` *("treat every risk flag as a hypothesis to be confirmed, not a verdict")*.

🔴 **The second half is not shipping anywhere.** *"Log every intervention — date, method,
response, outcome"* is an instruction to a model. It is **not** a schema, an append-only store,
a retention policy, or an artefact an auditor can be shown. 🔵 **So the cross-region
opportunity has inverted since `p288` wrote it**: the compliance *posture* is now a free,
permissive starting point, and the **billable deliverable is the record** — the thing that
proves a human decided, survives the student's challenge, and satisfies the EU AI Act's
high-risk tier, Vietnam's sole-basis clause, Korea's marking duty and the Oklahoma/Maryland
oversight rules **from one implementation**.

🟡 **One global supply datum, kept because it prices the above:** roughly **$1B of global edtech
venture funding in H1 2026, down 26% year over year** (HolonIQ). 🔵 Capital is selective, which
favours a deliverable that is **demonstrably required by regulation** over one that is
differentiated by model quality.

#### Pass 289 (`2026-10-18`) — the cross-region opportunity: curriculum provenance

🟢 **The one finding this pass that is genuinely global.** Curricula are being published **as
data** — ontologies, SHACL shapes, APIs, MCP servers — and the moment they are, *"aligned"*
becomes a claim a build can **check** and a licence becomes the binding constraint.

🔴 **And the licence layer is not what a scanner reports it to be.** `ACTION p289` measured it:
of 7 payloads over 5 repositories publishing a national curriculum, **5 were authoritative
licence bodies (the code) and 2 were `RESTATEMENT`s (the curriculum data)** — paraphrases of
OGL v3.0 whose authoritative text sits on a host that answers **403 to CONNECT**. `p742`,
and every off-the-shelf licence scanner, grades those two `GRANTED`.

🔵 **The sellable artefact, in any region:** a **curriculum-provenance gate** in the client's
CI — `P44` in [`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md) — that distinguishes *"we read
this grant"* from *"we read someone's description of this grant"*, records the authority host
it could not reach, and emits the attribution strings the payload prescribes rather than the
one its friendly summary suggests. 🔴 Both OGL payloads prescribe **two different**
attributions; **3 of 3** Oak licence files with a summary block state the obligation
differently there than in their terms.

🟡 **What is NOT global, and the distinction is the whole value of this subsection:** the
**corpus** is national. England's curriculum is ceded with commercial use explicit; the other
**ten jurisdictions probed this pass could not be read at all** (12 hosts, 2 egress paths,
12/12 `POLICY-REFUSED`). So the *pattern* ports everywhere and the *content* ports nowhere —
which is precisely the sentence a studio needs before it promises "curriculum-aligned" outside
the UK.

## Declared gaps

- **Japan: still no school-level education-AI adoption or policy data located.** Searches
  returned framework-law and general-adoption material only. What is new is **population** AI
  use (24.7%, Microsoft via secondary reporting) — a different measure, recorded above and
  explicitly **not** a substitute for classroom data. Do not model Japan from the APAC
  aggregate, and do not model Japanese schools from Japanese consumers.
- **Mexico's draft general AI regulation: status not established.** Sources agree it seeks to
  replicate the EU risk model; none located this pass gives a current legislative status.
  Mexico's *education* policy surface (SEP survey, roadmap, encuentros) is far better evidenced
  than its AI-statute surface.
- **Maryland `SB 720` is recorded as disputed**, not resolved: one tracker has it signed
  2026-05-26 (Ch. 634), another has it through one chamber only. Neither was checked against
  the legislature's own record, which is the only thing that would settle it.
- **No education-specific regulatory detail located for Canada beyond the "AI for All"
  strategy**, and none for APAC outside the six jurisdictions named above.
- **No education-specific regulatory detail for Canada, Mexico at federal level, or most of
  APAC beyond the four countries above.** What exists is general AI regulation, cited as such.
- **Every market-size number in this file is vendor- or aggregator-sourced.** None was
  confirmed against a primary research document. Treated as direction only.
- **Estado de México's April 2026 education reform** appeared in search results but the
  substantive provision was truncated in every source located. Recorded as unverified rather
  than guessed.

### Pass 268 addendum to the declared gaps

- **Ed-Fi's North American adoption is search-summary grade and must not be briefed as a count.**
  The licence is payload-verified Apache-2.0 on four repositories. The *adoption* is not: **neither
  Ed-Fi README names a single US state or education agency**, and the Alliance's own pages give no
  number. A third-party directory lists 14 statewide adoptions (AZ, NM, TX, TN, GA, SC, KS, NE, IN,
  MI, WI, MN, VT, DE); search additionally reports **South Carolina's own education-department page
  naming ODS/API 7.3 for SY2025-26** and a **Texas Education Exchange** deployment. ⚠️ **Both
  `ed.sc.gov` and `www.ed.sc.gov` returned `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND` from this environment**, so the
  one primary government source that would settle it was **not read**. Per `P950` it was not
  retried per host. **Quote the licence; let the client's procurement confirm their own state.**
- **The EU AI Act's education timeline is DISPUTED across four sources and this base should stop
  resolving it.** Located this pass: amendments *adopted June 2026*; entry into force *27 July
  2026*; a separate claim of *31 July 2026*; *"high-risk provisions enter into force Aug 2026"*;
  and one source asserting *"enforcement began Feb 2025"*. These cannot all be true. The
  **substance** is stable and is what belongs in a client conversation — education AI affecting
  **access or assessment** (admissions, student evaluation, exam scoring) is **high-risk**, requires
  a **conformity assessment before being placed on the market**, and requires **human oversight with
  the ability to intervene or shut the system down**. 🔵 **Brief the obligation, date the obligation
  from the Official Journal, and never from a vendor guide.**
- **EMEA sub-regional gap, stated rather than left silent: nothing was located for the Middle East
  or Africa this pass.** The EMEA leg returned EU, UK and Germany material only. An "EMEA" number in
  this file is, in practice, a **European** number — and this pass found no EdTech vendor market-share
  data for EMEA at all. 🔵 Pass 267's standing note to run **Kenya, Nigeria, South Africa and Egypt
  as four separate queries, never paired**, remains unrun and is the cheapest way to close this.
- **Figures located this pass that are already shelved, re-confirmed and not re-published as new:**
  the 2026 US state-legislation count and the named state bills, H.R. 8747's committee advance,
  Canada's "AI for All", the NYC moratorium, South Korea's AI Basic Act, Vietnam's high-risk
  education designation, UNESCO's LAC Observatory, and the TALIS/PISA/IESALC adoption percentages.
  🔵 **Recorded as a negative result on purpose:** a full regional sweep this pass produced **no new
  regulatory datum**, which is itself worth knowing — the regulatory surface of this industry has
  stopped moving weekly, and the research budget is better spent on the supply side, where three
  consecutive passes have each found shelf-grade rows that search alone never surfaces.

  none surfaced on any of the four legs. 🔵 **Stated as a hole rather than left as silence:** this

  any of them.** The region's remaining countries — the Gulf, the Maghreb, sub-Saharan Africa beyond
  the three named — are as unexamined as before, because the instruction that was run specified four
  countries.
- 🔴 **A PROPAGATION DEFECT, and it is the fourth cross-shelf failure in four passes.** Pass 266
  found a gap note contradicting a shelf; `p267` found Canvas mis-shelved; `p268` found the Ed-Fi
  rows in the register but not on the shelves and amended the method to *"grep `open-gaps.md` too."*
  **Pass 269 measured the same defect on the REGIONAL INTEL, by grepping each datum its own regional
  leg returned.** These are all carried in [`open-gaps.md`](open-gaps.md) and/or the README **and
  absent from this file, [`trends.md`](trends.md) and [`policy-matrix.tsv`](policy-matrix.tsv)**:
  the **US DOL AI Literacy Framework (2026-02-13)**, the **STUDENTS FIRST Act of 2026** student
  framework, **North Carolina's K-12 implementation guidance**, the **EMEA education-AI market figure
  of €/$2.64B (2026)**, **Finland / Estonia / Netherlands as K-12 leaders**, and the **Linux
  Foundation LATAM AI workforce report**. 🔴 **And the Vietnam case is worse than a propagation
  miss — it is an unreconciled contradiction.** Pass 269 went to add **Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg**
  (carried in the register and the README: education among six high-risk sectors, **2027-03-01**
  compliance, a further six-month grace for education systems already operating before
  **2026-08-15**) and found the matrix **already carrying Vietnam's `assessment_grading` as `GATED`
  under a *different instrument* — Decree 142/2026/ND-CP, effective 2026-04-30.** **Two instruments,
  two dates, one jurisdiction and one function, and no shelf has ever reconciled them.** The row is
  therefore marked **UNRECONCILED and both instruments named**, rather than merged or silently
  overwritten — Vietnam is the APAC jurisdiction whose education deadline this base is most often
  asked for, and **it currently has two.** 🔵 **The register is where this base thinks and the
  shelves are what it ships; six regional datums were sitting on the wrong side of that line, and the
  seventh turned out to be a conflict rather than an omission.** Pass 269 fixed the
  category for its own MEA findings — they went to this file and to the policy matrix in the same
  change — and registers the backlog rather than quietly inheriting it.


### Pass 270 addendum to the declared gaps

🟢 **`p269`'s six-datum backlog is DISCHARGED — and five of the six changed shape under
re-verification, which is why discharging a backlog is not the same as copying it.** Pass 270's
rule was: **re-run the leg, do not transcribe the register.** Outcome:

| backlog datum | verdict on re-verification |
|---|---|
| US DOL AI Literacy Framework (2026-02-13) | 🟢 **CONFIRMED** — and gained a citable identifier (**Training and Employment Notice No. 07-25**), five content areas and seven delivery principles |
| STUDENTS FIRST Act of 2026 | 🔴 **RECLASSIFIED** — not a bill, not law; a **student-written model policy**, 98 teens, **82-16 on 2026-07-19**, advisory only |
| North Carolina K-12 implementation guidance | 🔴 **RE-DATED** — the datum is a **2024-01-16** DPI guidebook; NC's 2026 layer is the **AI Leadership Council Strategic Roadmap (2026-07-01)**, and §7.39 of **Session Law 2026-41** is a third layer |
| EMEA market figure €/$2.64B (2026) | 🔴 **DISPUTED** — a second publisher (MarketDataForecast) gives **~$2.11B**; ~25% apart on the same year. Published as a **range**, not merged |
| Finland / Estonia / Netherlands as K-12 leaders | 🔴 **PARTIALLY REFUTED** — Estonia and Finland stand on **different** grounds; **the Netherlands is unsubstantiated** and the grouping traces to an aggregator with no source |
| Linux Foundation LATAM AI workforce report | 🔴 **RENAMED** — no artefact by that name exists; it is ***Economic and Workforce Impacts of AI in Latin America*** (LF Research, 4th in a Meta-sponsored series) |

🔴 **A FIFTH cross-shelf defect in five passes — and this one is a FALSE SHELVING CLAIM, which is
a new species.** The four before it were omissions and one contradiction: a datum sat in the
register and never reached a shelf. This one is different. Of the **Sanoma Learning 2026 European
Teacher Survey**, `agents/trending.md` states verbatim: *"Recorded in `intel/market.md` and
`intel/trends.md`."* **Measured this pass: `grep -c Sanoma intel/market.md intel/trends.md` →
`0` and `0`.** 🔵 **A pass asserted the propagation it had not performed, and every later pass
that grepped the register for unshelved datums would read that sentence as proof the work was
done.** 🟢 **The method amendment this forces is one line: a propagation claim is not evidence of
propagation — grep the TARGET file, never trust the sentence in the source file.** `p268` amended
the novelty check to grep the slug; **`p270` amends the propagation check to grep the destination.**
Both defects were invisible to the check that preceded them.

🟡 **Gaps this pass OPENED, stated so they are falsifiable:**
- 🔴 **The EMEA market figure has no primary source and now no consensus either.** Two publishers,
  ~25% apart, both secondary. **This file can no longer give a single EMEA number**, and says so.
- 🔴 **Netherlands: no national-level AI-in-education evidence located.** Named, costed next step:
  **Dutch-language queries against the Ministerie van OCW and SLO.** Not run this pass.
- 🟡 **The DOL instrument's identifier is itself unreconciled** — *Training and Employment Notice*
  No. 07-25 in one rendering, *TEGL* 07-25 in another. Both recorded; neither resolvable without
  the primary channel this environment lacks.
- 🟡 **`STUDENTS FIRST`'s acronym expansion is inconsistent across sources and is NOT resolved.**
- 🔴 **MEA remains unsized, unchanged from `p269`.** This pass added no MEA figure; the Gulf, the
  Maghreb and sub-Saharan Africa beyond `p269`'s four countries stay unexamined.
- 🔵 **APAC returned ZERO new named edtech players across a full regional leg.** Recorded as a
  measured nil return, the fifth consecutive pass in which the supply channel yields nothing
  education-specific that this base does not already hold.

🟢 **Gap 367 — spent in a THIRD language, closure upheld a third time.** `p269` ran the Hindi and
Chinese limbs; `p270` ran the **Spanish** one. Nine repositories returned, **eight university
student course portfolios**, and the single framework-grade result (`nmarafo/OpenDidactia`,
CC BY-SA 4.0, Spain/LOMLOE) was **already held and is secondary-band**. **Zero new repositories.**
🔵 **The nearest permissive primary-band artefact remains `DECK6/korean-elementary-learning-map`
(MIT) — and the distinction is load-bearing: it is an ontology of a primary CURRICULUM, not a
primary AI-LITERACY course.** Gap 367 asks for the latter and it still does not exist.
**One falsifiable limb remains, and it is free:** `datawhalechina/ai-edu-for-kids` carries

|---|---|

| `agents/top.md` rows, counted | **12 distinct slugs** |

NUMBER in the sentence.** Passes 267 and 268 added the servers and updated the count on one shelf
only. 🟢 **Amendment (`T37`): when a gap is quantified, the quantity is a carried datum and decays
like any other — re-count it from the rows, on every shelf that states it.**
